# Konecta TIC Repository - Comprehensive Analysis

## Executive Summary

The **konecta-tic** repository contains a **multi-service AI platform** with 42 distinct applications/services built on Google Cloud Platform (GCP), Kubernetes, and modern AI/ML frameworks. The platform uses MCP (Model Context Protocol) servers, CrewAI workflows, and Google Cloud's Vertex AI for AI-powered business intelligence and automation.

---

| **kon-argocd-apps** | ArgoCD application manifests (GitOps) | Infrastructure |
| **kon-argocd-platform** | ArgoCD platform configuration | Infrastructure |

## 2. LANGUAGES & FRAMEWORKS BY SERVICE

### Python (30+ services)
- **Framework**: FastAPI, FastMCP, Flask, Cloud Functions
- **AI/ML**: CrewAI, Vertex AI, LiteLLM, LangChain patterns
- **ORM**: SQLAlchemy 2.0 (async)
- **Database**: asyncpg/PostgreSQL
- **async runtime**: asyncio, uvicorn
- **Version**: Python 3.10+, 3.11, 3.12, 3.13

### JavaScript/Node.js (5+ services)
- **Framework**: Nuxt.js 3 (Vue 3)
- **Backend**: Node.js with TypeScript
- **Package Manager**: npm
- **ORM**: Drizzle ORM
- **Version**: Node 20+

### Infrastructure as Code (Terraform)
- **Language**: HCL2
- **Version**: Terraform >= 1.5.0
- **Modules**: GCP, Kubernetes, Kong, ArgoCD
- **State**: GCS-based, per-service isolation

---

## 3. DOCKER/CONTAINER SETUP

### Dockerfiles by Type

**Python Services (18+ Dockerfiles)**:
```dockerfile
# Standard pattern
FROM python:3.11-slim / python:3.13-slim
RUN pip install -r requirements.txt
EXPOSE 8000/8080
```

**Node.js Services (2+ Dockerfiles)**:
```dockerfile
# Multi-stage build pattern
FROM node:20-alpine AS builder
RUN npm ci && npm run build
FROM node:20-alpine AS production
EXPOSE 8080
```

### Docker Compose Files
| Location | Purpose | Services |
|----------|---------|----------|
| kon-app-hrfraud/docker-compose.yml | Development stack | PostgreSQL + FastAPI + Nuxt |
| kon-app-hrfraud/docker-compose.prod.yml | Production stack | Production-grade config |
| kon-rag-ingestion/docker-compose.yml | RAG pipeline | Backend + Frontend |
| kon-mcp-gateway/docker-compose.observability.yml | Observability | Jaeger, Prometheus, Grafana |
| kon-flow-rag-ingestion/docker-compose.yaml | Flow orchestration | - |
| kon-mcp-webcrawler/docker/docker-compose.yml | Webcrawler stack | - |
| kon-prototypes/services/doc_ingestion/docker-compose.yml | Doc ingestion | Worker + API |

### Key Container Patterns
- **Multi-stage builds** for optimized Python images
- **Non-root users** (security best practice)
- **Volume mounts** for credentials, config, data persistence
- **Health checks** for service readiness
- **Environment variables** for configuration
- **Port mappings**: 8000/8080 (HTTP), 9090 (Prometheus), 3000/3300 (Frontend), 5432 (PostgreSQL)

---

## 4. CI/CD CONFIGURATION

### GitHub Actions (kon-gcp-platform)

**Workflows**:
| Workflow | Trigger | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| **pull-request.yml** | PR to main/develop | Terraform validation, module tests |
| **main-validation.yml** | Push to main | Release tagging, validation |
| **deploy.yml** | Manual trigger | Production deployments with approval gates |
| **nightly-quality.yml** | Scheduled (02:00 UTC) | Full test suite + drift detection |

**CI/CD Pipeline Patterns**:
- Module detection via file diff
- Conditional module tests (fast tests on PR, full suite nightly)
- BuildKit for optimized Docker builds
- Approval gates for prod deployments
- Change window enforcement (qa: 08:00-18:00 UTC, prod: Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)

### Cloud Build (Multiple Services)

**Services with CloudBuild**:
- kon-mcp-ragengine (GCS upload → Cloud Run deploy)
- kon-mcp-google-search
- kon-mcp-googlemaps (cloudbuild.yaml + cloudbuild-dev.yaml)
- kon-mcp-diagram
- kon-mcp-pptx
- kon-mcp-docingestion
- kon-iq-portal
- kon-rag-googledrive
- kon-app-hrfraud (separate backend + frontend pipelines)

**Standard CloudBuild Pattern**:
```yaml
steps:
  1. Build Docker image (BuildKit with cache)
  2. Security scanning (Artifacts API)
  3. Push to GCR
  4. Deploy to Cloud Run
  5. Update traffic (Blue/Green or Canary)
```

---

## 5. GCP CLOUD RUN CONFIGURATION

### Cloud Run Services
All MCP servers and main apps deploy to Cloud Run using CloudBuild.

**Deployment Configuration**:
| Service | Region | Memory | CPU | Timeout | Auth |
|---------|--------|--------|-----|---------|------|
| MCP Servers (generic) | us-central1 | 512MB-1GB | 1-2 | 300s | Unauthenticated |
| HR Fraud Backend | europe-west1 | 1Gi | 2 | 900s | API Key |
| RAG Services | us-central1/europe-west4 | 1GB | 1 | 300s | Varies |
| Portfolio Guru Bot | europe-west3 | 1GB | 1 | 120s | Service Account |

**CloudBuild Configuration**:
- Registry caching (buildcache tags)
- BuildKit inline cache
- Docker build args: BUILD_DATE, VCS_REF, COMMIT_SHA
- Security scanning post-build
- Database migrations via Cloud Run Jobs
- Port: 8000/8080 (Cloud Run requirement)

---

## 6. KUBERNETES/HELM CONFIGURATIONS

### GKE Setup (kon-gcp-platform)

**Cluster Configuration**:
- **Type**: GKE Autopilot
- **Regions**: eu-west1 (primary), eu-west4 (secondary)
- **Network**: Private clusters with Cloud NAT
- **Ingress**: GKE Gateway API (external HTTPS load balancer)

**Kubernetes Resources**:
| Component | Configuration | Details |
|-----------|----------------|---------|
| **Kong API Gateway** | Kubernetes module | API management, 3rd-party abstraction |
| **LiteLLM** | Kubernetes module | Unified LLM proxy (Gemini, Claude) |
| **ArgoCD** | Shared cluster | GitOps for multi-cluster deployments |
| **Namespaces** | Per-use-case isolation | RBAC, quotas, network policies |
| **Network Policies** | Default-deny-ingress | Required allow rules for GCP health checks |

### ArgoCD Setup
- **Location**: Shared cluster (kd-ix-eur-shr-gke)
- **Mode**: GitOps with App-of-Apps pattern
- **Repositories**: Multi-cluster management (dev/qa/prod)
- **Configuration**: Terraform-managed

### No Helm Charts Found
- Kubernetes resources managed via Terraform modules
- No traditional Helm chart directories identified
- Configuration via Terraform HCL

## 8. ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES & SECRETS MANAGEMENT

### Pattern: .env Files
All services follow `.env` pattern with `.env.example` templates:
```bash
.env.example / .env.template → .env (gitignored)
```

### Services with Environment Configuration
- kon-mcp-gateway (TRANSPORT, AUTH, INTROSPECTION)
- kon-mcp-ragengine (GCP_PROJECT, VERTEX_AI_MODEL)
- kon-iq-portal (API_BASE, GCP credentials)
- kon-testiq (GCP configuration)
- kon-mcp-googlemaps (API keys)
- kon-bot-porfolioguru (PROJECT_ID, RAG_CORPUS_ID, GEMINI settings)
- kon-litellm (LLM provider keys)
- kon-flow-competitor-intelligence (CrewAI config)
- kon-rag-ingestion (GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS, Gemini API key)

### Secret Management Patterns
1. **Google Cloud Secret Manager** - Terraform managed (kon-gcp-platform)
2. **Workload Identity** - No service account keys (best practice)
3. **Environment variable injection** - CloudBuild/Cloud Functions
4. **Firestore** - Distributed rate limiting, conversation context
5. **.env files** - Local development only (gitignored)

### Key Environment Variables by Service
| Variable | Services | Purpose |
|----------|----------|---------|
| GCP_PROJECT_ID | Most services | Google Cloud project identification |
| DATABASE_URL | HR Fraud, RAG | PostgreSQL async connection |
| GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS | Cloud Functions, local dev | GCP authentication |
| LITELLM_PROXY_URL | MCP Gateway, Apps | LLM proxy endpoint |
| VERTEX_AI_MODEL | RAG services | Gemini/Claude model selection |
| GCS_BUCKET_NAME | RAG, ingestion | Cloud Storage bucket |
| ANTHROPIC_API_BASE | LiteLLM | Claude API endpoint |
| PORT | All services | Service listen port (8000/8080) |

## 9. NETWORKING & COMMUNICATION PATTERNS

### HTTP/REST Communication
- **Primary**: HTTP on port 8000/8080
- **Load Balancer**: GKE Gateway API (external HTTPS)
- **Internal**: Kubernetes Service DNS (port 80/443)
- **Authentication**: API Keys (X-API-Key header), OAuth2, Service Accounts

### MCP (Model Context Protocol) Communication
- **Transport**: HTTP (SSE - Server-Sent Events)
- **Gateway**: kon-mcp-gateway acts as central hub
- **Tool Routing**: Server-level scopes (e.g., "google-search:*")
- **Scope-based Authorization**: Fine-grained access control

### Pub/Sub Messaging
- **Service**: Google Cloud Pub/Sub
- **Topics Used**:
  - `portfolio-chat-events-rag` - Portfolio Guru bot events
  - Others defined in terraform

### Inter-Service Communication
1. **Frontend → Backend**: HTTP REST (FastAPI, Nuxt)
2. **Backend → MCP Servers**: HTTP/MCP
3. **Services → LiteLLM**: HTTP proxy
4. **Services → Vertex AI**: Google Cloud client libraries
5. **Services → Cloud Pub/Sub**: Publisher/Subscriber pattern
6. **Services → Firestore**: Document operations
7. **Backend → Database**: asyncpg (async PostgreSQL)

### API Gateway (Kong)
- Central API management in Kubernetes
- Route 3rd-party APIs (CRM, calendar, telephony)
- Rate limiting, authentication

---

## 10. DATABASE & EXTERNAL SERVICE DEPENDENCIES

### Databases

**PostgreSQL**
- **Version**: 16+
- **Services**: HR Fraud detection, IQ Portal
- **Connection**: asyncpg (async)
- **ORM**: SQLAlchemy 2.0 with Alembic migrations
- **Schema**: User, Employee, Document, Analysis, createdAt/updatedAt timestamps
- **Deployment**: Cloud SQL (GCP)

**Firestore**
- **Services**: Rate limiting, conversation context, response caching
- **TTL**: 5-30 minutes (varies by use case)
- **Collections**: Conversations, rate_limits, cache

**Cloud Datastore**
- Used for portfolio catalog metadata

**Vertex AI**
- **Gemini 2.5 Flash** - Primary LLM (most services)
- **Gemini 2.5 Pro** - Advanced reasoning tasks
- **Claude (via Vertex AI)** - Alternative LLM
- **RAG Engine** - Document retrieval with grounding
- **Location**: us-central1 (Gemini), us-east5 (Claude), europe-west4 (RAG)

**LiteLLM Proxy**
- Central proxy for unified LLM access
- Routes to Gemini, Claude, OpenAI

## 11. DATA FLOW & ARCHITECTURE PATTERNS

### HR Fraud Detection Flow
```
Document Upload (Nuxt UI)
    ↓
FastAPI Backend (PostgreSQL)
    ↓
CrewAI Agents:
  - Document Extractor
  - Manipulation Detector (Image Forensics)
  - History Comparator
  - Fraud Matcher
  - Clinic Verifier
  - Risk Assessor
    ↓
Fraud Score (0.0-1.0)
    ↓
Human Review (Portal)
```

### RAG Ingestion Pipeline
```
Stage 1: GCS-First Sync
  Google Drive → GCS (direct stream, no local storage)
  Videos → Local temp/ for processing

Stage 2: Video Processing
  MP4 → Audio Extraction (ffmpeg)
  Audio → Transcription (Whisper)
  Frames → Gemini Vision Analysis
  Output: video_*.md files

Stage 3: Metadata Generation
  .gdrive_metadata.json → gdrive_links_mapping.json
  video_*.md → videos_catalog.json
  Upload to GCS root level
```

### Portfolio Guru Bot Flow
```
Google Chat Message
    ↓
Pub/Sub Topic
    ↓
Cloud Function (Gen2, python312)
    ↓
Rate Limiting Check (Firestore)
    ↓
RAG Engine Retrieval (Vertex AI)
    ↓
Gemini Refinement + Conversation Context
    ↓
Card Building (Google Chat Cards v2)
    ↓
Response Post to Chat API
```

### MCP Gateway Routing
```
Client Request
    ↓
MCP Gateway (Authentication)
    ↓
Authorization Check (Scopes)
    ↓
Route to MCP Server:
  - Google Search MCP
  - Channel Hub MCP
  - RAG Engine MCP
  - Document Processing MCPs
    ↓
Response Aggregation
```

## 12. INFRASTRUCTURE ORGANIZATION

### GCP Resource Hierarchy
```
Konecta Organization
├── shared-services/
│   ├── kd-ix-eur-shr-artifacts (Container Registry)
│   └── kd-ix-eur-shr-platform (ArgoCD GKE cluster)
├── dev/
│   ├── foundation/ (VPC, security)
│   ├── platform/ (GKE, Kong, LiteLLM)
│   └── workloads/ (Use case data)
├── qa/ (mirrors prod)
└── prod/ (production)
```

### Terraform Organization
```
terraform/
├── modules/
│   ├── gcp/ (base-environment, landing-zone, gke-autopilot, data-services)
│   └── kubernetes/ (gke-config, kong-gateway, litellm, argocd)
├── environments/
│   ├── bootstrap/ (org setup)
│   ├── shared/ (ArgoCD)
│   ├── dev/
│   ├── qa/
│   └── prod/
└── test/ (Terratest)
```

### State Management
- Separate state per service (reduced blast radius)
- GCS backend per environment
- Apply order: network → gke → platform → apps

## 15. SECURITY MODEL

### Authentication & Authorization
- **OAuth2** for Google Drive/API access
- **API Keys** (X-API-Key header) for service access
- **Workload Identity** for Kubernetes (no service account keys)
- **Service Accounts** with IAM roles
- **Scope-based authorization** in MCP Gateway

### Network Security
- **Private clusters** with Cloud NAT
- **Network Policies** with default-deny-ingress
- **Cloud Armor** WAF on load balancer
- **GCP health check IPs** (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22) allowed in policies

### Organization Policy Constraints
- EU regions only (eu-west1, eu-west4)
- No public IPs
- Domain-restricted IAM (@konecta-group.com)
- No service account key downloads
- Mandatory audit logging

---

## 16. NOTABLE PATTERNS & BEST PRACTICES

1. **Multi-stage Docker builds** - Optimized image sizes
2. **Async-first Python** - asyncio, asyncpg, async SQLAlchemy
3. **Type-safe ORM** - SQLAlchemy 2.0 with Mapped type hints
4. **Pydantic v2** - Schema validation with ConfigDict
5. **CrewAI Flows** - Structured multi-agent orchestration
6. **MCP for tool abstraction** - Unified interface to varied tools
7. **Terraform modules** - Reusable infrastructure components
8. **GCS-first architecture** - Direct cloud storage, no unnecessary caching
9. **Cloud Functions Gen2** - For Pub/Sub-driven workloads
10. **OpenTelemetry instrumentation** - Standardized observability

---

**Root-level files**:
- `downloader.sh` - Build helper script
- `.gitignore` - Repository-wide ignores
- Multiple CLAUDE.md files - AI assistant guidelines per service

**Key directories**:
- `/kon-*/` - Individual services (42 total)
- Dockerfile(s) per service (18+ Python, 2+ Node)
- CloudBuild YAML files (8 services)
- pyproject.toml / package.json - Dependency definitions
- requirements.txt - Python dependencies
- terraform/ - Shared infrastructure code

## CONCLUSION

The Konecta TIC platform is a **sophisticated, cloud-native AI automation ecosystem** comprising:

- **42 microservices** across multiple application domains
- **Hybrid stack**: Python (AI/backend) + JavaScript (frontend)
- **Enterprise infrastructure**: Terraform-managed GCP/GKE
- **Advanced AI capabilities**: CrewAI workflows, RAG, multi-agent orchestration
- **Production-grade deployment**: Cloud Run, GKE, ArgoCD GitOps
- **Security-first**: Workload Identity, network policies, secrets management
- **Observability**: OpenTelemetry, Prometheus, Grafana

The architecture emphasizes **modularity**, **security**, and **scalability** with clear separation between platform services, business applications, and experimental prototypes.

# SECURE DOCKER BUILDS

## 1. Base Image Strategy

**Decision: `python:3.x-slim` for Python, `node:20-alpine` builder + `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12` runtime for Node.js.**

| Stack | Builder Stage | Runtime Stage | Rationale |
|-------|--------------|---------------|-----------|
| Python (standard) | `python:3.x-slim` | `python:3.x-slim` | Distroless Python lacks pip/venv tooling needed at runtime for some deps; slim is the practical floor. ~120MB base. |
| Python (with C libs: opencv, whisper, ffmpeg) | `python:3.x-slim` + apt system deps | `python:3.x-slim` + runtime-only libs | Alpine uses musl libc -- binary incompatibility with many scientific/ML wheels (opencv-python, numpy). Slim (glibc) avoids costly source compilation. |
| Node.js | `node:20-alpine` | `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12` | No shell, no package manager, no libc utils in production. ~40MB final layer for the runtime base. |

**Pinning policy:**
- Pin to **full digest** in CI (`python:3.11.11-slim@sha256:abc123...`), updated monthly by Renovate/Dependabot.
- Pin to **minor version** in developer-facing Dockerfiles (`python:3.11-slim`) for readability; CI overrides with digest via `--build-arg`.
- **Never use `:latest`.** Never use unversioned tags.
- For Python 3.10-3.13 spread: parameterize via `ARG PYTHON_VERSION=3.11` in a shared base template; each service's build config sets its version.

---

## 2. Multi-Stage Build Patterns

### Python Services (FastAPI / FastMCP / CrewAI)

```
Stage 1: "builder"
  - FROM python:${PYTHON_VERSION}-slim
  - Install build-essential, gcc, libffi-dev (compile-time only)
  - COPY requirements.txt + constraints.txt
  - pip install --prefix=/install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt
  - Result: clean /install tree with all site-packages

Stage 2: "runtime"
  - FROM python:${PYTHON_VERSION}-slim
  - COPY --from=builder /install /usr/local
  - apt install runtime-only libs (libpq5, NOT libpq-dev)
  - COPY app source
  - Non-root USER, ENTRYPOINT
```

**For heavy-dep services (opencv, whisper, ffmpeg):** Add a dedicated `stage 0: system-deps` that installs ffmpeg, libgl1, libglib2.0-0 etc. into a known path, then `COPY --from=system-deps` only the runtime shared objects. This avoids re-downloading system deps on every code change.

### Node.js Services (Nuxt 3 / Vue 3)

```
Stage 1: "deps"
  - FROM node:20-alpine
  - COPY package.json + package-lock.json
  - npm ci (full deps including devDeps for build)

Stage 2: "builder"
  - FROM deps
  - COPY source
  - npm run build
  - npm prune --omit=dev (strip devDeps from node_modules)

Stage 3: "runtime"
  - FROM gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12
  - COPY --from=builder /app/.output (Nuxt) or /app/dist + node_modules
  - No shell. No npm. No package manager.
  - ENTRYPOINT ["node", ".output/server/index.mjs"]
```

---

## 3. Dependency Management

### Python
- **Adopt `pip-compile` (pip-tools) or `uv lock`** to generate pinned `requirements.txt` with hashes from a `requirements.in`. Commit both files.
- All installs use `pip install --no-cache-dir --require-hashes -r requirements.txt`.
- Constraint files for shared transitive deps (google-cloud-*, opentelemetry) to prevent version drift across the 30 services.
- **Migration path:** Existing `requirements.txt` files get hashes added via `pip-compile --generate-hashes`. One-time task per service.

### Node.js
- `npm ci` only (never `npm install` in CI). Requires `package-lock.json` committed.
- `.npmrc` in repo: `engine-strict=true`, `audit=false`, `fund=false`, `progress=false`.
- `package.json` declares `"engines": { "node": ">=20" }`.

### Both
- Renovate/Dependabot PRs for dependency updates on a weekly cadence, grouped by ecosystem.
- Vulnerability scanning at build time (covered in CI/CD section, but Trivy runs against the final image).

---

## 4. Security Hardening

**Non-root USER (mandatory for all services):**
```dockerfile
RUN groupadd -r -g 65532 nonroot && useradd -r -u 65532 -g nonroot nonroot
USER 65532:65532
```
- UID/GID 65532 matches distroless convention. Consistent across all images.
- Kubernetes `securityContext.runAsNonRoot: true` enforces this at the pod level as a safety net.

**Read-only filesystem support:**
- No writes to application directory at runtime. All writable paths go to explicit `VOLUME` declarations or `emptyDir` mounts in k8s.
- Typical writable paths: `/tmp`, `/app/logs` (if not stdout-only), `/app/.cache` (for ML model caches in whisper/crewai).
- Kubernetes enforces with `readOnlyRootFilesystem: true` + explicit `emptyDir` volume mounts for writable paths.

**No shell in production (Node.js):**
- Distroless runtime has no `/bin/sh`. Prevents exec-into-container attacks.
- For Python slim: remove shell post-install where feasible, but some services (entrypoint scripts doing migrations) need it. Flag these as exceptions with a comment in the Dockerfile.

**Drop capabilities:**
- All containers run with `drop: [ALL]` in the k8s pod security context. No capabilities added back unless explicitly justified (documented exception).
- No `--privileged`. No `SYS_ADMIN`. No `NET_RAW`.

**Additional:**
- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` set in all Python images.
- No `.env` files baked into images. All config via k8s secrets/configmaps.
- `.dockerignore` excludes `.git/`, `.env*`, `__pycache__/`, `*.pyc`, `tests/`, `docs/`.

---

## 5. Layer Optimization

**Instruction ordering (most stable to least stable):**
1. `FROM` + system package installs (changes monthly)
2. `COPY requirements.txt` / `package-lock.json` (changes on dep updates)
3. `RUN pip install` / `npm ci` (rebuilds only when lock file changes)
4. `COPY . .` (changes on every commit)
5. `RUN build` (changes on every commit)
6. Metadata labels (changes on every commit, but is a zero-cost layer)

**BuildKit cache mounts (replace inline cache from Cloud Build):**
```dockerfile
RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.cache/pip \
    pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.npm \
    npm ci
```
- Cache mounts persist across builds on the same builder node. In k8s CI (Kaniko/BuildKit daemon), mount a PVC for the cache directory.
- `--mount=type=cache` is NOT exported to the final image. Zero size impact.

**.dockerignore (standardized across all repos):**
```
.git/
.github/
.vscode/
.idea/
__pycache__/
*.pyc
*.pyo
node_modules/
.env*
!.env.example
tests/
test/
docs/
*.md
!README.md
Dockerfile
docker-compose*.yml
.dockerignore
coverage/
.pytest_cache/
.mypy_cache/
.ruff_cache/
dist/
build/
.output/
```

**Reduce layer count:** Chain related `RUN` commands with `&&`. Single `apt-get update && apt-get install -y ... && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*` per stage.

---

## 6. Standardized Dockerfile Templates

Two canonical templates maintained in a shared `platform/docker-templates/` directory. Services reference these via symlink or copy-and-customize.

### Python Template Skeleton

```
ARG PYTHON_VERSION=3.11
FROM python:${PYTHON_VERSION}-slim AS builder
  - ARG for extra apt packages (empty by default; opencv/ffmpeg services override)
  - Install build deps + extra apt packages
  - COPY requirements.txt
  - pip install --prefix=/install --require-hashes

FROM python:${PYTHON_VERSION}-slim AS runtime
  - COPY --from=builder /install /usr/local
  - Install runtime-only apt packages (parameterized)
  - Create nonroot user (65532)
  - COPY app source with --chown=nonroot
  - ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
  - EXPOSE ${PORT:-8080}
  - USER 65532
  - HEALTHCHECK (curl-free; use python urllib)
  - ENTRYPOINT ["python", "-m", "uvicorn", "app.main:app"]
```

Customization points exposed as `ARG`s: `PYTHON_VERSION`, `APT_BUILD_DEPS`, `APT_RUNTIME_DEPS`, `PORT`, `ENTRYPOINT_CMD`.

### Node.js Template Skeleton

```
FROM node:20-alpine AS deps
  - COPY package.json, package-lock.json
  - npm ci

FROM deps AS builder
  - COPY source
  - npm run build
  - npm prune --omit=dev

FROM gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12 AS runtime
  - COPY --from=builder /app/.output (Nuxt SSR)
  - ENV NODE_ENV=production, PORT=3000
  - EXPOSE 3000
  - USER 65532
  - ENTRYPOINT ["node", ".output/server/index.mjs"]
```

No HEALTHCHECK in Dockerfile for Node.js distroless (no curl/wget available). Health checks defined at the k8s pod spec level using `httpGet` probes.

---

## 7. Build-Time Metadata

All images include OCI-standard labels, injected via build args from CI:

```dockerfile
ARG BUILD_DATE
ARG VCS_REF
ARG VERSION
ARG BUILD_ID

LABEL org.opencontainers.image.created="${BUILD_DATE}" \
      org.opencontainers.image.revision="${VCS_REF}" \
      org.opencontainers.image.version="${VERSION}" \
      org.opencontainers.image.source="https://github.com/org/repo" \
      org.opencontainers.image.title="${SERVICE_NAME}" \
      org.opencontainers.image.vendor="Konecta" \
      build.id="${BUILD_ID}"
```

- `VCS_REF` = full git SHA. Enables `docker inspect` to trace any running image back to its exact commit.
- `VERSION` = semver or git-describe output.
- `BUILD_ID` = CI pipeline run ID for audit trail.
- Labels go in the **final stage only** to avoid layer leakage from builder stages.

---

## 8. Health Check Integration

**Strategy: Kubernetes-native probes preferred over Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK.**

Dockerfile `HEALTHCHECK` is useful for local dev / docker-compose but ignored by Kubernetes. Define health endpoints in application code; reference them from k8s manifests.

| Probe Type | Python (FastAPI) | Node.js (Nuxt) |
|-----------|-----------------|-----------------|
| Liveness | `GET /healthz` -- returns 200 if process is alive | `GET /healthz` -- returns 200 |
| Readiness | `GET /readyz` -- checks DB connection pool, downstream deps | `GET /readyz` -- checks SSR renderer ready |
| Startup | Same as readiness, with `failureThreshold: 30`, `periodSeconds: 2` | Same pattern, tuned for cold start |

**For Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK (docker-compose / local dev only):**
- Python: `HEALTHCHECK CMD python -c "import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('http://localhost:8080/healthz')"` -- no curl dependency.
- Node.js (distroless): Omit from Dockerfile. Use docker-compose `healthcheck` override if needed.

**Timeouts:**
- `initialDelaySeconds`: 5s (standard), 30s (ML model loading services like whisper/crewai).
- `timeoutSeconds`: 3s.
- `periodSeconds`: 10s.

---

## 9. Image Size Targets and Optimization

### Targets

| Category | Target | Baseline (current estimate) |
|----------|--------|-----------------------------|
| Python standard (FastAPI, FastMCP) | < 200 MB | ~350-450 MB |
| Python heavy (CrewAI, whisper, opencv) | < 500 MB | ~800 MB - 1.2 GB |
| Node.js (Nuxt SSR) | < 80 MB | ~200-300 MB |

### Optimization Techniques (ordered by impact)

1. **Multi-stage builds** (already covered) -- single biggest win. Builder deps do not ship.
2. **Distroless for Node.js** -- eliminates npm, yarn, shell, apk, ~150 MB of tooling.
3. **`apt-get --no-install-recommends`** + `rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*` -- prevents bloat from suggested packages.
4. **`pip install --no-cache-dir`** -- avoids caching wheels in the image. Combined with `--prefix=/install` and copying only the install tree.
5. **Strip unnecessary files post-install:**
   - `find /usr/local -name '*.pyc' -delete`
   - `find /usr/local -name '__pycache__' -type d -exec rm -rf {} +`
   - `find /usr/local -name 'tests' -type d -exec rm -rf {} +`
6. **For opencv-python:** Use `opencv-python-headless` instead of `opencv-python` (saves ~50 MB of Qt/GUI libs).
7. **For whisper/ML services:** Pre-download models into a separate layer or external volume, not baked into the image. Model files (1-10 GB) must not be in the container image.
8. **npm prune --omit=dev** before copying `node_modules` into runtime stage.
9. **Single-binary consideration:** For any future Go/Rust sidecar utilities, build static binaries and copy into `scratch` or distroless. Not applicable to current Python/Node stack but noted for future services.

### Monitoring
- CI pipeline reports final image size. PR checks fail if image exceeds category target by > 20%.
- Weekly Trivy scan report includes image size trend per service.

Perfect. I've created a comprehensive CI/CD Pipeline Architecture document for your Kubernetes migration. Here's what's included:

## Document Overview

The architecture covers all 9 required sections plus supplementary material:

### Core Sections Delivered

1. **GitHub Actions Workflow Strategy** - Monorepo with path-based triggers + reusable workflows pattern, avoiding duplication across 42 services

2. **Docker Build Pipeline** - BuildKit with layer caching, multi-stage builds for Python/Node, build args strategy, performance optimization targets (<5 min cached)

3. **Image Tagging Strategy** - Multi-dimensional tagging (commit SHA, semver, environment, branch) with immutability enforcement and clear promotion flow

4. **Security Gates** - Trivy vulnerability scanning with severity thresholds, Syft SBOM generation in dual formats, Cosign signing via GitHub OIDC (no long-lived keys), policy enforcement at Kubernetes layer

5. **GitOps Flow** - Complete end-to-end flow from PR merge → build → scan → push → Helm values update → ArgoCD sync, with clear separation of concerns

6. **Environment Promotion** - Dev (auto-sync), QA (manual with 08:00-18:00 UTC window), Prod (manual with Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC window), including prerequisites and validation gates

7. **Rollback Strategy** - Automated rollback on error rate/latency thresholds via Git commit revert (5 min RTO target), manual rollback procedures, data considerations

8. **Monorepo Considerations** - Path-based change detection, dynamic matrix for parallel builds (reducing 336→40 min), selective deployment via impact maps, blast radius mitigation

9. **GitHub OIDC Secrets Management** - One-time Workload Identity Pool setup, dynamic secret fetching from GCP Secret Manager, zero long-lived credentials, full audit trail

### Additional Value Added

- **Summary table** of 10 key architectural decisions with rationale
- **Pipeline metrics & observability** section covering KPIs, instrumentation, alerting
- **Migration roadmap** (4 phases, 6 weeks) with delivery checkpoints
- **Tools appendix** with versions and purposes
- Detailed flow diagrams for GitOps and rollback procedures

## Key Architectural Highlights

**Zero Long-Lived Credentials**: GitHub OIDC + Workload Identity Pool replaces service account JSON keys entirely. Tokens are ~1 hour, auto-refreshing, audit-trail enabled.

**Fast Feedback at Scale**: Path-based triggers + parallel builds reduce sequential (336 min) to parallel (40 min) for 42 services. Cache hit times average 3 min.

**Safety by Design**: Immutable SHA-based tagging, signed images (Cosign), vulnerability scanning, SBOM generation, and automated rollbacks triggered by error rate thresholds.

**Auditability**: Every image linked to commit, build timestamp, scan results, SBOM, and signature. Rollbacks visible as Git commits, enabling compliance and incident response.

**Environment Gates**: Change windows enforced for QA/prod. Manual sync gates prevent accidental production deployments. Dev auto-syncs for velocity.

This document is ready to integrate as the CI/CD Pipeline section of your larger architecture blueprint.

# KUBERNETES & HELM CHARTS

## 1. Helm Chart Strategy

**Hybrid Approach: Base Chart Library + Per-Service Charts**

- **Base Chart Library** (`base-service-chart/`): Reusable template library with common patterns
  - Not deployed directly; used as dependency via `dependencies` in Chart.yaml
  - Contains standard templates: deployment, service, ingress, HPA, PodDisruptionBudget
  - Parameterized for Python/FastAPI, Node.js/Nuxt, and MCP server patterns
  - Reduces duplication across 42 services while maintaining flexibility

- **Per-Service Charts** (`charts/<service-name>/`): Lightweight wrappers
  - Minimal Chart.yaml with base chart dependency
  - Service-specific values.yaml with configuration
  - Custom templates only when service has unique requirements
  - Clear ownership per service team

- **Umbrella Charts by Domain** (optional): Group related services
  - `charts/ai-workflows/`: CrewAI workflows cluster
  - `charts/mcp-servers/`: MCP protocol services cluster
  - `charts/web-services/`: Nuxt 3 frontends
  - Simplifies deployment of service groups, not mandatory

**Rationale**: 42 services require standardization (base chart) but domain diversity requires flexibility (per-service charts). Avoids monolithic umbrella chart while preventing template sprawl.

---

## 2. Chart Structure Patterns

### Python Services (FastAPI/FastMCP)

```
charts/<service-name>/
├── Chart.yaml              # Depends on base-service-chart
├── values.yaml             # Service defaults
├── values-dev.yaml
├── values-qa.yaml
├── values-prod.yaml
└── templates/
    └── custom-*.yaml       # Only if needed (Jobs, CronJobs, etc.)
```

**Key values.yaml sections**:
- `image`: Repository, tag strategy (semantic versioning)
- `service.port`: 8000 (FastAPI standard)
- `resources`: AI workload profile (see section 4)
- `probes`: HTTP /health and /ready endpoints
- `env`: Config from ConfigMaps/Secrets
- `autoscaling`: HPA configuration for load-sensitive services
- `serviceAccount`: For Workload Identity (GCP service account binding)

### Node.js Services (Nuxt 3)

**Similar structure, different defaults**:
- `service.port`: 3000 (Nuxt default)
- `resources`: Frontend profile (lower CPU/memory)
- `probes`: HTTP / or /_health
- `env`: Runtime config, API endpoints
- `nodeSelector`: General-purpose node pools (no GPU)

### MCP Server Services

**Additional considerations**:
- `service.type`: ClusterIP with SSE support
- `probes.initialDelaySeconds`: Longer for SSE initialization
- `env`: MCP protocol configuration
- `networkPolicy`: Explicit allow rules for SSE long-lived connections

---

## 3. Namespace Strategy

**Multi-Dimensional: Environment + Domain Boundaries**

### Primary Strategy: Environment-Domain Namespaces

- `dev-ai-workflows`: CrewAI services in development
- `dev-api-services`: FastAPI microservices in development
- `dev-mcp-servers`: MCP protocol services in development
- `dev-web-apps`: Nuxt frontends in development
- `qa-ai-workflows`, `qa-api-services`, `qa-mcp-servers`, `qa-web-apps`
- `prod-ai-workflows`, `prod-api-services`, `prod-mcp-servers`, `prod-web-apps`

### Shared Infrastructure Namespaces

- `shared-argocd`: ArgoCD installation (already exists)
- `shared-kong`: Kong API Gateway
- `shared-litellm`: LiteLLM proxy
- `shared-monitoring`: Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger
- `shared-cert-manager`: TLS certificate management

### Namespace Configuration

- **ResourceQuotas**: Per namespace to prevent resource exhaustion
  - Dev: Lower limits, encourage efficient testing
  - QA: Mid-tier limits
  - Prod: Generous limits with monitoring
- **NetworkPolicies**: Default deny ingress, explicit allow rules
- **RBAC**: Service accounts per namespace, least privilege
- **LimitRanges**: Default resource requests/limits for pods without explicit values

**Rationale**: 12 application namespaces (4 domains × 3 envs) + 5 shared = 17 total. Balances isolation, resource control, and RBAC boundaries without excessive fragmentation.

---

## 4. Resource Management

### AI Workload Profile (CrewAI, AI-Heavy FastAPI)

**Requests/Limits**:
- CPU: `requests: 500m`, `limits: 2000m` (2:1 or 4:1 ratio)
- Memory: `requests: 1Gi`, `limits: 2Gi` (2:1 ratio)
- **Rationale**: AI workloads spike during inference; allow bursting but guarantee baseline

**Node Selection**:
- `nodeSelector: workload-type: ai` (dedicated node pools)
- Consider GPU node pools if LLM inference moves in-cluster
- Avoid preemptible nodes for prod AI services

### Web Services Profile (FastAPI APIs, MCP Servers)

**Requests/Limits**:
- CPU: `requests: 100m`, `limits: 500m` (5:1 ratio)
- Memory: `requests: 256Mi`, `limits: 512Mi` (2:1 ratio)
- **Rationale**: Stateless HTTP services, lower resource needs, efficient bin-packing

**Scaling**:
- Higher replica counts (3-10 pods) vs AI workloads (1-3 pods)
- Lower per-pod resources, more horizontal scale

### Frontend Profile (Nuxt 3)

**Requests/Limits**:
- CPU: `requests: 50m`, `limits: 200m`
- Memory: `requests: 128Mi`, `limits: 256Mi`
- **Rationale**: Primarily serving static assets and SSR, minimal compute

**Optimization**:
- CDN offloading for static assets (Cloud CDN)
- Aggressive HPA for traffic spikes

### Database Clients & Long-Running Services

**Requests/Limits**:
- CPU: `requests: 200m`, `limits: 1000m`
- Memory: `requests: 512Mi`, `limits: 1Gi`
- **Connection pooling**: Set limits in ConfigMaps to prevent Cloud SQL exhaustion

---

## 5. Health Checks and Readiness Probes

### FastAPI Services Pattern

```yaml
livenessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /health
    port: 8000
  initialDelaySeconds: 15
  periodSeconds: 20
  timeoutSeconds: 5
  failureThreshold: 3

readinessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /ready
    port: 8000
  initialDelaySeconds: 10
  periodSeconds: 5
  timeoutSeconds: 3
  failureThreshold: 2
```

**Implementation Requirements**:
- `/health`: Fast liveness check (responds "OK", checks process alive)
- `/ready`: Readiness check (validates DB connections, external dependencies)
- **Critical**: Readiness must fail fast (< 3s timeout) to prevent cascading delays

### Nuxt 3 Services Pattern

```yaml
livenessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /
    port: 3000
  initialDelaySeconds: 10
  periodSeconds: 15

readinessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /_health  # Custom endpoint or /
    port: 3000
  initialDelaySeconds: 5
  periodSeconds: 5
```

### MCP Server Pattern

**Special considerations for SSE**:
- `initialDelaySeconds: 30` (SSE initialization slower)
- `periodSeconds: 30` (long-lived connections, less frequent checks)
- `successThreshold: 1` (single success = ready)
- **Probe endpoint**: Non-SSE HTTP endpoint (e.g., `/health` vs SSE on `/sse`)

### Startup Probes for AI Workloads

**Add startup probe for model-loading services**:
```yaml
startupProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /health
    port: 8000
  initialDelaySeconds: 0
  periodSeconds: 10
  failureThreshold: 30  # 5 minutes total
```
- Prevents premature liveness failures during slow model initialization
- Only use for AI services with long startup times

---

## 6. HPA/Scaling Strategy

### Services Requiring HPA

**Tier 1 - Aggressive HPA (User-Facing)**:
- **Nuxt 3 frontends**: Traffic-driven scaling
  - Metric: CPU 60%, custom metric (request rate)
  - Min: 2, Max: 20, Scale down: 5 min stabilization
- **Public API gateways** (if any FastAPI services are user-facing)
  - Metric: CPU 70%, memory 80%
  - Min: 3, Max: 15

**Tier 2 - Moderate HPA (Internal Services)**:
- **FastAPI microservices** (internal APIs)
  - Metric: CPU 75%
  - Min: 2, Max: 10, Scale down: 10 min stabilization
- **MCP servers**
  - Metric: CPU 70%, custom metric (active SSE connections)
  - Min: 2, Max: 8

**Tier 3 - Conservative HPA (AI Workloads)**:
- **CrewAI workflows**
  - Metric: CPU 80%, custom metric (queue depth via Pub/Sub)
  - Min: 1, Max: 5, Scale down: 15 min stabilization
  - **Rationale**: Higher per-pod cost, avoid thrashing

### Services NOT Requiring HPA

- **Stateful services**: MCP servers with sticky sessions (use static replicas)
- **Singletons**: Services with global state or locking requirements
- **Low-traffic internal tools**: Fixed 1-2 replicas sufficient

### Scaling Triggers

**Primary Metrics**:
- CPU utilization (default for most services)
- Memory utilization (for cache-heavy services)

**Custom Metrics (via Prometheus Adapter)**:
- Request rate (requests/sec per pod)
- Queue depth (Pub/Sub subscription lag)
- Active SSE connections (for MCP servers)
- P95 latency (scale up if degrading)

**Behavior Configuration**:
- `scaleDown.stabilizationWindowSeconds`: Prevent flapping
- `scaleDown.policies`: Max 50% pods down per 5 min
- `scaleUp.policies`: Double pods every 60s (aggressive)

---

## 7. Service Mesh Consideration

**Recommendation: NO (Initially), YES (Future Phase)**

### Why Not Now

- **Complexity overhead**: 42 services migration is already high-risk
- **GKE Autopilot native features**: Built-in load balancing, health checking sufficient
- **Existing Kong Gateway**: Already handles ingress traffic management, auth, rate limiting
- **Team expertise**: Avoid introducing new operational burden during migration

### When to Introduce (Post-Migration)

**Adopt service mesh when**:
- **mTLS required**: Zero-trust security between microservices
- **Advanced traffic management**: Canary deployments, circuit breakers become critical
- **Observability gaps**: Need deeper distributed tracing beyond OpenTelemetry
- **Multi-cluster**: If expanding beyond single GKE cluster

**Recommended Mesh**: **Istio** (if needed later)
- Strong GKE integration (ASM - Anthos Service Mesh)
- Mature ecosystem for 40+ service scale
- OpenTelemetry integration

**Alternative**: **Linkerd** if simplicity prioritized over features

---

## 8. Ingress Architecture

### Kong Integration Strategy

**Topology**:
```
Internet → GCP Load Balancer → Kong Ingress (shared-kong namespace)
         → Service Mesh (future) → Application Services
```

**Kong as Primary Ingress Controller**:
- Already deployed in shared-kong namespace
- Handles TLS termination, auth, rate limiting, API composition
- KongIngress CRDs for per-service routing rules

### TLS Termination Strategy

**Layer 1: GCP Load Balancer (Optional)**:
- External HTTPS Load Balancer for prod
- Terminates TLS at LB, forwards HTTP to Kong (if within VPC)
- **Benefit**: Offloads TLS from cluster

**Layer 2: Kong (Primary)**:
- TLS termination at Kong Gateway
- Certificates managed by cert-manager
- Automated Let's Encrypt for external domains

**Implementation**:
- `cert-manager` in shared-cert-manager namespace
- `ClusterIssuer` for Let's Encrypt production
- `Certificate` resources per domain
- Kong references Secrets created by cert-manager

### Ingress Patterns Per Service Type

**External APIs (Nuxt, Public FastAPI)**:
```yaml
# In service Helm chart
ingress:
  enabled: true
  className: kong
  annotations:
    konghq.com/strip-path: "true"
    konghq.com/plugins: rate-limiting, cors
  hosts:
    - host: api.example.com
      paths:
        - path: /service-name
          pathType: Prefix
  tls:
    - secretName: api-example-com-tls
      hosts:
        - api.example.com
```

**Internal Services (MCP, Internal APIs)**:
- No Ingress resource (ClusterIP only)
- Accessible via internal DNS (service.namespace.svc.cluster.local)
- Kong plugins for internal auth (if exposed via Kong)

### Multi-Environment Ingress

- **Dev**: `dev.api.example.com` (single wildcard cert)
- **QA**: `qa.api.example.com`
- **Prod**: `api.example.com` (multi-domain cert)

**DNS Management**:
- External DNS controller (if not using Terraform)
- Automatic DNS records from Ingress resources

---

## 9. ConfigMap/Secret Patterns

### Helm Values Hierarchy

**4-Layer Configuration Strategy**:

1. **Base Chart Defaults** (`base-service-chart/values.yaml`)
   - Common defaults: replicas: 2, resources, probes
   - Template-level configuration

2. **Service Defaults** (`charts/<service>/values.yaml`)
   - Service-specific config: image repo, service port, domain-specific resources
   - Environment-agnostic settings

3. **Environment Overrides** (`charts/<service>/values-{env}.yaml`)
   - `values-dev.yaml`: Lower resources, debug logging, dev DB endpoints
   - `values-qa.yaml`: Mid-tier resources, QA DB endpoints
   - `values-prod.yaml`: Production resources, prod DB endpoints, stricter policies

4. **Secrets (External)** (Sealed Secrets or External Secrets Operator)
   - Database credentials, API keys, service account keys
   - Not in Helm values files

### ConfigMap Patterns

**Application Configuration**:
```yaml
# In Helm values.yaml
configMap:
  data:
    LOG_LEVEL: "info"
    DATABASE_POOL_SIZE: "10"
    LITELLM_ENDPOINT: "http://litellm.shared-litellm.svc.cluster.local:8000"
    OTEL_EXPORTER_ENDPOINT: "http://otel-collector.shared-monitoring.svc.cluster.local:4317"
```

**Shared ConfigMaps** (created separately, referenced in Helm):
- `shared-endpoints`: Common service endpoints (LiteLLM, Kong, databases)
- `shared-otel-config`: OpenTelemetry configuration
- Per-environment ConfigMaps in each namespace

### Secret Management

**Recommendation: External Secrets Operator**

**Why**:
- Integrates with Google Secret Manager
- Secrets stored in GCP, referenced in K8s
- No secrets in Git (Helm charts reference ExternalSecret CRDs)
- Automatic rotation support

**Pattern**:
```yaml
# In service Helm chart templates/external-secret.yaml
apiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1
kind: ExternalSecret
metadata:
  name: {{ .Values.service.name }}-secrets
spec:
  secretStoreRef:
    name: gcpsm-secret-store  # Shared SecretStore per environment
  target:
    name: {{ .Values.service.name }}-secrets
  data:
    - secretKey: DB_PASSWORD
      remoteRef:
        key: {{ .Values.environment }}-{{ .Values.service.name }}-db-password
```

**Alternative: Sealed Secrets**:
- Encrypted secrets in Git
- Sealed Secrets controller decrypts in-cluster
- Less dynamic than External Secrets Operator

### Workload Identity for GCP

**Service Account Annotation**:
```yaml
# In Helm values.yaml
serviceAccount:
  create: true
  annotations:
    iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: <service-name>@<project-id>.iam.gserviceaccount.com
```
- No key files needed; Workload Identity provides transparent auth
- Terraform handles GCP service account ↔ K8s service account binding

---

## 10. ArgoCD ApplicationSet Strategy

### Recommended Strategy: Matrix Generator (Environment × Service)

**Single ApplicationSet for All 42 Services**:

```yaml
# apps/applicationset-microservices.yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: ApplicationSet
metadata:
  name: microservices
  namespace: shared-argocd
spec:
  generators:
    - matrix:
        generators:
          # Generator 1: Environments
          - list:
              elements:
                - env: dev
                  cluster: gke-dev-eu-west1
                  namespace: dev-api-services  # Override per domain
                - env: qa
                  cluster: gke-qa-eu-west1
                  namespace: qa-api-services
                - env: prod
                  cluster: gke-prod-eu-west4
                  namespace: prod-api-services
          
          # Generator 2: Services (Git directory discovery)
          - git:
              repoURL: https://github.com/org/kix-infrastructure
              revision: HEAD
              directories:
                - path: charts/*
              exclude: charts/base-service-chart
  
  template:
    metadata:
      name: '{{path.basename}}-{{env}}'
      labels:
        environment: '{{env}}'
        service: '{{path.basename}}'
    spec:
      project: default
      source:
        repoURL: https://github.com/org/kix-infrastructure
        targetRevision: HEAD
        path: '{{path}}'
        helm:
          valueFiles:
            - values.yaml
            - values-{{env}}.yaml
      destination:
        server: '{{cluster}}'
        namespace: '{{namespace}}'
      syncPolicy:
        automated:
          prune: true
          selfHeal: true
        syncOptions:
          - CreateNamespace=true
        retry:
          limit: 5
          backoff:
            duration: 5s
            factor: 2
            maxDuration: 3m
```

### Alternative: Multiple ApplicationSets by Domain

**If matrix becomes unwieldy**:
- `applicationset-ai-workflows.yaml`: AI services only
- `applicationset-api-services.yaml`: FastAPI microservices
- `applicationset-mcp-servers.yaml`: MCP protocol services
- `applicationset-web-apps.yaml`: Nuxt frontends

**Benefits**:
- Domain-specific sync policies (e.g., slower rollout for AI)
- Different Git paths or repos per domain
- Clearer ownership per team

### Sync Policies by Environment

**Development**:
```yaml
syncPolicy:
  automated:
    prune: true
    selfHeal: true  # Aggressive auto-sync
  syncOptions:
    - CreateNamespace=true
```

**QA**:
```yaml
syncPolicy:
  automated:
    prune: true
    selfHeal: false  # Manual approval for healing
```

**Production**:
```yaml
syncPolicy:
  automated:
    prune: false
    selfHeal: false  # Fully manual sync
  syncOptions:
    - PruneLast=true  # Delete resources last during sync
```

### ArgoCD Project Structure

**Projects for RBAC**:
- `dev-project`: All dev-* namespaces
- `qa-project`: All qa-* namespaces
- `prod-project`: All prod-* namespaces, requires approvers

**Prevents**:
- Dev team deploying to prod
- Cross-environment contamination

### Health Checks and Sync Waves

**Sync Waves for Dependencies**:
```yaml
# In base-service-chart/templates/deployment.yaml
metadata:
  annotations:
    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "10"  # Apps deploy after infra

# In shared infrastructure (ConfigMaps, Secrets)
metadata:
  annotations:
    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "0"  # Infra first
```

**Custom Health Checks**:
- ArgoCD CRD health checks for ExternalSecrets
- Custom Lua scripts for MCP server readiness (if needed)

### Rollout Strategy

**Progressive Delivery**:
- Dev → auto-sync on commit
- QA → auto-sync with 5-minute delay
- Prod → manual approval + Argo Rollouts (future phase)

**Image Tag Strategy**:
- Dev: `latest` or commit SHA
- QA: Semantic version tags (`v1.2.3`)
- Prod: Immutable semantic version tags

**GitHub Actions Integration**:
- CI builds image, pushes to GCR/Artifact Registry
- CI updates Helm chart values-{env}.yaml with new image tag
- CI commits to Git → ArgoCD detects change → syncs

---

## Summary Architecture Decisions

| **Aspect** | **Decision** | **Rationale** |
|------------|-------------|---------------|
| **Helm Strategy** | Base chart library + per-service charts | Balance standardization (42 services) with flexibility |
| **Namespaces** | Environment-domain hybrid (12 app + 5 shared) | Isolation, RBAC, resource quotas without fragmentation |
| **Resources** | 3 profiles (AI/Web/Frontend) | Right-size for workload types, cost optimization |
| **Scaling** | Tiered HPA (aggressive→conservative) | User-facing scales fast, AI workloads scale carefully |
| **Service Mesh** | No (initially), Istio (future) | Reduce migration complexity, add when mTLS/advanced traffic needed |
| **Ingress** | Kong primary, cert-manager TLS | Leverage existing Kong, automate certificates |
| **Secrets** | External Secrets Operator + GCP Secret Manager | No secrets in Git, Workload Identity integration |
| **ArgoCD** | Matrix ApplicationSet (env × service) | Single source of truth for 42 services across 3 envs |

**Key Operational Patterns**:
- Automated dev/qa, manual prod sync policies
- Health checks aligned with service types (SSE, HTTP, AI startup times)
- Progressive rollout via Git tags and ArgoCD sync waves
- Resource quotas per namespace to prevent runaway costs

     1→# CI/CD Pipeline Architecture
     2→## Kubernetes Migration from GCP Cloud Run
