     4→---
     5→
     6→## 1. GitHub Actions Workflow Strategy
     7→
     8→### Monorepo Structure
     9→- **Approach**: Monorepo with path-based triggers + reusable workflows
    10→- **Rationale**: 42 microservices require efficient change detection and parallel builds without duplicating workflow code
    11→- **Implementation**:
    12→  - Single `workflows/` directory housing all CI/CD logic
    13→  - Reusable workflows for common tasks (build, scan, push, deploy)
    14→  - Service-level workflows triggered by paths: `services/*/` changes trigger builds for that service only
    15→  - Centralized shared workflows prevent drift and reduce maintenance burden
    16→
    17→### Workflow Architecture
    18→```
    19→Reusable Workflows (callable from any service)
    20→├── build-image.yaml       # Docker build, layer caching, BuildKit
    21→├── scan-image.yaml        # Trivy scanning, SBOM generation, cosign signing
    22→├── push-registry.yaml     # Push to Artifact Registry with tagging strategy
    23→├── deploy-helm.yaml       # Update Helm values, trigger ArgoCD sync
    24→└── validate-rollback.yaml # Verify rollback readiness
    25→
    26→Service-Level Workflows
    27→├── services/api-service/workflow.yaml
    28→├── services/auth-service/workflow.yaml
    29→└── ... (one per service or grouping)
    30→
    31→Shared Workflows
    32→├── terraform-validate.yaml (existing)
    33→├── terraform-deploy.yaml (existing)
    34→└── quality-nightly.yaml (existing)
    35→```
    36→
    37→### Call Flow
    38→1. Developer pushes to feature branch → PR validators run (lint, unit tests)
    39→2. Merge to main branch → service workflow triggered by path filter
    40→3. Service workflow calls reusable workflows in sequence: build → scan → push → deploy
    41→4. ArgoCD watches git repository (Helm values) and auto-syncs dev, manual-syncs prod
    42→
    43→### Advantages
    44→- **No duplication**: Reusable workflows define build logic once
    45→- **Selective execution**: Only modified services rebuild
    46→- **Parallel runs**: Multiple services can build simultaneously
    47→- **Easy scaling**: New services inherit workflow pattern
    48→- **Clear separation**: Build pipeline separate from deploy pipeline (ArgoCD handles CD)
    49→
    50→---
    51→
    52→## 2. Docker Build Pipeline
    53→
    54→### BuildKit Configuration
    55→- **Builder**: Use GitHub Actions native BuildKit (buildx)
    56→- **Layer caching**: Leverage GitHub Actions cache backend for multi-platform builds
    57→- **Cache strategy**:
    58→  - Primary: `type=gha,mode=max` (stores all build layers)
    59→  - Fallback: Build without cache on cache miss
    60→  - Scope caches by service to prevent cross-service pollution
    61→
    62→### Multi-Stage Build Pattern
    63→- **Python services**:
    64→  - Stage 1 (builder): Full pip dependencies, compile wheels, run tests
    65→  - Stage 2 (runtime): Copy only wheels, slim base image (python:3.12-slim)
    66→  - Result: ~500MB → ~150MB image reduction
    67→- **Node.js services**:
    68→  - Stage 1 (build): Node build tools, run npm build, generate artifacts
    69→  - Stage 2 (runtime): Serve artifacts with lightweight node:20-alpine or distroless
    70→  - Result: ~800MB → ~100MB image reduction
    71→
    72→### Build Arguments & Secrets
    73→- **Build args passed via GitHub Actions context**:
    74→  - `BUILD_DATE`: Current timestamp
    75→  - `VCS_REF`: Git SHA for traceability
    76→  - `VERSION`: Git tag or semantic version
    77→  - `SERVICE_NAME`: For multi-service builds
    78→- **Secrets handling**:
    79→  - NPM_TOKEN, PIP_TOKEN, etc. passed as `--secret` flags (not ENV vars in image)
    80→  - Secrets never appear in layer history
    81→  - Use GitHub OIDC (see Section 9) to fetch secrets dynamically, not store in Actions secrets
    82→
    83→### Performance Optimization
    84→- **Cache invalidation strategy**:
    85→  - `package.json` / `pyproject.toml` changes invalidate dependency layer
    86→  - Source code changes reuse dependency cache
    87→  - Build args (VERSION) do NOT invalidate cache (use labels instead)
    88→- **Parallel builds**: Service workflows run independently; buildx supports multi-platform (amd64, arm64) in single build
    89→- **Minimal base images**:
    90→  - Python: `python:3.12-slim` or distroless
    91→  - Node: `node:20-alpine` or distroless
    92→  - Go: `scratch` or `distroless/base`
    93→- **Time target**: <5 min per service (with cache hit), <12 min cold build
    94→
    95→---
    96→
    97→## 3. Image Tagging Strategy
    98→
    99→### Tag Format
   100→Each image receives multiple tags for different purposes:
   101→
   102→| Tag | Format | Example | Purpose |
   103→|-----|--------|---------|---------|
   104→| **Commit SHA** | `{service}:sha-{7-char-sha}` | `api-service:sha-a1b2c3d` | Unique build identifier, maps PR→image |
   105→| **Semver** | `{service}:v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | `api-service:v2.3.1` | Release versions (git tags only) |
   106→| **Latest Release** | `{service}:latest` | `api-service:latest` | Production release pointer |
   107→| **Environment** | `{service}:{environment}-latest` | `api-service:dev-latest` | CI/CD promotion flow (dev→qa→prod) |
   108→| **Branch** | `{service}:{branch-name}` | `api-service:main`, `api-service:feature-x` | Feature branch images for dev testing |
   109→
   110→### Tagging Logic
   111→- **Every commit** to main/PRs: Always tag with commit SHA
   112→- **On git tag** (semver like `v1.2.3`): Additionally tag with semver + `latest`
   113→- **Environment tags**: Updated by deployment pipelines, not by build pipeline
   114→  - Build pushes `api-service:sha-a1b2c3d`
   115→  - Deploy step (post-validation) tags as `api-service:dev-latest`
   116→  - After QA sign-off: Tag as `api-service:qa-latest`
   117→  - After prod deployment: Tag as `api-service:latest` and semver
   118→
   119→### Registry Path
   120→- **GCP Artifact Registry**: `us-central1-docker.pkg.dev/project-id/kix-services/{service}:{tag}`
   121→- **Namespace**: Separate Artifact Registry repos per environment if required (dev/qa/prod), or single repo with environment tags
   122→- **Rationale**: Single repository reduces complexity; environment tags in Helm values enable promotion
   123→
   124→### Image Immutability
   125→- **Rule**: Never overwrite image tags (SHA tags are immutable)
   126→- **Implementation**: Use `--tag-with-ref --tag-with-sha` in buildx, always tag SHA
   127→- **Rationale**: Prevents accidental image mutation, enables audit trail, safe rollback
   128→
   129→---
   130→
   131→## 4. Security Gates in Pipeline
   132→
   133→### Vulnerability Scanning
   134→
   135→#### Container Image Scanning (Trivy)
   136→- **When**: Runs immediately after build, before push to registry
   137→- **Scope**: Scan built image for known CVEs
   138→- **Severity levels**:
   139→  - CRITICAL/HIGH: Block push, fail workflow, require manual review
   140→  - MEDIUM: Log and alert, allow push (governance gate)
   141→  - LOW: Log only
   142→- **Tool**: Trivy in GitHub Actions (open-source, no external dependencies)
   143→- **Output**: JSON report stored as artifact for audit trail
   144→
   145→#### SBOM Generation (Syft)
   146→- **When**: After Trivy scan passes
   147→- **Output format**: SPDX JSON + CycloneDX (dual format for tool compatibility)
   148→- **Storage**: Attached to image as annotation in Artifact Registry
   149→- **Use case**: License compliance scanning, supply chain visibility, vulnerability tracking during image lifecycle
   150→
   151→#### Dependency Scanning
   152→- **Build time**: Existing unit test phase covers dependency audits
   153→  - `pip audit` for Python services
   154→  - `npm audit` for Node.js services
   155→- **Runtime**: ArgoCD integrates with Snyk (optional) for continuous monitoring post-deployment
   156→
   157→### Image Signing (Cosign)
   158→- **When**: After image push to registry succeeds
   159→- **Signing method**: GitHub OIDC token (no private key in Actions secrets)
   160→  - Cosign issues ephemeral certificate from Sigstore using OIDC
   161→  - Signature attached to image as OCI artifact
   162→- **Verification**:
   163→  - Dev deployments: Optional (fast feedback)
   164→  - Prod deployments: Mandatory (Kyverno policy enforces signed images)
   165→- **Key**: No long-lived keys; time-bound OIDC tokens issued per build
   166→
   167→### Policy Enforcement Points
   168→1. **Build phase**: Lint checks, unit tests (existing)
   169→2. **Scan phase**: CVE threshold, SBOM generation (new)
   170→3. **Sign phase**: Cosign signing with OIDC (new)
   171→4. **Registry**: Artifact Registry allowed-list (can enforce signed images)
   172→5. **Deployment phase**: Kyverno in Kubernetes cluster enforces signed images for prod (Helm values control strictness per environment)
   173→
   174→### Audit Trail
   175→- **Every image** linked to:
   176→  - Git commit (VCS_REF build arg)
   177→  - GitHub Actions run ID
   178→  - Build timestamp
   179→  - Scanner results (Trivy JSON)
   180→  - SBOM (Syft SPDX)
   181→  - Signature (Cosign metadata)
   182→- **Storage**: Artifact Registry image metadata + GitHub Actions logs
   183→- **Compliance**: Enables audit for security policies, incident response, change tracking
   184→
   185→---
   186→
   187→## 5. GitOps Flow: Code Change to Production
   188→
   189→### Flow Diagram
   190→```
   191→Developer Push
   192→    ↓
   193→GitHub PR Created
   194→    ↓
   195→Validation Workflows Run (lint, unit test, security scan)
   196→    ↓
   197→PR Merged to main
   198→    ↓
   199→Service Workflow Triggered (path-based filter)
   200→    ├─ Build image (BuildKit)
   201→    ├─ Scan (Trivy, SBOM, Cosign)
   202→    ├─ Push to Artifact Registry (tag: sha-{7-char})
   203→    └─ Trigger Helm values update
   204→    ↓
   205→Update Git Repository (services/{name}/values-{env}.yaml)
   206→    ├─ values-dev.yaml updated with image: sha-{7-char}
   207→    └─ Commit pushed to main
   208→    ↓
   209→ArgoCD Detects Change (watches main branch)
   210→    ├─ Dev: Auto-sync (immediate deployment)
   211→    ├─ QA: Manual sync (requires approval, respects change windows)
   212→    └─ Prod: Manual sync (requires approval, respects change windows)
   213→    ↓
   214→ArgoCD Deploys Helm Release
   215→    ├─ Renders manifest using updated values
   216→    ├─ Applies to kix-infrastructure cluster
   217→    ├─ Monitors rollout (Helm hooks for validation)
   218→    └─ Reports status back to Git (commit status)
   219→    ↓
   220→Monitoring & Validation
   221→    ├─ Health checks (service readiness probes)
   222→    ├─ Smoke tests (synthetic transactions)
   223→    ├─ Metrics validation (error rates, latency)
   224→    └─ Rollback trigger (automated if thresholds breached)
   225→```
   226→
   227→### Implementation Details
   228→
   229→**Step 1: Build & Scan**
   230→- GitHub Actions workflow builds image from `services/{name}/Dockerfile`
   231→- Scans for vulnerabilities, generates SBOM, signs with Cosign
   232→- Pushes to `us-central1-docker.pkg.dev/project-id/kix-services/{name}:sha-{7-char}`
   233→
   234→**Step 2: Update Helm Values**
   235→- Post-build step commits updated values to Git:
   236→  - File: `services/{name}/helm/values-dev.yaml`
   237→  - Change: `image.tag: "sha-{7-char}"` (only tag changes, digest pinning optional)
   238→  - Commit author: GitHub Actions bot
   239→  - Commit message: `[ci] Update {name} image to sha-{7-char}`
   240→- Commit pushed directly to main (no PR required for tag-only changes)
   241→
   242→**Step 3: ArgoCD Sync**
   243→- ArgoCD Application CRD references:
   244→  - Repo: `https://github.com/org/kix-infrastructure.git`
   245→  - Path: `helm/releases/{name}/`
   246→  - Values files: `values-{environment}.yaml` selected via kustomization or helm-values overlay
   247→- **Dev environment**: `syncPolicy.automated` enabled → auto-deploys on Git change
   248→- **QA/Prod environments**: `syncPolicy.automated` disabled → manual sync via ArgoCD UI or CLI
   249→- Sync respects change windows (enforced via Kubernetes CronJob or ArgoCD webhook)
   250→
   251→**Step 4: Progressive Rollout**
   252→- Helm deployment includes:
   253→  - Rolling strategy (RollingUpdate: maxSurge=1, maxUnavailable=0)
   254→  - Health checks (liveness + readiness probes)
   255→  - Optional: Canary via Flagger (if advanced progressive delivery needed)
   256→- ArgoCD waits for deployment to reach healthy state before marking sync successful
   257→
   258→### Rollback in GitOps
   259→- **Trigger**: Automated if error rate > threshold OR manual rollback command
   260→- **Method**: Revert commit in Git (values-{env}.yaml reverted to previous image tag)
   261→- **Timeline**: Rollback visible as Git commit; ArgoCD detects change and syncs old image within 2-3 minutes
   262→- **No kubectl apply required**: Full rollback via Git, improving audit trail and auditability
   263→
   264→---
   265→
   266→## 6. Environment Promotion Strategy
   267→
   268→### Three-Environment Model
   269→
   270→#### Development (Auto-Deployed)
   271→- **Trigger**: Every merge to main
   272→- **Deployment**: Automatic (ArgoCD auto-sync enabled)
   273→- **Change window**: No restrictions (24/7)
   274→- **Validation**: Basic health checks only
   275→- **Rollback**: Automatic on health probe failure
   276→- **Purpose**: Rapid feedback, developer testing, integration testing
   277→
   278→#### QA (Manual with Gates)
   279→- **Trigger**: Manual promotion from dev (explicit decision point)
   280→- **Deployment**: Manual sync via ArgoCD, respects change window (08:00-18:00 UTC)
   281→- **Prerequisites**:
   282→  - Dev deployment stable for ≥30 minutes (configurable)
   283→  - All smoke tests passing in dev
   284→  - Security scanning passed
   285→  - Optional: Manual approval step (Slack/GitHub issue)
   286→- **Validation**: Extended health checks, integration tests, performance benchmarks
   287→- **Rollback**: Manual (QA team initiates via ArgoCD)
   288→- **Purpose**: Regression testing, performance validation, UAT preparation
   289→
   290→#### Production (Strict Gates)
   291→- **Trigger**: Manual promotion from QA (deliberate release)
   292→- **Deployment**: Manual sync via ArgoCD, respects change window (Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)
   293→- **Prerequisites**:
   294→  - QA validation complete (documented sign-off)
   295→  - No production incidents open
   296→  - Change advisory board approval (if applicable)
   297→  - Optional: Canary deployment to 10% of traffic first
   298→- **Validation**: Full health checks, synthetic user tests, real user monitoring (RUM)
   299→- **Rollback**: Automated if error rate exceeds threshold OR manual
   300→- **Purpose**: Live user traffic, monetization, SLA-tracked
   301→
   302→### Promotion Workflow (GitOps)
   303→
   304→| Action | Method | Who | Output |
   305→|--------|--------|-----|--------|
   306→| Promote Dev→QA | Update `helm/releases/{name}/values-qa.yaml` image tag | DevOps/Release bot | Git commit, ArgoCD detects & syncs |
   307→| Promote QA→Prod | Update `helm/releases/{name}/values-prod.yaml` image tag | Release manager | Git commit, ArgoCD detects & syncs |
   308→| Rollback from Prod | Revert `values-prod.yaml` commit | On-call engineer | Git revert, ArgoCD auto-syncs old image |
   309→
   310→### Implementation
   311→- **Tools**: GitHub Actions for automated gates, ArgoCD UI/CLI for manual gates
   312→- **Change window enforcement**: GitHub Actions checks `${NOW_UTC}` before allowing QA/prod workflow
   313→- **Approval mechanism**: GitHub CODEOWNERS or required reviews on values-*.yaml for prod
   314→- **Notification**: Slack/email on promotion, rollback, and deployment completion
   315→
   316→---
   317→
   318→## 7. Rollback Strategy
   319→
   320→### Automated Rollback (Production Only)
   321→
   322→#### Triggers
   323→- **Error rate** > 5% for 3 minutes (compared to pre-deployment baseline)
   324→- **Latency (p95)** > 2x baseline for 5 minutes
   325→- **Pod crash loop**: CrashLoopBackOff for >2 consecutive restarts
   326→- **Health check failure**: >50% pods failing liveness probes
   327→- **Custom metric**: Business KPI thresholds (e.g., conversion rate drop)
   328→
   329→#### Rollback Process
   330→1. Monitoring detects threshold breach (Prometheus rules + Alertmanager)
   331→2. Trigger Lambda/webhook that reverts Git commit in `values-prod.yaml`
   332→3. ArgoCD detects Git change, syncs to previous image within 2-3 minutes
   333→4. Slack notification sent to #incident-channel with rollback details
   334→5. Manual validation: On-call confirms service health restored
   335→6. Post-mortem: Root cause analysis before next deployment attempt
   336→
   337→#### Design Rationale
   338→- **Git as source of truth**: Rollback = Git revert (visible, auditable, reversible)
   339→- **No kubectl required**: Reduces blast radius, operator doesn't need cluster access
   340→- **Fast detection**: <5 min to trigger rollback, <3 min to restore
   341→- **Prevention**: Canary deployments reduce blast radius before full rollout
   342→
   343→### Manual Rollback (Any Environment)
   344→
   345→#### Procedure
   346→1. On-call engineer identifies issue (e.g., bug in production code)
   347→2. Identifies previous stable image tag (from Git history or image metadata)
   348→3. Updates Git: `helm/releases/{name}/values-prod.yaml` with previous image tag
   349→4. Commits with message: `[rollback] {name} from sha-{new} to sha-{old} - reason`
   350→5. ArgoCD auto-syncs (or manual `argocd app sync` if manual mode)
   351→6. Service restores to previous version within 2-3 minutes
   352→
   353→#### Recovery Time Objective (RTO)
   354→- **Detection**: <3 minutes (alerts on error rate spike)
   355→- **Analysis**: <5 minutes (on-call triages logs)
   356→- **Rollback execution**: <1 minute (Git commit)
   357→- **Deployment**: <3 minutes (ArgoCD sync)
   358→- **Total**: <12 minutes (target: <5 min MTTR)
   359→
   360→### Data Considerations
   361→- **Stateless services**: Rollback immediate, no data conflicts
   362→- **Database schema changes**: Use blue-green or expand/contract pattern (handled separately from code rollback)
   363→- **Irreversible operations**: Document as non-reversible; require additional approval gates
   364→
   365→### Rollback Validation
   366→- Helm hooks (pre/post-rollback tests) ensure rolled-back version still functions
   367→- Smoke tests validate service endpoints respond correctly
   368→- Metrics confirm error rate returned to baseline
   369→
   370→---
   371→
   372→## 8. Monorepo Considerations
   373→
   374→### Monorepo Structure
   375→```
   376→kix-services/ (root)
   377→├── services/
   378→│   ├── api-service/
   379→│   │   ├── Dockerfile
   380→│   │   ├── src/
   381→│   │   └── helm/
   382→│   │       ├── Chart.yaml
   383→│   │       ├── values-dev.yaml
   384→│   │       ├── values-qa.yaml
   385→│   │       └── values-prod.yaml
   386→│   ├── auth-service/
   387→│   │   ├── Dockerfile
   388→│   │   ├── src/
   389→│   │   └── helm/
   390→│   ├── ... (40 more services)
   391→│   └── shared/  # Shared libraries, build utilities
   392→├── .github/
   393→│   └── workflows/
   394→│       ├── build-image.yaml (reusable)
   395→│       ├── scan-image.yaml (reusable)
   396→│       ├── service-ci.yaml (triggers on services/* changes)
   397→│       └── shared-ci.yaml (triggers on shared/* changes)
   398→└── kix-infrastructure/ (separate repo, Helm releases + values)
   399→```
   400→
   401→### Change Detection Strategy
   402→
   403→**Monorepo Challenge**: 42 services in one repo; must rebuild only modified services, not all 42 on every commit.
   404→
   405→**Solution: Path-Based Triggers in GitHub Actions**
   406→```yaml
   407→on:
   408→  push:
   409→    branches: [main]
   410→    paths:
   411→      - 'services/api-service/**'
   412→      - '.github/workflows/build-image.yaml'
   413→      - 'shared/**'
   414→  workflow_dispatch:
   415→    inputs:
   416→      service: {description: "Service name (leave empty for changed services)"}
   417→```
   418→
   419→- **Trigger logic**: Workflow runs only if files in `services/api-service/` or `shared/` changed
   420→- **Shared changes**: If `shared/` changes, rebuild all services (detected via matrix strategy)
   421→- **Manual override**: `workflow_dispatch` allows rebuilding specific service or all services
   422→
   423→### Parallel Builds
   424→
   425→**Implementation**:
   426→1. Workflow script detects changed service paths (compare main to HEAD)
   427→2. Creates dynamic matrix from changed services:
   428→   ```
   429→   matrix:
   430→     service: [api-service, auth-service, gateway-service]
   431→   ```
   432→3. GitHub Actions launches 3 parallel jobs, each building one service
   433→4. Jobs run simultaneously (limited by GitHub Actions runner quota)
   434→5. Artifacts (images) pushed concurrently to Artifact Registry
   435→
   436→**Time improvement**:
   437→- Sequential: 42 services × 8 min avg = 336 minutes
   438→- Parallel (12 runners): 8 min per batch = 40 minutes
   439→- With cache: 42 services × 3 min avg = 126 minutes cached
   440→
   441→### Selective Deployment
   442→
   443→**Problem**: If shared library updated, all 42 services need rebuilding but only specific services need redeploying (e.g., only services using updated library).
   444→
   445→**Solution: Impact Analysis**
   446→1. Build step detects which services changed (path-based)
   447→2. Deploy step reads a `IMPACT_MAP.json` or Git tags indicating service dependencies:
   448→   ```json
   449→   {
   450→     "shared/auth-lib": ["auth-service", "api-service", "gateway-service"],
   451→     "shared/logging": ["all"]
   452→   }
   453→   ```
   454→3. Deploy step filters affected services before triggering Helm values updates
   455→4. Only affected services' values-*.yaml files updated, reducing noise in kix-infrastructure repo
   456→
   457→### Monorepo-Specific Risks
   458→- **Merge conflicts**: Multiple services updated simultaneously in main → potential conflicts in values files
   459→  - Mitigation: Use separate PRs per service or batch updates
   460→- **Blast radius**: Single bad commit affects multiple services
   461→  - Mitigation: Strict PR review, automated tests on all services, granular rollbacks
   462→- **Build time creep**: As services grow, builds slow down
   463→  - Mitigation: Split into smaller monorepos or multi-repo as necessary
   464→
   465→---
   466→
   467→## 9. Secrets Management: GitHub OIDC with GCP
   468→
   469→### Problem Statement
   470→- Current state: Cloud Build uses service account JSON keys (long-lived, high-risk)
   471→- Target: No long-lived credentials in GitHub Actions; use OIDC for dynamic auth
   472→
   473→### GitHub OIDC Integration
   474→
   475→#### Prerequisites
   476→- GitHub enterprise account with OIDC provider enabled
   477→- GCP Workload Identity Pool configured with GitHub as external identity provider
   478→- Workload Identity Provider bound to GitHub repo + branch/tag conditions
   479→
   480→#### Setup (One-Time)
   481→1. Create GCP Workload Identity Pool: `github-pool`
   482→2. Add GitHub OIDC provider: `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`
   483→3. Create Workload Identity binding:
   484→   - **Subject**: `repo:org/repo:ref:refs/heads/main` (only main branch)
   485→   - **Service Account**: `github-actions@project-id.iam.gserviceaccount.com`
   486→4. Grant IAM roles to service account:
   487→   - `roles/artifactregistry.writer` (push images)
   488→   - `roles/storage.objectViewer` (read build cache, logs)
   489→   - `roles/container.developer` (if deploying to GKE, though ArgoCD handles this)
   490→
   491→#### Runtime Workflow
   492→1. GitHub Actions job requests OIDC token from GitHub token endpoint
   493→2. GitHub returns signed JWT containing:
   494→   - Subject: `repo:org/repo:ref:refs/heads/main`
   495→   - Issued at: current timestamp
   496→   - Expiration: 1 hour (automatic refresh)
   497→3. Actions job exchanges JWT for GCP access token via Workload Identity
   498→4. Access token (~1 hour lifetime) used to authenticate to Artifact Registry
   499→5. On token expiry, automatic refresh happens transparently
   500→
   501→#### GitHub Actions Implementation
   502→```yaml
   503→- name: Authenticate to GCP
   504→  uses: google-github-actions/auth@v2
   505→  with:
   506→    workload_identity_provider: projects/PROJECT_ID/locations/global/workloadIdentityPools/github-pool/providers/github-provider
   507→    service_account: github-actions@PROJECT_ID.iam.gserviceaccount.com
   508→    token_format: 'access_token'
   509→
   510→- name: Set up Docker
   511→  uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v3
   512→
   513→- name: Authenticate to Artifact Registry
   514→  run: |
   515→    gcloud auth application-default print-access-token | docker login -u oauth2accesstoken --password-stdin us-central1-docker.pkg.dev
   516→```
   517→
   518→### Secrets Management in Workflows
   519→
   520→#### Avoid Storing in GitHub Secrets
   521→- ~~NPM_TOKEN, PIP_TOKEN, etc.~~ ← Anti-pattern
   522→- Instead: Fetch dynamically using OIDC
   523→
   524→#### Dynamic Secret Fetching
   525→1. GitHub Actions authenticates to GCP via OIDC (see above)
   526→2. Fetch NPM_TOKEN from Google Secret Manager:
   527→   ```bash
   528→   gcloud secrets versions access latest --secret="npm-token" > /tmp/npm_token
   529→   ```
   530→3. Pass as build arg or env var:
   531→   ```bash
   532→   docker build --build-context npm_token=/tmp/npm_token ...
   533→   docker buildx build --secret npm_token=/tmp/npm_token ...
   534→   ```
   535→4. In Dockerfile: `RUN --mount=type=secret,id=npm_token npm install` (secret never in layer)
   536→
   537→#### Alternative: Federated Credentials for Artifact Registry
   538→- Use `docker-buildx-bake` with native GCP auth (no manual login required)
   539→- Buildx automatically authenticates via Application Default Credentials
   540→
   541→### No Long-Lived Credentials
   542→- **Service Account JSON key**: Not stored in GitHub secrets or repo
   543→- **OIDC token**: Issued per-job, ~1 hour lifetime, automatically refreshed
   544→- **Access token**: Issued per job, ~1 hour lifetime
   545→- **Cosign signing**: Uses OIDC to issue ephemeral certificate (see Section 4)
   546→
   547→### Audit Trail
   548→- Every action tied to GitHub OIDC token (repo, branch, commit, timestamp)
   549→- GCP Cloud Audit Logs capture all Artifact Registry uploads
   550→- No service account key access logs (keys not used)
   551→
   552→---
   553→
   554→## 10. Summary: Key Architectural Decisions
   555→
   556→| Decision | Rationale | Implementation |
   557→|----------|-----------|-----------------|
   558→| **Reusable workflows** | DRY principle, reduce maintenance, easy scaling to 42 services | Central workflows/ dir, callable from service workflows |
   559→| **Path-based triggers** | Only rebuild modified services, faster feedback | GitHub Actions `paths` filter + dynamic matrix |
   560→| **BuildKit + layer caching** | Fast builds with cache, multi-platform support | `docker/buildx-action`, `type=gha` cache |
   561→| **Commit SHA tagging** | Unique image ID per build, maps code→image→deployment | Primary tag format, semver secondary |
   562→| **Trivy + SBOM + Cosign** | Supply chain security, vulnerability tracking, signing | Pre-push scanning, OIDC-based signing |
   563→| **ArgoCD auto-sync (dev only)** | Fast feedback for developers | Manual sync for QA/prod with change windows |
   564→| **Git-based rollback** | Auditability, reversibility, no kubectl required | Revert values-*.yaml commit, ArgoCD syncs |
   565→| **GitHub OIDC** | No long-lived credentials, automatic refresh | Workload Identity Pool + service account binding |
   566→| **Single repo (kix-infrastructure)** | Centralized Helm releases, easier to audit | One repo with environment-specific values overlays |
   567→| **Selective promotion** | Prevent over-deployment, reduce change blast radius | Manual sync + change window gates |
   568→
   569→---
   570→
   571→## 11. Pipeline Metrics & Observability
   572→
   573→### Key Metrics
   574→- **Deployment frequency**: Target >10 per day (aggregate across services)
   575→- **Lead time**: <1 hour from commit to production deployment
   576→- **MTTR**: <30 minutes (goal: <5 min via automated rollback)
   577→- **Change failure rate**: <5%
   578→- **Build time**: <5 min (cache hit), <12 min (cold build)
   579→
   580→### Instrumentation
   581→- **GitHub Actions**: Dashboard tracking workflow run times, failure rates, queue time
   582→- **Artifact Registry**: Image push times, scan results, signature verification
   583→- **ArgoCD**: Sync times, deployment success rate, rollback frequency
   584→- **Prometheus**: Application-level metrics (error rate, latency) to trigger rollback
   585→- **Datadog/Grafana**: Unified visibility across build, deploy, runtime
   586→
   587→### Alerting
   588→- Workflow failures → Slack #devops-alerts
   589→- CVE detection in scan → GitHub security tab + email
   590→- Deployment failure → #incident-channel
   591→- Rollback triggered → #incident-channel + PagerDuty
   592→- Service degradation → #incident-channel + page on-call
   593→
   594→---
   595→
   596→## 12. Migration Roadmap
   597→
   598→### Phase 1: Foundation (Weeks 1-2)
   599→- Set up Workload Identity Pool (GitHub OIDC)
   600→- Create reusable workflows (build-image, scan-image, push-registry)
   601→- Migrate Terraform Cloud Build to GitHub Actions for 3 pilot services
   602→- Configure Artifact Registry with tagging strategy
   603→
   604→### Phase 2: Expansion (Weeks 3-4)
   605→- Migrate 42 services to new CI/CD pipeline (batch by team)
   606→- Set up ArgoCD applications for dev environment (auto-sync)
   607→- Enable path-based triggers, validate selective deployment
   608→- Integrate Trivy + Cosign into build pipeline
   609→
   610→### Phase 3: Production Ready (Weeks 5-6)
   611→- Configure ArgoCD manual sync for QA/prod with change windows
   612→- Implement automated rollback (error rate threshold + Git revert)
   613→- Set up monitoring dashboards, alerting rules
   614→- Runbook documentation, team training
   615→
   616→### Phase 4: Optimization (Weeks 7+)
   617→- Measure metrics, identify bottlenecks
   618→- Implement canary deployments (Flagger) if needed
   619→- Cost optimization (Artifact Registry cleanup, Wiz scans)
   620→- Continuous improvement feedback loop
   621→
   622→---
   623→
   624→## Appendix: Tools & Versions
   625→
   626→| Tool | Version | Purpose |
   627→|------|---------|---------|
   628→| GitHub Actions | Native | CI orchestration |
   629→| Docker Buildx | 0.13+ | Multi-platform builds, caching |
   630→| Trivy | 0.48+ | Container scanning |
   631→| Cosign | 2.2+ | Image signing |
   632→| Syft | 0.80+ | SBOM generation |
   633→| Helm | 3.13+ | Kubernetes package management |
   634→| ArgoCD | 2.8+ | GitOps continuous delivery |
   635→| GCP Workload Identity | Native | OIDC federation |
   636→| Artifact Registry | Native | Container registry |
   637→
   638→---

   641→- Helm Chart Architecture (separate section)
   642→- ArgoCD Application Design (separate section)
   643→- Kubernetes Deployment Strategy (separate section)
   644→- Security Scanning & Compliance (separate section)
   645→- Disaster Recovery & Backup (separate section)

  "content": "# Architecture Blueprint: Migration to KIX-Infrastructure Platform\n\n## Konecta TIC — Cloud Run to Kubernetes (Helm + ArgoCD + GitHub Actions)\n\n---\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n1. [Executive Summary](#1-executive-summary)\n2. [Current State](#2-current-state)\n3. [Target Architecture](#3-target-architecture)\n4. [Service Inventory & Tiering](#4-service-inventory--tiering)\n5. [Migration Strategy](#5-migration-strategy)\n6. [Kubernetes & Helm Chart Design](#6-kubernetes--helm-chart-design)\n7. [Secure Docker Builds](#7-secure-docker-builds)\n8. [CI/CD Pipeline (GitHub Actions + ArgoCD)](#8-cicd-pipeline-github-actions--argocd)\n9. [Security Architecture](#9-security-architecture)\n10. [Observability](#10-observability)\n11. [Cost & Operational Considerations](#11-cost--operational-considerations)\n12. [Risk Register](#12-risk-register)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Executive Summary\n\nThis blueprint defines the architecture for migrating ~42 microservices from GCP Cloud Run to the **kix-infrastructure** Kubernetes platform. The migration adopts:\n\n- **Helm charts** for declarative Kubernetes packaging\n- **Secure multi-stage Docker builds** with vulnerability scanning and image signing\n- **GitHub Actions** for CI (build, scan, push)\n- **ArgoCD** for CD (GitOps-driven deployment across dev/qa/prod)\n\n**Key principles**: Simple, secure, auditable, repeatable.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Current State\n\n### Infrastructure\n- **Compute**: GCP Cloud Run (most services), GKE Autopilot (Kong, LiteLLM, ArgoCD), Cloud Functions (1 bot)\n- **Clusters**: GKE Autopilot in eu-west1/eu-west4, private with Cloud NAT\n- **CI/CD**: Cloud Build (8 services), GitHub Actions (Terraform only)\n- **Registry**: GCP Artifact Registry\n- **IaC**: Terraform with modular structure\n\n### Services (42 total)\n| Category | Count | Stack |\n|----------|-------|-------|\n| MCP Servers | 14 | Python (FastMCP) |\n| CrewAI Flows & Tools | 12 | Python (CrewAI) |\n| Business Apps | 4 | Python (FastAPI) + Node.js (Nuxt 3) |\n| Data Pipelines | 3 | Python |\n| Infrastructure Repos | 4 | Terraform/HCL |\n| Config/Proxies | 2 | Python (LiteLLM) |\n| Bots | 1 | Python (Cloud Functions) |\n| Prototypes | 2 | Python |\n\n### External Dependencies (remain as managed services)\n- **Cloud SQL** (PostgreSQL 16+)\n- **Firestore** (rate limiting, conversations)\n- **Vertex AI** (Gemini, Claude via Vertex, RAG Engine)\n- **Cloud Storage** (GCS)\n- **Pub/Sub** (async messaging)\n- **Google APIs** (Search, Maps, Drive, Chat)\n\n---\n\n## 3. Target Architecture\n\n### High-Level Component Diagram\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                           INTERNET                                      │\n└─────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                      │ HTTPS\n┌─────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    GCP Load Balancer + Cloud Armor (WAF)                 │\n└─────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                      │\n┌─────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│              KIX-INFRASTRUCTURE GKE CLUSTER (eu-west1)                  │\n│                                                                         │\n│  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │\n│  │  shared-kong     │  │  shared-monitoring                          │  │\n│  │  (Kong Gateway)  │  │  Prometheus / Grafana / Jaeger / OTel       │  │\n│  └────────┬────────┘  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │\n│           │                                                             │\n│  ┌────────▼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │\n│  │  Application Namespaces (per environment × domain)              │    │\n│  │                                                                 │    │\n│  │  {env}-api-services     → FastAPI backends, business apps       │    │\n│  │  {env}-mcp-servers      → MCP protocol servers (SSE)            │    │\n│  │  {env}-ai-workflows     → CrewAI flows & tools                  │    │\n│  │  {env}-web-apps         → Nuxt 3 frontends                      │    │\n│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │\n│                                                                         │\n│  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐                 │\n│  │ shared-litellm│  │ shared-argocd│  │ shared-secrets│                 │\n│  │ (LLM Proxy)  │  │ (GitOps CD)  │  │ (ESO + CSI)  │                 │\n│  └──────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └───────────────┘                 │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n         │                    │                    │\n         ▼                    ▼                    ▼\n┌─────────────┐  ┌───────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐\n│  Cloud SQL  │  │  Vertex AI / GCS  │  │  GCP Secret Manager │\n│ (PostgreSQL)│  │  Pub/Sub / APIs   │  │                     │\n└─────────────┘  └───────────────────┘  └─────────────────────┘\n```\n\n### CI/CD Flow\n\n```\nDeveloper Push → GitHub PR → GitHub Actions CI\n                                ├── Lint + Unit Tests\n                                ├── Docker Build (BuildKit, multi-stage)\n                                ├── Trivy Scan + SBOM (Syft)\n                                ├── Push to Artifact Registry\n                                └── Cosign Sign (OIDC)\n                                         │\n                              Update Helm values-{env}.yaml in Git\n                                         │\n                              ArgoCD Detects Git Change\n                                ├── Dev: Auto-sync\n                                ├── QA: Manual sync (08:00-18:00 UTC)\n                                └── Prod: Manual sync (Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Service Inventory & Tiering\n\n### Tier 1 — Critical (Zero-Downtime Required)\n| Service | Type | Why Critical |\n|---------|------|-------------|\n| kon-mcp-gateway | MCP Gateway | Central routing hub for all MCP communication |\n| kon-iq-portal | Nuxt + API | User-facing business application |\n| kon-app-hrfraud | FastAPI + Nuxt | Business-critical fraud detection |\n| kon-litellm | LLM Proxy | All AI services depend on it |\n| Kong Gateway | API Gateway | External traffic entry point |\n\n### Tier 2 — Important (Brief Downtime Tolerable)\n| Service | Type |\n|---------|------|\n| kon-mcp-ragengine | RAG retrieval |\n| kon-mcp-channelhub | Email/messaging |\n| kon-mcp-google-search | Search integration |\n| kon-flow-competitor-intelligence | CrewAI workflow |\n| kon-flow-marketintelligence | CrewAI workflow |\n| kon-mcp-docingestion | Document processing |\n| kon-rag-ingestion | Data pipeline |\n\n### Tier 3 — Standard (Downtime Acceptable During Migration)\nAll remaining MCP servers, CrewAI tools, prototypes, and bots.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Migration Strategy\n\n### Phased Approach\n\n**Phase 1 — Foundation & Pilot (3 services)**\n- Set up Helm base chart library\n- Configure ArgoCD ApplicationSets\n- Set up GitHub Actions reusable workflows (build, scan, push)\n- Configure GitHub OIDC → GCP Workload Identity Pool\n- Migrate 3 Tier-3 services as pilots (e.g., kon-mcp-diagram, kon-mcp-markdown, kon-mcp-pptx)\n- Validate full CI/CD pipeline end-to-end\n- Establish monitoring baseline\n\n**Phase 2 — MCP Servers & Tools (15 services)**\n- Migrate remaining MCP servers (internal, low traffic)\n- Migrate CrewAI tool wrappers\n- Validate MCP gateway routing to K8s-hosted backends\n- Run dual-stack (Cloud Run + K8s) during transition\n\n**Phase 3 — AI Workflows & Data Pipelines (10 services)**\n- Migrate CrewAI flows (higher resource requirements)\n- Migrate RAG ingestion pipelines\n- Tune resource profiles for AI workloads\n- Migrate Cloud Functions bot to K8s CronJob/Deployment\n\n**Phase 4 — Business Applications & Cutover (remaining services)**\n- Migrate Tier 1 services (kon-iq-portal, kon-app-hrfraud, kon-mcp-gateway)\n- Blue-green DNS cutover per service\n- Decommission Cloud Run services after 2-week soak period\n- Final Cloud Run teardown\n\n### DNS & Traffic Cutover Strategy\n- Each service gets a K8s Service + Ingress via Kong\n- During migration: Cloud Run and K8s versions run side-by-side\n- DNS switch per service (weighted routing or instant CNAME swap)\n- Rollback: Revert DNS to Cloud Run if K8s version fails\n- Post-migration: Remove Cloud Run service after validation soak\n\n### Stateless vs Stateful Handling\n- **All application services are stateless** — state lives in Cloud SQL, Firestore, GCS\n- Container images contain no data; configuration via ConfigMaps/Secrets\n- No persistent volumes needed for application pods\n- Temporary storage (`emptyDir`) for: `/tmp`, ML model caches, video processing scratch space\n\n---\n\n## 6. Kubernetes & Helm Chart Design\n\n### 6.1 Helm Chart Strategy\n\n**Hybrid: Base Chart Library + Per-Service Charts**\n\n```\ncharts/\n├── base-service-chart/          # Reusable library chart (not deployed directly)\n│   ├── Chart.yaml               # type: library\n│   └── templates/\n│       ├── _deployment.tpl\n│       ├── _service.tpl\n│       ├── _ingress.tpl\n│       ├── _hpa.tpl\n│       ├── _pdb.tpl\n│       ├── _networkpolicy.tpl\n│       ├── _serviceaccount.tpl\n│       └── _external-secret.tpl\n│\n├── kon-mcp-gateway/             # Per-service chart (lightweight wrapper)\n│   ├── Chart.yaml               # depends on base-service-chart\n│   ├── values.yaml              # Service defaults\n│   ├── values-dev.yaml          # Dev overrides\n│   ├── values-qa.yaml           # QA overrides\n│   └── values-prod.yaml         # Prod overrides\n│\n├── kon-iq-portal/\n│   ├── Chart.yaml\n│   ├── values.yaml\n│   └── values-{env}.yaml\n│\n└── ... (one per service)\n```\n\n**Rationale**: 42 services require standardization (base chart), but domain diversity demands flexibility (per-service values). Avoids monolithic umbrella chart while preventing template sprawl.\n\n### 6.2 Namespace Strategy\n\n**Environment × Domain Boundaries (17 namespaces total)**\n\n| Namespace Pattern | Contents |\n|-------------------|----------|\n| `{env}-api-services` | FastAPI backends, business apps |\n| `{env}-mcp-servers` | MCP protocol servers |\n| `{env}-ai-workflows` | CrewAI flows and tools |\n| `{env}-web-apps` | Nuxt 3 frontends |\n| `shared-kong` | Kong API Gateway |\n| `shared-litellm` | LiteLLM proxy |\n| `shared-argocd` | ArgoCD (existing) |\n| `shared-monitoring` | Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger |\n| `shared-cert-manager` | TLS certificate management |\n\nEach namespace gets: ResourceQuotas, LimitRanges, default NetworkPolicies (deny-all ingress), RBAC bindings.\n\n### 6.3 Resource Profiles\n\n| Profile | CPU Request | CPU Limit | Mem Request | Mem Limit | Use For |\n|---------|------------|-----------|-------------|-----------|---------|\n| **AI Workload** | 500m | 2000m | 1Gi | 2Gi | CrewAI flows, RAG services |\n| **Web Service** | 100m | 500m | 256Mi | 512Mi | FastAPI APIs, MCP servers |\n| **Frontend** | 50m | 200m | 128Mi | 256Mi | Nuxt 3 SSR |\n\n### 6.4 Health Probes\n\n| Service Type | Liveness | Readiness | Startup |\n|-------------|----------|-----------|---------|\n| FastAPI | `GET /healthz` 8000 | `GET /readyz` 8000 (checks DB) | N/A |\n| MCP Server (SSE) | `GET /healthz` 8000, period=30s | `GET /readyz` 8000, initial=30s | N/A |\n| Nuxt 3 | `GET /` 3000 | `GET /_health` 3000 | N/A |\n| AI/CrewAI | `GET /healthz` 8000 | `GET /readyz` 8000 | failureThreshold=30 (5min) |\n\n### 6.5 Autoscaling (HPA)\n\n| Tier | Min | Max | Target CPU | Scale-Down Window |\n|------|-----|-----|------------|-------------------|\n| User-facing (frontends, public APIs) | 2 | 20 | 60% | 5 min |\n| Internal APIs, MCP servers | 2 | 10 | 75% | 10 min |\n| AI workflows | 1 | 5 | 80% | 15 min |\n\nCustom metrics via Prometheus Adapter: request rate, Pub/Sub queue depth, active SSE connections.\n\n### 6.6 Service Mesh\n\n**Decision: No (initially).** Kong handles ingress traffic management, auth, and rate limiting. Introducing Istio/Linkerd during a 42-service migration adds unnecessary risk. Revisit post-migration if mTLS between services or advanced traffic management is required.\n\n### 6.7 Ingress Architecture\n\n```\nInternet → GCP LB (TLS termination) → Kong Ingress Controller → Application Services\n```\n\n- **External services**: Kong Ingress with `konghq.com/plugins` annotations (rate-limiting, CORS, auth)\n- **Internal services**: ClusterIP only, accessed via `service.namespace.svc.cluster.local`\n- **TLS**: cert-manager + Let's Encrypt (ClusterIssuer) for automated certificate management\n- **DNS per environment**: `dev.api.konecta.io`, `qa.api.konecta.io`, `api.konecta.io`\n\n### 6.8 ArgoCD ApplicationSet Strategy\n\n**Matrix Generator: Environment × Service**\n\n- Single ApplicationSet discovers all `charts/*/` directories and creates Applications across 3 environments\n- Each Application references `values.yaml` + `values-{env}.yaml`\n- **Dev**: `syncPolicy.automated` (auto-sync on commit)\n- **QA**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, business-hours window)\n- **Prod**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)\n- ArgoCD Projects for RBAC: `dev-project`, `qa-project`, `prod-project`\n- Sync waves: infrastructure resources (wave 0) → application deployments (wave 10)\n\n---\n\n## 7. Secure Docker Builds\n\n### 7.1 Base Image Policy\n\n| Stack | Builder Stage | Runtime Stage |\n|-------|--------------|---------------|\n| Python (standard) | `python:3.x-slim` | `python:3.x-slim` |\n| Python (ML: opencv, whisper, ffmpeg) | `python:3.x-slim` + apt deps | `python:3.x-slim` + runtime-only libs |\n| Node.js | `node:20-alpine` | `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12` |\n\n- Pin to **full digest** in CI (updated monthly by Renovate/Dependabot)\n- Parameterize Python version via `ARG PYTHON_VERSION=3.11`\n- **Never use `:latest`**\n\n### 7.2 Multi-Stage Build Patterns\n\n**Python services:**\n```\nStage 1 \"builder\": python:3.x-slim → install build deps → pip install --prefix=/install\nStage 2 \"runtime\": python:3.x-slim → COPY --from=builder /install → non-root USER → ENTRYPOINT\n```\n\n**Node.js services:**\n```\nStage 1 \"deps\": node:20-alpine → npm ci\nStage 2 \"builder\": COPY source → npm run build → npm prune --omit=dev\nStage 3 \"runtime\": distroless/nodejs20 → COPY .output → no shell → ENTRYPOINT\n```\n\n### 7.3 Security Hardening\n\n- **Non-root user** (UID 65532) in all images, enforced by `runAsNonRoot: true` in pod spec\n- **Read-only root filesystem** — writable paths via `emptyDir` mounts (`/tmp`, `/app/.cache`)\n- **No shell** in Node.js production images (distroless)\n- **Drop ALL capabilities** in pod security context\n- **No `.env` files** baked into images; all config via K8s secrets/configmaps\n- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` for Python images\n\n### 7.4 Dependency Management\n\n- Python: `pip-compile` or `uv lock` for pinned requirements with hashes\n- Node.js: `npm ci` only (never `npm install` in CI)\n- Weekly Renovate/Dependabot PRs for dependency updates\n\n### 7.5 Build Metadata (OCI Labels)\n\nAll images include: `org.opencontainers.image.created`, `.revision` (git SHA), `.version`, `.source`, `.title`, `.vendor` — final stage only.\n\n### 7.6 Image Size Targets\n\n| Category | Target | Optimization |\n|----------|--------|-------------|\n| Python standard | < 200 MB | Multi-stage, no cache, strip pyc |\n| Python ML/heavy | < 500 MB | opencv-headless, models as external volumes |\n| Node.js (Nuxt) | < 80 MB | Distroless runtime, npm prune |\n\n---\n\n## 8. CI/CD Pipeline (GitHub Actions + ArgoCD)\n\n### 8.1 Workflow Strategy\n\n**Monorepo with path-based triggers + reusable workflows**\n\n```\n.github/workflows/\n├── _build-image.yaml        # Reusable: Docker build + cache\n├── _scan-image.yaml         # Reusable: Trivy + SBOM + Cosign\n├── _push-registry.yaml      # Reusable: Push to Artifact Registry\n├── _deploy-helm.yaml        # Reusable: Update Helm values in Git\n├── ci-service.yaml          # Triggered per-service on path change\n└── ci-shared.yaml           # Triggered on shared lib changes (rebuilds dependents)\n```\n\n- Path filters ensure only modified services rebuild\n- Dynamic matrix strategy for parallel builds across services\n- Shared library changes trigger dependent services via `IMPACT_MAP.json`\n\n### 8.2 Image Tagging Strategy\n\n| Tag | When | Purpose |\n|-----|------|---------|\n| `sha-{7-char}` | Every build | Immutable build identifier |\n| `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | On git tag | Release version |\n| `{env}-latest` | On deploy | Environment pointer |\n\n**Rule**: SHA tags are immutable. Never overwrite.\n\n### 8.3 Security Gates\n\n```\nBuild → Trivy Scan → SBOM Generation (Syft) → Push → Cosign Sign (OIDC)\n         ↓ block                                        ↓\n   CRITICAL/HIGH CVE                           Kyverno admission policy\n   fails the build                             enforces signed images (prod)\n```\n\n- **Trivy**: CRITICAL/HIGH block push; MEDIUM logged; LOW informational\n- **SBOM**: SPDX + CycloneDX dual format, attached as OCI annotation\n- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore (no long-lived keys)\n- **Kyverno**: Cluster admission controller verifies image signatures before deploy (prod only)\n\n### 8.4 GitOps Flow\n\n1. PR merged → GitHub Actions builds and pushes image (`sha-abc1234`)\n2. Post-build step updates `charts/{service}/values-dev.yaml` with new image tag\n3. Commits to main: `[ci] Update {service} image to sha-abc1234`\n4. ArgoCD detects Git change → syncs to cluster\n5. Dev auto-syncs immediately; QA/Prod require manual sync\n\n### 8.5 Environment Promotion\n\n| Environment | Trigger | Sync Mode | Change Window |\n|-------------|---------|-----------|---------------|\n| Dev | Every merge to main | Auto-sync | 24/7 |\n| QA | Manual promotion | Manual sync | 08:00-18:00 UTC |\n| Prod | Manual promotion + approval | Manual sync | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |\n\n**Promotion**: Update `values-{target-env}.yaml` with the image tag from the previous environment.\n\n### 8.6 Rollback Strategy\n\n- **Automated** (prod): Prometheus alert on error rate >5% for 3 min → webhook reverts Git commit → ArgoCD syncs previous image\n- **Manual**: Engineer reverts `values-prod.yaml` to previous SHA tag, commits, ArgoCD syncs\n- **RTO target**: <5 min (detection + revert + sync)\n- **Git as source of truth**: Every rollback is a visible Git commit\n\n### 8.7 Secrets Management (CI)\n\n- **GitHub OIDC** → GCP Workload Identity Pool (no JSON keys)\n- Dynamic secret fetching from GCP Secret Manager at build time\n- Build secrets via `docker build --secret` (never in image layers)\n- Token lifetime ~1 hour, auto-refreshing, full audit trail\n\n---\n\n## 9. Security Architecture\n\n### 9.1 Container Image Security\n\n- **Multi-stage builds**: Builder dependencies never ship in runtime image\n- **Base image policy**: Pinned digests, updated monthly, slim/distroless only\n- **Vulnerability scanning**: Trivy pre-push, CRITICAL/HIGH block deployment\n- **Image signing**: Cosign keyless (OIDC) — every image cryptographically signed\n- **SBOM**: Generated for every build, stored as OCI annotation\n- **Registry**: Artifact Registry with vulnerability scanning enabled, IAM-restricted access\n\n### 9.2 Supply Chain Security\n\n- **Dependency pinning**: Hash-verified `requirements.txt` (Python), `package-lock.json` (Node.js)\n- **Dependency scanning**: `pip audit` / `npm audit` in CI, Renovate for updates\n- **GitHub Actions hardening**:\n  - Pin action versions by SHA (not tag)\n  - Minimal `GITHUB_TOKEN` permissions per workflow\n  - No third-party actions without security review\n  - OIDC for all GCP interactions (no stored credentials)\n\n### 9.3 Runtime Security\n\n- **Pod Security Standards**: `restricted` profile enforced via namespace labels\n  - `runAsNonRoot: true`\n  - `readOnlyRootFilesystem: true`\n  - `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`\n  - `capabilities.drop: [ALL]`\n  - `seccompProfile.type: RuntimeDefault`\n- **No privileged containers**. No `hostNetwork`, `hostPID`, `hostIPC`.\n- Writable paths only via explicit `emptyDir` volume mounts\n\n### 9.4 Secrets Management (Runtime)\n\n**External Secrets Operator (ESO) + GCP Secret Manager**\n\n- ESO installed in `shared-secrets` namespace\n- `SecretStore` per environment, authenticated via Workload Identity\n- `ExternalSecret` CRDs in each service Helm chart pull secrets from GCP\n- Secrets never stored in Git or Helm values files\n- Automatic rotation support via ESO refresh interval\n\n**Workload Identity for GCP API access**:\n```yaml\nserviceAccount:\n  annotations:\n    iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: {service}@{project}.iam.gserviceaccount.com\n```\nNo service account JSON keys. Ever.\n\n### 9.5 Network Security\n\n- **Default deny ingress** on all application namespaces\n- **Explicit NetworkPolicies** per service: allow only expected sources\n- **GCP health check CIDRs** allowed (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)\n- **Cloud Armor WAF** on external load balancer (OWASP top-10 rules)\n- **Private cluster**: No public node IPs, egress via Cloud NAT\n- **DNS policies**: Restrict external DNS resolution where possible\n\n### 9.6 RBAC & Namespace Isolation\n\n- **Service accounts per namespace** with least-privilege Roles\n- **ArgoCD Projects** restrict which namespaces each team can deploy to\n- **Namespace ResourceQuotas** prevent resource exhaustion attacks\n- **Kyverno policies**:\n  - Enforce signed images (prod)\n  - Block `latest` tag\n  - Require resource limits\n  - Enforce label standards\n\n### 9.7 Admission Control\n\n- **Kyverno** (preferred over OPA/Gatekeeper for readability):\n  - Verify Cosign image signatures\n  - Enforce pod security standards\n  - Block privileged containers\n  - Require Konecta Artifact Registry as image source\n  - Enforce annotation/label standards\n\n---\n\n## 10. Observability\n\n### Stack\n| Component | Tool | Purpose |\n|-----------|------|---------|\n| Metrics | Prometheus | Scrape K8s + application metrics |\n| Dashboards | Grafana | Visualization, alerting |\n| Tracing | Jaeger | Distributed traces (OpenTelemetry) |\n| Logging | GCP Cloud Logging | Centralized log aggregation |\n| Instrumentation | OpenTelemetry SDK | App-level traces, metrics, logs |\n| Collection | OTel Collector (DaemonSet) | Receive, process, export telemetry |\n\n### Application Integration\n- All services emit OpenTelemetry traces/metrics (already instrumented)\n- OTel Collector as DaemonSet in `shared-monitoring` receives and exports\n- Prometheus scrapes `/metrics` endpoints via PodMonitor/ServiceMonitor CRDs\n- Grafana dashboards per service tier (AI workloads, APIs, frontends)\n\n### Key Alerts\n- Pod CrashLoopBackOff > 2 restarts\n- Error rate > 5% for 3 min (triggers rollback)\n- P95 latency > 2x baseline\n- HPA at max replicas for > 10 min\n- Certificate expiry < 14 days\n- ArgoCD sync failure\n\n---\n\n## 11. Cost & Operational Considerations\n\n### Cloud Run vs GKE Cost Model\n\n| Factor | Cloud Run | GKE (Target) |\n|--------|-----------|---------------|\n| Billing | Per-request (scale to zero) | Reserved capacity (always-on) |\n| Idle cost | $0 | Node pool minimum |\n| Burst cost | High (cold starts + per-invocation) | Lower (pre-provisioned pods) |\n| Predictability | Variable | Predictable |\n\n**Mitigation for idle cost**: GKE Autopilot scales node pools down. Low-traffic Tier 3 services can run with 1 replica (HPA min=1). Consider `minReplicas: 0` with KEDA for truly idle services.\n\n### Operational Overhead\n- **Helm chart maintenance**: Base chart library reduces per-service overhead\n- **ArgoCD operations**: Self-healing reduces manual intervention\n- **Monitoring**: Prometheus + Grafana replaces fragmented Cloud Run metrics\n- **On-call**: Standardized runbooks across all services (same platform, same patterns)\n\n### Disaster Recovery\n- **Multi-region**: Secondary GKE cluster in eu-west4 (already provisioned)\n- **Failover**: DNS-based failover to secondary cluster\n- **Data**: Cloud SQL cross-region replicas, GCS multi-regional buckets\n- **RPO**: Near-zero (managed services handle replication)\n- **RTO**: <15 min (ArgoCD can deploy to secondary cluster from same Git state)\n\n---\n\n## 12. Risk Register\n\n| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |\n|------|-----------|--------|------------|\n| Service downtime during migration | Medium | High | Dual-stack (Cloud Run + K8s) during transition, DNS rollback |\n| Resource sizing incorrect for K8s | Medium | Medium | Start with generous limits, tune based on metrics after migration |\n| AI workloads exceed pod memory | Medium | Medium | Startup probes, proper memory limits, HPA scale-out |\n| Secret exposure during migration | Low | Critical | External Secrets Operator, no secrets in Git, Workload Identity only |\n| ArgoCD sync storms (42 services) | Low | Medium | Sync waves, rate limiting, progressive rollout |\n| Team unfamiliarity with Helm/K8s | Medium | Medium | Pilot phase with 3 services, runbook documentation, training |\n| Build pipeline bottleneck | Low | Medium | Parallel builds, BuildKit caching, reusable workflows |\n| Kong ingress misconfiguration | Low | High | Canary routing during cutover, automated integration tests |\n\n---\n\n## Appendix A: Technology Stack Summary\n\n| Component | Technology | Version |\n|-----------|-----------|---------|\n| Container Orchestration | GKE Autopilot | Latest |\n| Package Management | Helm | 3.13+ |\n| GitOps CD | ArgoCD | 2.8+ |\n| CI | GitHub Actions | N/A |\n| Container Registry | GCP Artifact Registry | N/A |\n| API Gateway | Kong | Existing |\n| LLM Proxy | LiteLLM | Existing |\n| Secrets | External Secrets Operator + GCP Secret Manager | Latest |\n| Policy Engine | Kyverno | 1.11+ |\n| Cert Management | cert-manager + Let's Encrypt | Latest |\n| Vulnerability Scanning | Trivy | 0.48+ |\n| Image Signing | Cosign (Sigstore) | 2.2+ |\n| SBOM | Syft | 0.80+ |\n| Metrics | Prometheus | Latest |\n| Dashboards | Grafana | Latest |\n| Tracing | Jaeger + OpenTelemetry | Latest |\n| IaC | Terraform | >= 1.5.0 |\n\n## Appendix B: Namespace Map\n\n```\nshared-argocd          (existing)\nshared-kong            (existing)\nshared-litellm         (existing)\nshared-monitoring      (new)\nshared-cert-manager    (new)\nshared-secrets         (new — ESO)\n\ndev-api-services       dev-mcp-servers       dev-ai-workflows       dev-web-apps\nqa-api-services        qa-mcp-servers        qa-ai-workflows        qa-web-apps\nprod-api-services      prod-mcp-servers      prod-ai-workflows      prod-web-apps\n```\n\n## Appendix C: Service-to-Namespace Mapping\n\n| Service | Namespace Domain | Tier |\n|---------|-----------------|------|\n| kon-mcp-gateway | mcp-servers | 1 |\n| kon-iq-portal (backend) | api-services | 1 |\n| kon-iq-portal (frontend) | web-apps | 1 |\n| kon-app-hrfraud (backend) | api-services | 1 |\n| kon-app-hrfraud (frontend) | web-apps | 1 |\n| kon-litellm | shared-litellm | 1 |\n| kon-mcp-ragengine | mcp-servers | 2 |\n| kon-mcp-channelhub | mcp-servers | 2 |\n| kon-mcp-google-search | mcp-servers | 2 |\n| kon-mcp-googlemaps | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-docingestion | mcp-servers | 2 |\n| kon-mcp-docx | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-diagram | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-pptx | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-markdown | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-webcrawler | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-crawl4ai | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-gcs | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-mcp-client | mcp-servers | 3 |\n| kon-flow-competitor-intelligence | ai-workflows | 2 |\n| kon-flow-marketintelligence | ai-workflows | 2 |\n| kon-flow-pptx | ai-workflows | 3 |\n| kon-flow-rag-ingestion | ai-workflows | 2 |\n| kon-crew-diagram | ai-workflows | 3 |\n| kon-crew-docx | ai-workflows | 3 |\n| kon-crewai-google-search-tool | ai-workflows | 3 |\n| kon-crewai-ragengine-tool | ai-workflows | 3 |\n| kon-crewai-channelhub-tool | ai-workflows | 3 |\n| kon-crewai-diagram-tool | ai-workflows | 3 }

     1→# Architecture Blueprint: Migration to KIX-Infrastructure Platform
     2→
     3→## Konecta TIC — Cloud Run to Kubernetes (Helm + ArgoCD + GitHub Actions)
     4→
     5→---
     6→
     7→## Table of Contents
     8→
     9→1. [Executive Summary](#1-executive-summary)
    10→2. [Current State](#2-current-state)
    11→3. [Target Architecture](#3-target-architecture)
    12→4. [Service Inventory & Tiering](#4-service-inventory--tiering)
    13→5. [Migration Strategy](#5-migration-strategy)

    22→---
    23→
    24→## 1. Executive Summary
    25→
    26→This blueprint defines the architecture for migrating ~42 microservices from GCP Cloud Run to the **kix-infrastructure** Kubernetes platform. The migration adopts:
    27→
    28→- **Helm charts** for declarative Kubernetes packaging
    29→- **Secure multi-stage Docker builds** with vulnerability scanning and image signing
    30→- **GitHub Actions** for CI (build, scan, push)
    31→- **ArgoCD** for CD (GitOps-driven deployment across dev/qa/prod)
    32→
    33→**Key principles**: Simple, secure, auditable, repeatable.
    34→
    35→---
    36→
    37→## 2. Current State
    38→
    39→### Infrastructure
    40→- **Compute**: GCP Cloud Run (most services), GKE Autopilot (Kong, LiteLLM, ArgoCD), Cloud Functions (1 bot)
    41→- **Clusters**: GKE Autopilot in eu-west1/eu-west4, private with Cloud NAT
    42→- **CI/CD**: Cloud Build (8 services), GitHub Actions (Terraform only)
    43→- **Registry**: GCP Artifact Registry
    44→- **IaC**: Terraform with modular structure
    45→
    46→### Services (42 total)
    47→| Category | Count | Stack |
    48→|----------|-------|-------|
    49→| MCP Servers | 14 | Python (FastMCP) |
    50→| CrewAI Flows & Tools | 12 | Python (CrewAI) |
    51→| Business Apps | 4 | Python (FastAPI) + Node.js (Nuxt 3) |
    52→| Data Pipelines | 3 | Python |
    53→| Infrastructure Repos | 4 | Terraform/HCL |
    54→| Config/Proxies | 2 | Python (LiteLLM) |
    55→| Bots | 1 | Python (Cloud Functions) |
    56→| Prototypes | 2 | Python |
    57→
    58→### External Dependencies (remain as managed services)
    59→- **Cloud SQL** (PostgreSQL 16+)
    60→- **Firestore** (rate limiting, conversations)
    61→- **Vertex AI** (Gemini, Claude via Vertex, RAG Engine)
    62→- **Cloud Storage** (GCS)
    63→- **Pub/Sub** (async messaging)
    64→- **Google APIs** (Search, Maps, Drive, Chat)
    65→
    66→---
    67→
    68→## 3. Target Architecture
    69→
    70→### High-Level Component Diagram
    71→
    72→```
    73→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    74→│                           INTERNET                                      │
    75→└─────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    76→                      │ HTTPS
    77→┌─────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    78→│                    GCP Load Balancer + Cloud Armor (WAF)                 │
    79→└─────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    80→                      │
    81→┌─────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    82→│              KIX-INFRASTRUCTURE GKE CLUSTER (eu-west1)                  │
    83→│                                                                         │
    84→│  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
    85→│  │  shared-kong     │  │  shared-monitoring                          │  │
    86→│  │  (Kong Gateway)  │  │  Prometheus / Grafana / Jaeger / OTel       │  │
    87→│  └────────┬────────┘  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
    88→│           │                                                             │
    89→│  ┌────────▼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    90→│  │  Application Namespaces (per environment × domain)              │    │
    91→│  │                                                                 │    │
    92→│  │  {env}-api-services     → FastAPI backends, business apps       │    │
    93→│  │  {env}-mcp-servers      → MCP protocol servers (SSE)            │    │
    94→│  │  {env}-ai-workflows     → CrewAI flows & tools                  │    │

    96→│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    97→│                                                                         │

    99→│  │ shared-litellm│  │ shared-argocd│  │ shared-secrets│                 │
   100→│  │ (LLM Proxy)  │  │ (GitOps CD)  │  │ (ESO + CSI)  │                 │
   101→│  └──────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └───────────────┘                 │
   102→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   103→         │                    │                    │

   105→┌─────────────┐  ┌───────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐

   107→│ (PostgreSQL)│  │  Pub/Sub / APIs   │  │                     │
