   110→
   111→### CI/CD Flow
   112→
   113→```
   114→Developer Push → GitHub PR → GitHub Actions CI
   115→                                ├── Lint + Unit Tests
   116→                                ├── Docker Build (BuildKit, multi-stage)
   117→                                ├── Trivy Scan + SBOM (Syft)
   118→                                ├── Push to Artifact Registry
   119→                                └── Cosign Sign (OIDC)
   120→                                         │
   121→                              Update Helm values-{env}.yaml in Git
   122→                                         │
   123→                              ArgoCD Detects Git Change
   124→                                ├── Dev: Auto-sync
   125→                                ├── QA: Manual sync (08:00-18:00 UTC)
   126→                                └── Prod: Manual sync (Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)
   127→```
   128→
   129→---
   130→
   131→## 4. Service Inventory & Tiering
   132→
   133→### Tier 1 — Critical (Zero-Downtime Required)
   134→| Service | Type | Why Critical |
   135→|---------|------|-------------|
   136→| kon-mcp-gateway | MCP Gateway | Central routing hub for all MCP communication |
   137→| kon-iq-portal | Nuxt + API | User-facing business application |
   138→| kon-app-hrfraud | FastAPI + Nuxt | Business-critical fraud detection |
   139→| kon-litellm | LLM Proxy | All AI services depend on it |
   140→| Kong Gateway | API Gateway | External traffic entry point |
   141→
   142→### Tier 2 — Important (Brief Downtime Tolerable)
   143→| Service | Type |
   144→|---------|------|
   145→| kon-mcp-ragengine | RAG retrieval |
   146→| kon-mcp-channelhub | Email/messaging |
   147→| kon-mcp-google-search | Search integration |
   148→| kon-flow-competitor-intelligence | CrewAI workflow |
   149→| kon-flow-marketintelligence | CrewAI workflow |
   150→| kon-mcp-docingestion | Document processing |
   151→| kon-rag-ingestion | Data pipeline |
   152→
   153→### Tier 3 — Standard (Downtime Acceptable During Migration)
   154→All remaining MCP servers, CrewAI tools, prototypes, and bots.
   155→
   156→---
   157→
   158→## 5. Migration Strategy
   159→
   160→### Phased Approach
   161→
   162→**Phase 1 — Foundation & Pilot (3 services)**
   163→- Set up Helm base chart library
   164→- Configure ArgoCD ApplicationSets
   165→- Set up GitHub Actions reusable workflows (build, scan, push)
   166→- Configure GitHub OIDC → GCP Workload Identity Pool
   167→- Migrate 3 Tier-3 services as pilots (e.g., kon-mcp-diagram, kon-mcp-markdown, kon-mcp-pptx)
   168→- Validate full CI/CD pipeline end-to-end
   169→- Establish monitoring baseline
   170→
   171→**Phase 2 — MCP Servers & Tools (15 services)**
   172→- Migrate remaining MCP servers (internal, low traffic)
   173→- Migrate CrewAI tool wrappers
   174→- Validate MCP gateway routing to K8s-hosted backends
   175→- Run dual-stack (Cloud Run + K8s) during transition
   176→
   177→**Phase 3 — AI Workflows & Data Pipelines (10 services)**
   178→- Migrate CrewAI flows (higher resource requirements)
   179→- Migrate RAG ingestion pipelines
   180→- Tune resource profiles for AI workloads
   181→- Migrate Cloud Functions bot to K8s CronJob/Deployment
   182→
   183→**Phase 4 — Business Applications & Cutover (remaining services)**
   184→- Migrate Tier 1 services (kon-iq-portal, kon-app-hrfraud, kon-mcp-gateway)
   185→- Blue-green DNS cutover per service
   186→- Decommission Cloud Run services after 2-week soak period
   187→- Final Cloud Run teardown
   188→
   189→### DNS & Traffic Cutover Strategy
   190→- Each service gets a K8s Service + Ingress via Kong
   191→- During migration: Cloud Run and K8s versions run side-by-side
   192→- DNS switch per service (weighted routing or instant CNAME swap)
   193→- Rollback: Revert DNS to Cloud Run if K8s version fails
   194→- Post-migration: Remove Cloud Run service after validation soak
   195→
   196→### Stateless vs Stateful Handling
   197→- **All application services are stateless** — state lives in Cloud SQL, Firestore, GCS
   198→- Container images contain no data; configuration via ConfigMaps/Secrets
   199→- No persistent volumes needed for application pods
   200→- Temporary storage (`emptyDir`) for: `/tmp`, ML model caches, video processing scratch space
   201→
   202→---
   203→
   204→## 6. Kubernetes & Helm Chart Design
   205→
   206→### 6.1 Helm Chart Strategy
   207→
   208→**Hybrid: Base Chart Library + Per-Service Charts**
   209→
   210→```
   211→charts/
   212→├── base-service-chart/          # Reusable library chart (not deployed directly)
   213→│   ├── Chart.yaml               # type: library
   214→│   └── templates/
   215→│       ├── _deployment.tpl
   216→│       ├── _service.tpl
   217→│       ├── _ingress.tpl
   218→│       ├── _hpa.tpl
   219→│       ├── _pdb.tpl
   220→│       ├── _networkpolicy.tpl
   221→│       ├── _serviceaccount.tpl
   222→│       └── _external-secret.tpl
   223→│
   224→├── kon-mcp-gateway/             # Per-service chart (lightweight wrapper)
   225→│   ├── Chart.yaml               # depends on base-service-chart
   226→│   ├── values.yaml              # Service defaults
   227→│   ├── values-dev.yaml          # Dev overrides
   228→│   ├── values-qa.yaml           # QA overrides
   229→│   └── values-prod.yaml         # Prod overrides
   230→│
   231→├── kon-iq-portal/
   232→│   ├── Chart.yaml
   233→│   ├── values.yaml
   234→│   └── values-{env}.yaml
   235→│
   236→└── ... (one per service)
   237→```
   238→
   239→**Rationale**: 42 services require standardization (base chart), but domain diversity demands flexibility (per-service values). Avoids monolithic umbrella chart while preventing template sprawl.
   240→
   241→### 6.2 Namespace Strategy
   242→
   243→**Environment × Domain Boundaries (17 namespaces total)**
   244→
   245→| Namespace Pattern | Contents |
   246→|-------------------|----------|
   247→| `{env}-api-services` | FastAPI backends, business apps |
   248→| `{env}-mcp-servers` | MCP protocol servers |
   249→| `{env}-ai-workflows` | CrewAI flows and tools |
   250→| `{env}-web-apps` | Nuxt 3 frontends |
   251→| `shared-kong` | Kong API Gateway |
   252→| `shared-litellm` | LiteLLM proxy |
   253→| `shared-argocd` | ArgoCD (existing) |
   254→| `shared-monitoring` | Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger |
   255→| `shared-cert-manager` | TLS certificate management |
   256→
   257→Each namespace gets: ResourceQuotas, LimitRanges, default NetworkPolicies (deny-all ingress), RBAC bindings.
   258→
   259→### 6.3 Resource Profiles
   260→
   261→| Profile | CPU Request | CPU Limit | Mem Request | Mem Limit | Use For |
   262→|---------|------------|-----------|-------------|-----------|---------|
   263→| **AI Workload** | 500m | 2000m | 1Gi | 2Gi | CrewAI flows, RAG services |
   264→| **Web Service** | 100m | 500m | 256Mi | 512Mi | FastAPI APIs, MCP servers |
   265→| **Frontend** | 50m | 200m | 128Mi | 256Mi | Nuxt 3 SSR |
   266→
   267→### 6.4 Health Probes
   268→
   269→| Service Type | Liveness | Readiness | Startup |
   270→|-------------|----------|-----------|---------|
   271→| FastAPI | `GET /healthz` 8000 | `GET /readyz` 8000 (checks DB) | N/A |
   272→| MCP Server (SSE) | `GET /healthz` 8000, period=30s | `GET /readyz` 8000, initial=30s | N/A |
   273→| Nuxt 3 | `GET /` 3000 | `GET /_health` 3000 | N/A |
   274→| AI/CrewAI | `GET /healthz` 8000 | `GET /readyz` 8000 | failureThreshold=30 (5min) |
   275→
   276→### 6.5 Autoscaling (HPA)
   277→
   278→| Tier | Min | Max | Target CPU | Scale-Down Window |
   279→|------|-----|-----|------------|-------------------|
   280→| User-facing (frontends, public APIs) | 2 | 20 | 60% | 5 min |
   281→| Internal APIs, MCP servers | 2 | 10 | 75% | 10 min |
   282→| AI workflows | 1 | 5 | 80% | 15 min |
   283→
   284→Custom metrics via Prometheus Adapter: request rate, Pub/Sub queue depth, active SSE connections.
   285→
   286→### 6.6 Service Mesh
   287→
   288→**Decision: No (initially).** Kong handles ingress traffic management, auth, and rate limiting. Introducing Istio/Linkerd during a 42-service migration adds unnecessary risk. Revisit post-migration if mTLS between services or advanced traffic management is required.
   289→
   290→### 6.7 Ingress Architecture
   291→
   292→```
   293→Internet → GCP LB (TLS termination) → Kong Ingress Controller → Application Services
   294→```
   295→
   296→- **External services**: Kong Ingress with `konghq.com/plugins` annotations (rate-limiting, CORS, auth)
   297→- **Internal services**: ClusterIP only, accessed via `service.namespace.svc.cluster.local`
   298→- **TLS**: cert-manager + Let's Encrypt (ClusterIssuer) for automated certificate management
   299→- **DNS per environment**: `dev.api.konecta.io`, `qa.api.konecta.io`, `api.konecta.io`
   300→
   301→### 6.8 ArgoCD ApplicationSet Strategy
   302→
   303→**Matrix Generator: Environment × Service**
   304→
   305→- Single ApplicationSet discovers all `charts/*/` directories and creates Applications across 3 environments
   306→- Each Application references `values.yaml` + `values-{env}.yaml`
   307→- **Dev**: `syncPolicy.automated` (auto-sync on commit)
   308→- **QA**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, business-hours window)
   309→- **Prod**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)
   310→- ArgoCD Projects for RBAC: `dev-project`, `qa-project`, `prod-project`
   311→- Sync waves: infrastructure resources (wave 0) → application deployments (wave 10)
   312→
   313→---
   314→
   315→## 7. Secure Docker Builds
   316→
   317→### 7.1 Base Image Policy
   318→
   319→| Stack | Builder Stage | Runtime Stage |
   320→|-------|--------------|---------------|
   321→| Python (standard) | `python:3.x-slim` | `python:3.x-slim` |
   322→| Python (ML: opencv, whisper, ffmpeg) | `python:3.x-slim` + apt deps | `python:3.x-slim` + runtime-only libs |
   323→| Node.js | `node:20-alpine` | `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12` |
   324→
   325→- Pin to **full digest** in CI (updated monthly by Renovate/Dependabot)
   326→- Parameterize Python version via `ARG PYTHON_VERSION=3.11`
   327→- **Never use `:latest`**
   328→
   329→### 7.2 Multi-Stage Build Patterns
   330→
   331→**Python services:**
   332→```
   333→Stage 1 "builder": python:3.x-slim → install build deps → pip install --prefix=/install
   334→Stage 2 "runtime": python:3.x-slim → COPY --from=builder /install → non-root USER → ENTRYPOINT
   335→```
   336→
   337→**Node.js services:**
   338→```
   339→Stage 1 "deps": node:20-alpine → npm ci
   340→Stage 2 "builder": COPY source → npm run build → npm prune --omit=dev
   341→Stage 3 "runtime": distroless/nodejs20 → COPY .output → no shell → ENTRYPOINT
   342→```
   343→
   344→### 7.3 Security Hardening
   345→
   346→- **Non-root user** (UID 65532) in all images, enforced by `runAsNonRoot: true` in pod spec
   347→- **Read-only root filesystem** — writable paths via `emptyDir` mounts (`/tmp`, `/app/.cache`)
   348→- **No shell** in Node.js production images (distroless)
   349→- **Drop ALL capabilities** in pod security context
   350→- **No `.env` files** baked into images; all config via K8s secrets/configmaps
   351→- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` for Python images
   352→
   353→### 7.4 Dependency Management
   354→
   355→- Python: `pip-compile` or `uv lock` for pinned requirements with hashes
   356→- Node.js: `npm ci` only (never `npm install` in CI)
   357→- Weekly Renovate/Dependabot PRs for dependency updates
   358→
   359→### 7.5 Build Metadata (OCI Labels)
   360→
   361→All images include: `org.opencontainers.image.created`, `.revision` (git SHA), `.version`, `.source`, `.title`, `.vendor` — final stage only.
   362→
   363→### 7.6 Image Size Targets
   364→
   365→| Category | Target | Optimization |
   366→|----------|--------|-------------|
   367→| Python standard | < 200 MB | Multi-stage, no cache, strip pyc |
   368→| Python ML/heavy | < 500 MB | opencv-headless, models as external volumes |
   369→| Node.js (Nuxt) | < 80 MB | Distroless runtime, npm prune |
   370→
   371→---
   372→
   373→## 8. CI/CD Pipeline (GitHub Actions + ArgoCD)
   374→
   375→### 8.1 Workflow Strategy
   376→
   377→**Monorepo with path-based triggers + reusable workflows**
   378→
   379→```
   380→.github/workflows/
   381→├── _build-image.yaml        # Reusable: Docker build + cache
   382→├── _scan-image.yaml         # Reusable: Trivy + SBOM + Cosign
   383→├── _push-registry.yaml      # Reusable: Push to Artifact Registry
   384→├── _deploy-helm.yaml        # Reusable: Update Helm values in Git
   385→├── ci-service.yaml          # Triggered per-service on path change
   386→└── ci-shared.yaml           # Triggered on shared lib changes (rebuilds dependents)
   387→```
   388→
   389→- Path filters ensure only modified services rebuild
   390→- Dynamic matrix strategy for parallel builds across services
   391→- Shared library changes trigger dependent services via `IMPACT_MAP.json`
   392→
   393→### 8.2 Image Tagging Strategy
   394→
   395→| Tag | When | Purpose |
   396→|-----|------|---------|
   397→| `sha-{7-char}` | Every build | Immutable build identifier |
   398→| `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | On git tag | Release version |
   399→| `{env}-latest` | On deploy | Environment pointer |
   400→
   401→**Rule**: SHA tags are immutable. Never overwrite.
   402→
   403→### 8.3 Security Gates
   404→
   405→```
   406→Build → Trivy Scan → SBOM Generation (Syft) → Push → Cosign Sign (OIDC)
   407→         ↓ block                                        ↓
   408→   CRITICAL/HIGH CVE                           Kyverno admission policy
   409→   fails the build                             enforces signed images (prod)
   410→```
   411→
   412→- **Trivy**: CRITICAL/HIGH block push; MEDIUM logged; LOW informational
   413→- **SBOM**: SPDX + CycloneDX dual format, attached as OCI annotation
   414→- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore (no long-lived keys)
   415→- **Kyverno**: Cluster admission controller verifies image signatures before deploy (prod only)
   416→
   417→### 8.4 GitOps Flow
   418→
   419→1. PR merged → GitHub Actions builds and pushes image (`sha-abc1234`)
   420→2. Post-build step updates `charts/{service}/values-dev.yaml` with new image tag
   421→3. Commits to main: `[ci] Update {service} image to sha-abc1234`
   422→4. ArgoCD detects Git change → syncs to cluster
   423→5. Dev auto-syncs immediately; QA/Prod require manual sync
   424→
   425→### 8.5 Environment Promotion
   426→
   427→| Environment | Trigger | Sync Mode | Change Window |
   428→|-------------|---------|-----------|---------------|
   429→| Dev | Every merge to main | Auto-sync | 24/7 |
   430→| QA | Manual promotion | Manual sync | 08:00-18:00 UTC |
   431→| Prod | Manual promotion + approval | Manual sync | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |
   432→
   433→**Promotion**: Update `values-{target-env}.yaml` with the image tag from the previous environment.
   434→
   435→### 8.6 Rollback Strategy
   436→
   437→- **Automated** (prod): Prometheus alert on error rate >5% for 3 min → webhook reverts Git commit → ArgoCD syncs previous image
   438→- **Manual**: Engineer reverts `values-prod.yaml` to previous SHA tag, commits, ArgoCD syncs
   439→- **RTO target**: <5 min (detection + revert + sync)
   440→- **Git as source of truth**: Every rollback is a visible Git commit
   441→
   442→### 8.7 Secrets Management (CI)
   443→
   444→- **GitHub OIDC** → GCP Workload Identity Pool (no JSON keys)
   445→- Dynamic secret fetching from GCP Secret Manager at build time
   446→- Build secrets via `docker build --secret` (never in image layers)
   447→- Token lifetime ~1 hour, auto-refreshing, full audit trail
   448→
   449→---
   450→
   451→## 9. Security Architecture
   452→
   453→### 9.1 Container Image Security
   454→
   455→- **Multi-stage builds**: Builder dependencies never ship in runtime image
   456→- **Base image policy**: Pinned digests, updated monthly, slim/distroless only
   457→- **Vulnerability scanning**: Trivy pre-push, CRITICAL/HIGH block deployment
   458→- **Image signing**: Cosign keyless (OIDC) — every image cryptographically signed
   459→- **SBOM**: Generated for every build, stored as OCI annotation
   460→- **Registry**: Artifact Registry with vulnerability scanning enabled, IAM-restricted access
   461→
   462→### 9.2 Supply Chain Security
   463→
   464→- **Dependency pinning**: Hash-verified `requirements.txt` (Python), `package-lock.json` (Node.js)
   465→- **Dependency scanning**: `pip audit` / `npm audit` in CI, Renovate for updates
   466→- **GitHub Actions hardening**:
   467→  - Pin action versions by SHA (not tag)
   468→  - Minimal `GITHUB_TOKEN` permissions per workflow
   469→  - No third-party actions without security review
   470→  - OIDC for all GCP interactions (no stored credentials)
   471→
   472→### 9.3 Runtime Security
   473→
   474→- **Pod Security Standards**: `restricted` profile enforced via namespace labels
   475→  - `runAsNonRoot: true`
   476→  - `readOnlyRootFilesystem: true`
   477→  - `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`
   478→  - `capabilities.drop: [ALL]`
   479→  - `seccompProfile.type: RuntimeDefault`
   480→- **No privileged containers**. No `hostNetwork`, `hostPID`, `hostIPC`.
   481→- Writable paths only via explicit `emptyDir` volume mounts
   482→
   483→### 9.4 Secrets Management (Runtime)
   484→
   485→**External Secrets Operator (ESO) + GCP Secret Manager**
   486→
   487→- ESO installed in `shared-secrets` namespace
   488→- `SecretStore` per environment, authenticated via Workload Identity
   489→- `ExternalSecret` CRDs in each service Helm chart pull secrets from GCP
   490→- Secrets never stored in Git or Helm values files
   491→- Automatic rotation support via ESO refresh interval
   492→
   493→**Workload Identity for GCP API access**:
   494→```yaml
   495→serviceAccount:
   496→  annotations:
   497→    iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: {service}@{project}.iam.gserviceaccount.com
   498→```
   499→No service account JSON keys. Ever.
   500→
   501→### 9.5 Network Security
   502→
   503→- **Default deny ingress** on all application namespaces
   504→- **Explicit NetworkPolicies** per service: allow only expected sources
   505→- **GCP health check CIDRs** allowed (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)
   506→- **Cloud Armor WAF** on external load balancer (OWASP top-10 rules)
   507→- **Private cluster**: No public node IPs, egress via Cloud NAT
   508→- **DNS policies**: Restrict external DNS resolution where possible
   509→
   510→### 9.6 RBAC & Namespace Isolation
   511→
   512→- **Service accounts per namespace** with least-privilege Roles
   513→- **ArgoCD Projects** restrict which namespaces each team can deploy to
   514→- **Namespace ResourceQuotas** prevent resource exhaustion attacks
   515→- **Kyverno policies**:
   516→  - Enforce signed images (prod)
   517→  - Block `latest` tag
   518→  - Require resource limits
   519→  - Enforce label standards
   520→
   521→### 9.7 Admission Control
   522→
   523→- **Kyverno** (preferred over OPA/Gatekeeper for readability):
   524→  - Verify Cosign image signatures
   525→  - Enforce pod security standards
   526→  - Block privileged containers
   527→  - Require Konecta Artifact Registry as image source
   528→  - Enforce annotation/label standards
   529→
   530→---
   531→
   532→## 10. Observability
   533→
   534→### Stack
   535→| Component | Tool | Purpose |
   536→|-----------|------|---------|
   537→| Metrics | Prometheus | Scrape K8s + application metrics |
   538→| Dashboards | Grafana | Visualization, alerting |
   539→| Tracing | Jaeger | Distributed traces (OpenTelemetry) |
   540→| Logging | GCP Cloud Logging | Centralized log aggregation |
   541→| Instrumentation | OpenTelemetry SDK | App-level traces, metrics, logs |
   542→| Collection | OTel Collector (DaemonSet) | Receive, process, export telemetry |
   543→
   544→### Application Integration
   545→- All services emit OpenTelemetry traces/metrics (already instrumented)
   546→- OTel Collector as DaemonSet in `shared-monitoring` receives and exports
   547→- Prometheus scrapes `/metrics` endpoints via PodMonitor/ServiceMonitor CRDs
   548→- Grafana dashboards per service tier (AI workloads, APIs, frontends)
   549→
   550→### Key Alerts
   551→- Pod CrashLoopBackOff > 2 restarts
   552→- Error rate > 5% for 3 min (triggers rollback)
   553→- P95 latency > 2x baseline
   554→- HPA at max replicas for > 10 min
   555→- Certificate expiry < 14 days
   556→- ArgoCD sync failure
   557→
   558→---
   559→
   560→## 11. Cost & Operational Considerations
   561→
   562→### Cloud Run vs GKE Cost Model
   563→
   564→| Factor | Cloud Run | GKE (Target) |
   565→|--------|-----------|---------------|
   566→| Billing | Per-request (scale to zero) | Reserved capacity (always-on) |
   567→| Idle cost | $0 | Node pool minimum |
   568→| Burst cost | High (cold starts + per-invocation) | Lower (pre-provisioned pods) |
   569→| Predictability | Variable | Predictable |
   570→
   571→**Mitigation for idle cost**: GKE Autopilot scales node pools down. Low-traffic Tier 3 services can run with 1 replica (HPA min=1). Consider `minReplicas: 0` with KEDA for truly idle services.
   572→
   573→### Operational Overhead
   574→- **Helm chart maintenance**: Base chart library reduces per-service overhead
   575→- **ArgoCD operations**: Self-healing reduces manual intervention
   576→- **Monitoring**: Prometheus + Grafana replaces fragmented Cloud Run metrics
   577→- **On-call**: Standardized runbooks across all services (same platform, same patterns)
   578→
   579→### Disaster Recovery
   580→- **Multi-region**: Secondary GKE cluster in eu-west4 (already provisioned)
   581→- **Failover**: DNS-based failover to secondary cluster
   582→- **Data**: Cloud SQL cross-region replicas, GCS multi-regional buckets
   583→- **RPO**: Near-zero (managed services handle replication)
   584→- **RTO**: <15 min (ArgoCD can deploy to secondary cluster from same Git state)
   585→
   586→---
   587→
   588→## 12. Risk Register
   589→
   590→| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
   591→|------|-----------|--------|------------|
   592→| Service downtime during migration | Medium | High | Dual-stack (Cloud Run + K8s) during transition, DNS rollback |
   593→| Resource sizing incorrect for K8s | Medium | Medium | Start with generous limits, tune based on metrics after migration |
   594→| AI workloads exceed pod memory | Medium | Medium | Startup probes, proper memory limits, HPA scale-out |
   595→| Secret exposure during migration | Low | Critical | External Secrets Operator, no secrets in Git, Workload Identity only |
   596→| ArgoCD sync storms (42 services) | Low | Medium | Sync waves, rate limiting, progressive rollout |
   597→| Team unfamiliarity with Helm/K8s | Medium | Medium | Pilot phase with 3 services, runbook documentation, training |
   598→| Build pipeline bottleneck | Low | Medium | Parallel builds, BuildKit caching, reusable workflows |
   599→| Kong ingress misconfiguration | Low | High | Canary routing during cutover, automated integration tests |
   600→
   601→---
   602→
   603→## Appendix A: Technology Stack Summary
   604→
   605→| Component | Technology | Version |
   606→|-----------|-----------|---------|
   607→| Container Orchestration | GKE Autopilot | Latest |
   608→| Package Management | Helm | 3.13+ |
   609→| GitOps CD | ArgoCD | 2.8+ |
   610→| CI | GitHub Actions | N/A |
   611→| Container Registry | GCP Artifact Registry | N/A |
   612→| API Gateway | Kong | Existing |
   613→| LLM Proxy | LiteLLM | Existing |
   614→| Secrets | External Secrets Operator + GCP Secret Manager | Latest |
   615→| Policy Engine | Kyverno | 1.11+ |
   616→| Cert Management | cert-manager + Let's Encrypt | Latest |
   617→| Vulnerability Scanning | Trivy | 0.48+ |
   618→| Image Signing | Cosign (Sigstore) | 2.2+ |
   619→| SBOM | Syft | 0.80+ |
   620→| Metrics | Prometheus | Latest |
   621→| Dashboards | Grafana | Latest |
   622→| Tracing | Jaeger + OpenTelemetry | Latest |
   623→| IaC | Terraform | >= 1.5.0 |
   624→

   626→

   628→shared-argocd          (existing)
   629→shared-kong            (existing)
   630→shared-litellm         (existing)

   639→

  "old_string": "### 6.1 Helm Chart Strategy\n\n**Hybrid: Base Chart Library + Per-Service Charts**\n\n```\ncharts/\n├── base-service-chart/          # Reusable library chart (not deployed directly)\n│   ├── Chart.yaml               # type: library\n│   └── templates/\n│       ├── _deployment.tpl\n│       ├── _service.tpl\n│       ├── _ingress.tpl\n│       ├── _hpa.tpl\n│       ├── _pdb.tpl\n│       ├── _networkpolicy.tpl\n│       ├── _serviceaccount.tpl\n│       └── _external-secret.tpl\n│\n├── kon-mcp-gateway/             # Per-service chart (lightweight wrapper)\n│   ├── Chart.yaml               # depends on base-service-chart\n│   ├── values.yaml              # Service defaults\n│   ├── values-dev.yaml          # Dev overrides\n│   ├── values-qa.yaml           # QA overrides\n│   └── values-prod.yaml         # Prod overrides\n│\n├── kon-iq-portal/\n│   ├── Chart.yaml\n│   ├── values.yaml\n│   └── values-{env}.yaml\n│\n└── ... (one per service)\n```\n\n**Rationale**: 42 services require standardization (base chart), but domain diversity demands flexibility (per-service values). Avoids monolithic umbrella chart while preventing template sprawl.",
  "new_string": "### 6.1 Helm Chart Strategy\n\n**Base Chart Library (shared repo) + Per-Service Chart (in each service repo) + GitOps Repo (value overrides)**\n\nThree-layer separation matching the repo-per-service model:\n\n```\nRepo: kon-helm-base-chart/           # Shared library chart, versioned & published to OCI registry\n├── Chart.yaml                       # type: library, version: semver\n└── templates/\n    ├── _deployment.tpl\n    ├── _service.tpl\n    ├── _ingress.tpl\n    ├── _hpa.tpl\n    ├── _pdb.tpl\n    ├── _networkpolicy.tpl\n    ├── _serviceaccount.tpl\n    └── _external-secret.tpl\n\nRepo: kon-mcp-gateway/ (each service repo)\n├── src/                             # Application source code\n├── Dockerfile\n├── chart/                           # Service-specific Helm chart\n│   ├── Chart.yaml                   # depends on kon-helm-base-chart (OCI reference)\n│   ├── values.yaml                  # Service defaults (port, probes, resources)\n│   └── templates/                   # Only if service has custom K8s resources\n└── .github/workflows/\n    └── ci.yaml                      # Calls reusable workflows from kon-ci-workflows\n\nRepo: kon-gitops/                    # ArgoCD watches this repo only\n├── apps/\n│   └── applicationset.yaml          # Matrix generator: env × service\n└── environments/\n    ├── dev/kon-mcp-gateway.yaml     # Overrides: image.tag, replicas, env-specific config\n    ├── qa/kon-mcp-gateway.yaml\n    └── prod/kon-mcp-gateway.yaml\n```\n\n**Flow**: Service repo CI builds image → dispatches to kon-gitops → updates `environments/dev/{service}.yaml` with new tag → ArgoCD syncs.\n\n**Base chart versioning**: Published to Artifact Registry as OCI Helm chart. Service charts pin to a specific version (`~0.5.0`). Base chart updates are rolled out by bumping the version in each service's `Chart.yaml` (Renovate automates this).\n\n**Rationale**: Repo-per-service gives teams autonomy, independent release cycles, and clear ownership. The shared base chart enforces platform standards without coupling deployments. The GitOps repo is the single source of truth for cluster state."
}
```

CI/CD Pipeline (GitHub Actions + ArgoCD)\n\n### 8.1 Workflow Strategy\n\n**Monorepo with path-based triggers + reusable workflows**\n\n```\n.github/workflows/\n├── _build-image.yaml        # Reusable: Docker build + cache\n├── _scan-image.yaml         # Reusable: Trivy + SBOM + Cosign\n├── _push-registry.yaml      # Reusable: Push to Artifact Registry\n├── _deploy-helm.yaml        # Reusable: Update Helm values in Git\n├── ci-service.yaml          # Triggered per-service on path change\n└── ci-shared.yaml           # Triggered on shared lib changes (rebuilds dependents)\n```\n\n- Path filters ensure only modified services rebuild\n- Dynamic matrix strategy for parallel builds across services\n- Shared library changes trigger dependent services via `IMPACT_MAP.json`\n\n### 8.2 Image Tagging Strategy\n\n| Tag | When | Purpose |\n|-----|------|---------|\n| `sha-{7-char}` | Every build | Immutable build identifier |\n| `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | On git tag | Release version |\n| `{env}-latest` | On deploy | Environment pointer |\n\n**Rule**: SHA tags are immutable. Never overwrite.\n\n### 8.3 Security Gates\n\n```\nBuild → Trivy Scan → SBOM Generation (Syft) → Push → Cosign Sign (OIDC)\n         ↓ block                                        ↓\n   CRITICAL/HIGH CVE                           Kyverno admission policy\n   fails the build                             enforces signed images (prod)\n```\n\n- **Trivy**: CRITICAL/HIGH block push; MEDIUM logged; LOW informational\n- **SBOM**: SPDX + CycloneDX dual format, attached as OCI annotation\n- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore (no long-lived keys)\n- **Kyverno**: Cluster admission controller verifies image signatures before deploy (prod only)\n\n### 8.4 GitOps Flow\n\n1. PR merged → GitHub Actions builds and pushes image (`sha-abc1234`)\n2. Post-build step updates `charts/{service}/values-dev.yaml` with new image tag\n3. Commits to main: `[ci] Update {service} image to sha-abc1234`\n4. ArgoCD detects Git change → syncs to cluster\n5. Dev auto-syncs immediately; QA/Prod require manual sync\n\n### 8.5 Environment Promotion\n\n| Environment | Trigger | Sync Mode | Change Window |\n|-------------|---------|-----------|---------------|\n| Dev | Every merge to main | Auto-sync | 24/7 |\n| QA | Manual promotion | Manual sync | 08:00-18:00 UTC |\n| Prod | Manual promotion + approval | Manual sync | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |\n\n**Promotion**: Update `values-{target-env}.yaml` with the image tag from the previous environment.\n\n### 8.6 Rollback Strategy\n\n- **Automated** (prod): Prometheus alert on error rate >5% for 3 min → webhook reverts Git commit → ArgoCD syncs previous image\n- **Manual**: Engineer reverts `values-prod.yaml` to previous SHA tag, commits, ArgoCD syncs\n- **RTO target**: <5 min (detection + revert + sync)\n- **Git as source of truth**: Every rollback is a visible Git commit\n\n### 8.7 Secrets Management (CI)\n\n- **GitHub OIDC** → GCP Workload Identity Pool (no JSON keys)\n- Dynamic secret fetching from GCP Secret Manager at build time\n- Build secrets via `docker build --secret` (never in image layers)\n- Token lifetime ~1 hour, auto-refreshing, full audit trail",
  "new_string": "## 8. CI/CD Pipeline (GitHub Actions + ArgoCD)\n\n### 8.1 Standard Pipeline Architecture (Repo-Per-Service)\n\nEvery service repo follows the **same pipeline** by calling reusable workflows from the shared `kon-ci-workflows` repo. A service onboards by adding a single `ci.yaml` file.\n\n**Shared Workflows Repo (`kon-ci-workflows/.github/workflows/`)**:\n\n```\nkon-ci-workflows/\n└── .github/workflows/\n    ├── standard-pipeline.yaml      # Main orchestrator (called by every service)\n    ├── _build-image.yaml           # Reusable: Docker build + BuildKit cache\n    ├── _scan-and-sign.yaml         # Reusable: Trivy + SBOM (Syft) + Cosign sign\n    ├── _push-registry.yaml         # Reusable: Push to Artifact Registry\n    ├── _update-gitops.yaml         # Reusable: Dispatch to kon-gitops repo\n    ├── _promote.yaml               # Reusable: Promote image tag between environments\n    └── _lint-and-test.yaml         # Reusable: Language-aware lint + unit tests\n```\n\n**Standard Service Repo Pipeline (every service copies this)**:\n\n```yaml\n# .github/workflows/ci.yaml (in each service repo — identical across all repos)\nname: CI\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n  workflow_dispatch:\n\njobs:\n  pipeline:\n    uses: konecta-org/kon-ci-workflows/.github/workflows/standard-pipeline.yaml@v1\n    with:\n      service-name: ${{ github.event.repository.name }}\n      dockerfile-path: ./Dockerfile\n      chart-path: ./chart\n      python-version: \"3.11\"     # or node-version: \"20\" for Node.js services\n    secrets: inherit\n```\n\n**That's it.** Every service repo has one small workflow file. All logic lives in the shared workflows repo. Updates to the pipeline roll out to all services by bumping the `@v1` tag.\n\n### 8.2 Standard Pipeline Stages\n\nEvery PR and merge goes through the same stages:\n\n```\n┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐\n│  1. LINT &   │──▶│  2. BUILD    │──▶│  3. SCAN &   │──▶│  4. PUSH &   │──▶│  5. DEPLOY   │\n│     TEST     │   │    IMAGE     │   │     SIGN     │   │   REGISTRY   │   │   (GitOps)   │\n└──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘\n  PR + merge         merge only         merge only         merge only         merge only\n  ├ ruff/eslint      ├ BuildKit         ├ Trivy scan       ├ Push to AR       ├ Dispatch to\n  ├ unit tests       ├ multi-stage      ├ SBOM (Syft)      ├ Tag: sha-xxx     │ kon-gitops\n  ├ type checks      ├ GHA cache        ├ Cosign sign      └ Tag: v1.2.3      ├ Update dev\n  └ pip audit/       └ OCI labels         (OIDC)             (on git tag)     │ values.yaml\n    npm audit                                                                  └ ArgoCD syncs\n```\n\n**On PR**: Only stage 1 runs (lint, test). Fast feedback.\n**On merge to main**: All 5 stages run. Image built, scanned, signed, pushed, deployed to dev.\n\n### 8.3 Image Tagging Strategy\n\n| Tag | When | Purpose |\n|-----|------|---------|\n| `sha-{7-char}` | Every build | Immutable build identifier |\n| `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | On git tag | Release version |\n| `{env}-latest` | On promotion | Environment pointer (mutable) |\n\n**Rule**: SHA tags are immutable. Never overwrite. Promotion copies the SHA tag reference, not the image.\n\n### 8.4 Security Gates\n\n```\nBuild → Trivy Scan → SBOM Generation (Syft) → Push → Cosign Sign (OIDC)\n         ↓ block                                        ↓\n   CRITICAL/HIGH CVE                           Kyverno admission policy\n   fails the build                             enforces signed images (prod)\n```\n\n- **Trivy**: CRITICAL/HIGH block push; MEDIUM logged; LOW informational\n- **SBOM**: SPDX + CycloneDX dual format, attached as OCI annotation\n- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore (no long-lived keys)\n- **Kyverno**: Cluster admission controller verifies image signatures before deploy (prod only)\n\n### 8.5 GitOps Flow (Cross-Repo)\n\n```\nService Repo (kon-mcp-gateway)              GitOps Repo (kon-gitops)\n─────────────────────────────               ─────────────────────────\n1. PR merged to main\n2. Pipeline builds sha-abc1234\n3. Image pushed to Artifact Registry\n4. Pipeline triggers repository_dispatch ──▶ 5. Automated PR or direct commit:\n                                                environments/dev/kon-mcp-gateway.yaml\n                                                  image.tag: sha-abc1234\n                                             6. ArgoCD detects change → syncs dev\n```\n\n**Cross-repo dispatch**: The `_update-gitops.yaml` workflow uses a GitHub App token to commit to `kon-gitops`. This keeps service repos decoupled from deployment concerns.\n\n### 8.6 Environment Promotion\n\n| Environment | Trigger | Sync Mode | Change Window |\n|-------------|---------|-----------|---------------|\n| Dev | Every merge to main | Auto-sync | 24/7 |\n| QA | Manual workflow dispatch or PR to kon-gitops | Manual sync | 08:00-18:00 UTC |\n| Prod | Manual workflow dispatch + approval | Manual sync | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |\n\n**Promotion workflow** (from `kon-ci-workflows/_promote.yaml`):\n1. Engineer triggers `promote` workflow on the service repo (or directly on kon-gitops)\n2. Workflow copies the image tag from `environments/dev/{service}.yaml` to `environments/qa/{service}.yaml`\n3. Creates a PR on kon-gitops (QA/Prod promotions always go through PR review)\n4. After merge, ArgoCD syncs\n\n### 8.7 Rollback Strategy\n\n- **Automated** (prod): Prometheus alert on error rate >5% for 3 min → webhook reverts commit in kon-gitops → ArgoCD syncs previous image\n- **Manual**: Engineer reverts the `environments/prod/{service}.yaml` entry to previous SHA tag via PR to kon-gitops\n- **RTO target**: <5 min (detection + revert + sync)\n- **Git as source of truth**: Every rollback is a visible Git commit in kon-gitops\n\n### 8.8 Secrets Management (CI)\n\n- **GitHub OIDC** → GCP Workload Identity Pool (no JSON keys, configured per org)\n- Dynamic secret fetching from GCP Secret Manager at build time\n- Build secrets via `docker build --secret` (never in image layers)\n- Token lifetime ~1 hour, auto-refreshing, full audit trail\n- **GitHub App token** for cross-repo dispatch (kon-gitops commits), scoped to minimal permissions\n\n### 8.9 Onboarding a New Service\n\nAdding a new service to the platform:\n\n1. **Create repo** from a template repo (`kon-service-template`) that includes:\n   - Standard `Dockerfile` (Python or Node.js)\n   - Standard `chart/` directory with `Chart.yaml` depending on base chart\n   - Standard `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` calling shared pipeline\n   - Standard `.dockerignore`, `CODEOWNERS`\n2. **Add to kon-gitops**: Create `environments/{dev,qa,prod}/{service-name}.yaml` files\n3. **ArgoCD auto-discovers** via ApplicationSet git directory generator\n4. **First push** triggers the standard pipeline — fully operational\n\nNo pipeline code to write. No Helm templates to create. Just fill in `values.yaml` with service-specific config."

Each file in `environments/{env}/` defines an application.\n\n- ApplicationSet scans `environments/dev/`, `environments/qa/`, `environments/prod/` directories\n- Each `{service}.yaml` file contains the Helm source repo URL, chart path, and environment values\n- ArgoCD renders the Helm chart from the **service repo** using values from **kon-gitops**\n- **Dev**: `syncPolicy.automated` (auto-sync on commit to kon-gitops)\n- **QA**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, business-hours window)\n- **Prod**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)\n- ArgoCD Projects for RBAC: `dev-project`, `qa-project`, `prod-project`\n- Sync waves: infrastructure resources (wave 0) → application deployments (wave 10)\n- New services are auto-discovered when their files are added to `environments/` directories"

Shared Terraform modules + Helm base chart\n│   │   ├── terraform/                  # Existing Terraform modules\n│   │   └── helm/base-service-chart/    # Shared Helm library chart (published to OCI)\n│   ├── kix-platform-ci-workflows       # Shared reusable GitHub Actions workflows (NEW)\n│   │   └── .github/workflows/\n│   │       ├── standard-pipeline.yaml\n│   │       ├── _build-image.yaml\n│   │       ├── _scan-and-sign.yaml\n│   │       ├── _push-registry.yaml\n│   │       ├── _update-gitops.yaml\n│   │       └── _promote.yaml\n│   ├── kix-platform-service-template   # Template repo for bootstrapping (NEW)\n│   └── kix-platform-docs              # Documentation\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-mcp-servers (MCP Integration Team)\n│   ├── kix-mcp-gateway                # Each MCP server has its own repo\n│   ├── kix-mcp-ragengine\n│   ├── kix-mcp-channelhub\n│   ├── kix-mcp-google-search\n│   └── kix-mcp-*                      # One per server (~14 repos)\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-apps (Apps Team)\n│   ├── kix-app-hrfraud\n│   ├── kix-app-hrportal\n│   └── kix-app-*                      # One per app\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-workloads (Use Case Teams)\n│   ├── kix-usecase-iq-portal\n│   ├── kix-usecase-testiq\n│   └── kix-usecase-*                  # All GitOps state (what's deployed where) lives in `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure`.   "old_string": "**Base Chart Library (shared repo) + Per-Service Chart (in each service repo) + GitOps Repo (value overrides)**\n\nThree-layer separation matching the repo-per-service model:\n\n```\nRepo: kon-helm-base-chart/           # Shared library chart, versioned & published to OCI registry\n├── Chart.yaml                       # type: library, version: semver\n└── templates/\n    ├── _deployment.tpl\n    ├── _service.tpl\n    ├── _ingress.tpl\n    ├── _hpa.tpl\n    ├── _pdb.tpl\n    ├── _networkpolicy.tpl\n    ├── _serviceaccount.tpl\n    └── _external-secret.tpl\n\nRepo: kon-mcp-gateway/ (each service repo)\n├── src/                             # Application source code\n├── Dockerfile\n├── chart/                           # Service-specific Helm chart\n│   ├── Chart.yaml                   # depends on kon-helm-base-chart (OCI reference)\n│   ├── values.yaml                  # Service defaults (port, probes, resources)\n│   └── templates/                   # Only if service has custom K8s resources\n└── .github/workflows/\n    └── ci.yaml                      # Calls reusable workflows from kon-ci-workflows\n\nRepo: kon-gitops/                    # ArgoCD watches this repo only\n├── apps/\n│   └── applicationset.yaml          # Matrix generator: env × service\n└── environments/\n    ├── dev/kon-mcp-gateway.yaml     # Overrides: image.tag, replicas, env-specific config\n    ├── qa/kon-mcp-gateway.yaml\n    └── prod/kon-mcp-gateway.yaml\n```\n\n**Flow**: Service repo CI builds image → dispatches to kon-gitops → updates `environments/dev/{service}.yaml` with new tag → ArgoCD syncs.\n\n**Base chart versioning**: Published to Artifact Registry as OCI Helm chart. Service charts pin to a specific version (`~0.5.0`). Base chart updates are rolled out by bumping the version in each service's `Chart.yaml` (Renovate automates this).\n\n**Rationale**: Repo-per-service gives teams autonomy, independent release cycles, and clear ownership. The shared base chart enforces platform standards without coupling deployments. The GitOps repo is the single source of truth for cluster state.",
  "new_string": "**Base Chart Library (platform org) + Per-Service Chart (in each service repo) + GitOps Repo (value overrides)**\n\nThree-layer separation matching the multi-org, repo-per-service model:\n\n```\nOrg: konecta-ix-platform\nRepo: kix-platform-modules/helm/base-service-chart/\n├── Chart.yaml                       # type: library, version: semver\n└── templates/                       # Published to Artifact Registry as OCI chart\n    ├── _deployment.tpl\n    ├── _service.tpl\n    ├── _ingress.tpl\n    ├── _hpa.tpl\n    ├── _pdb.tpl\n    ├── _networkpolicy.tpl\n    ├── _serviceaccount.tpl\n    └── _external-secret.tpl\n\nOrg: konecta-ix-mcp-servers (or any service org)\nRepo: kix-mcp-gateway/              # Each service repo\n├── src/                             # Application source code\n├── Dockerfile\n├── chart/                           # Service-specific Helm chart\n│   ├── Chart.yaml                   # depends on base-service-chart (OCI reference)\n│   ├── values.yaml                  # Service defaults (port, probes, resources)\n│   └── templates/                   # Only if service has custom K8s resources\n└── .github/workflows/\n    └── ci.yaml                      # Calls reusable workflows from kix-platform-ci-workflows\n\nOrg: konecta-ix-platform\nRepo: kix-platform-infrastructure/   # ArgoCD watches this repo only\n├── argocd/\n│   └── applicationset.yaml          # Matrix generator: env × service\n└── environments/\n    ├── dev/kix-mcp-gateway.yaml     # Overrides: image.tag, replicas, env-specific config\n    ├── qa/kix-mcp-gateway.yaml\n    └── prod/kix-mcp-gateway.yaml\n```\n\n**Flow**: Service repo CI builds image → dispatches to `kix-platform-infrastructure` → updates `environments/dev/{service}.yaml` with new tag → ArgoCD syncs.\n\n**Base chart versioning**: Published to Artifact Registry as OCI Helm chart. Service charts pin to a specific version (`~0.5.0`). Base chart updates are rolled out by bumping the version in each service's `Chart.yaml` (Renovate automates this across all orgs).\n\n**Cross-org access**: The shared CI workflows in `konecta-ix-platform` are accessible to all other orgs via GitHub Enterprise's internal visibility setting. A GitHub App installed across all orgs handles cross-repo dispatch to the gitops repo.\n\n**Rationale**: Repo-per-service with org-level boundaries gives teams autonomy and clear ownership. The platform org owns the standards (base chart, CI workflows, gitops state). Service teams own their code and Helm values."
}
```

All logic lives in the shared workflows repo. Updates to the pipeline roll out to all services by bumping the `@v1` tag.",
  "new_string": "Every service repo follows the **same pipeline** by calling reusable workflows from `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows`. A service onboards by adding a single `ci.yaml` file.\n\n**Shared Workflows Repo (`konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows/.github/workflows/`)**:\n\n```\nkix-platform-ci-workflows/\n└── .github/workflows/\n    ├── standard-pipeline.yaml      # Main orchestrator (called by every service)\n    ├── _build-image.yaml           # Reusable: Docker build + BuildKit cache\n    ├── _scan-and-sign.yaml         # Reusable: Trivy + SBOM (Syft) + Cosign sign\n    ├── _push-registry.yaml         # Reusable: Push to Artifact Registry\n    ├── _update-gitops.yaml         # Reusable: Dispatch to kix-platform-infrastructure\n    ├── _promote.yaml               # Reusable: Promote image tag between environments\n    └── _lint-and-test.yaml         # Reusable: Language-aware lint + unit tests\n```\n\n**Standard Service Repo Pipeline (every service copies this)**:\n\n```yaml\n# .github/workflows/ci.yaml (in each service repo — identical across all repos)\nname: CI\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n  workflow_dispatch:\n\njobs:\n  pipeline:\n    uses: konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows/.github/workflows/standard-pipeline.yaml@v1\n    with:\n      service-name: ${{ github.event.repository.name }}\n      dockerfile-path: ./Dockerfile\n      chart-path: ./chart\n      python-version: \"3.11\"     # or node-version: \"20\" for Node.js services\n    secrets: inherit\n```\n\n**That's it.** Every service repo has one small workflow file. All logic lives in the shared workflows repo. Updates to the pipeline roll out to all services by bumping the `@v1` tag.\n\n**Cross-org workflow sharing**: GitHub Enterprise allows sharing workflows across organizations. The `kix-platform-ci-workflows` repo is set to **internal** visibility, making it callable from `konecta-ix-mcp-servers`, `konecta-ix-apps`, `konecta-ix-workloads`, etc."
}

Engineer triggers `promote` workflow on the service repo (or directly on kix-platform-infrastructure)\n2.   "new_string": "### 8.9 Onboarding a New Service\n\nAdding a new service to the platform:\n\n1. **Create repo** in the appropriate org from `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-service-template`:\n   - Standard `Dockerfile` (Python or Node.js variant)\n   - Standard `chart/` directory with `Chart.yaml` depending on base chart\n   - Standard `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` calling shared pipeline\n   - Standard `.dockerignore`, `CODEOWNERS`\n2. **Add to gitops repo**: Create `environments/{dev,qa,prod}/{service-name}.yaml` in `kix-platform-infrastructure`\n3. **ArgoCD auto-discovers** via ApplicationSet git directory generator\n4. **First push** triggers the standard pipeline — fully operational\n\nNo pipeline code to write. > AGENT

The blueprint is complete at `KIX_MIGRATION_BLUEPRINT.md` (833 lines). Here's a summary of what's covered:

**Document**: `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/KIX_MIGRATION_BLUEPRINT.md`

### What it covers (12 sections):

1. **Executive Summary** — 24 in-scope services (CrewAI, infra repos, config/proxies excluded)

2. **Current State** — Inventories the existing Cloud Run, GKE, Cloud Build, and Terraform setup

3. **Target Architecture** — Component diagram showing GKE cluster with Kong ingress, 3 namespace domains (`api-services`, `mcp-servers`, `web-apps`) × 3 environments + shared namespaces. Full repo model mapped to your GitHub Enterprise orgs (`konecta-ix-platform`, `konecta-ix-mcp-servers`, `konecta-ix-apps`, `konecta-ix-workloads`, `konecta-ix-demos`)

4. **Service Tiering** — 3 tiers (critical/important/standard) for migration ordering

5. **Migration Strategy** — 4 phases: Foundation & Pilot → MCP Servers → Data Pipelines & Bot → Business Apps & Cutover. Dual-stack with DNS rollback.

6. **Kubernetes & Helm Design** — Base chart library in `kix-platform-modules`, per-service charts in each repo, GitOps values in `kix-platform-infrastructure`. Namespace strategy, resource profiles, health probes, HPA, ingress via Kong, ArgoCD ApplicationSet.

7. **Secure Docker Builds** — Multi-stage patterns for Python and Node.js, distroless runtimes, non-root users, image size targets, OCI labels, pinned digests.

8. **CI/CD Pipeline** — **Standard pipeline** that every service uses via a single `ci.yaml` file calling reusable workflows from `kix-platform-ci-workflows`. Cross-org dispatch to gitops repo. Environment promotion (dev auto-sync → QA manual → prod manual with change windows). Automated rollback. GitHub OIDC (no long-lived credentials). New service onboarding in 4 steps.

9. **Security** — Supply chain (Trivy, SBOM, Cosign signing), runtime (Pod Security Standards `restricted`, drop all caps, read-only root), secrets (External Secrets Operator + GCP Secret Manager + Workload Identity), network (default deny, Cloud Armor WAF), RBAC, Kyverno admission policies.

10. **Observability** — Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger, OTel Collector, key alerts.

11. **Cost & DR** — Cloud Run vs GKE cost model, multi-region failover.

12. **Risk Register** — 8 risks with mitigations.

   377→### 6.8 ArgoCD ApplicationSet Strategy
   378→
   379→**Git Directory Generator on kix-platform-infrastructure**
   380→
   381→ArgoCD watches `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure`. Each file in `environments/{env}/` defines an application.
   382→
   383→- ApplicationSet scans `environments/dev/`, `environments/qa/`, `environments/prod/` directories
   384→- Each `{service}.yaml` file contains the Helm source repo URL (across any org), chart path, and environment values
   385→- ArgoCD renders the Helm chart from the **service repo** using values from **kix-platform-infrastructure**
   386→- **Dev**: `syncPolicy.automated` (auto-sync on commit to kix-platform-infrastructure)
   387→- **QA**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, business-hours window)
   388→- **Prod**: `syncPolicy.automated: false` (manual sync, Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)
   389→- ArgoCD Projects for RBAC: `dev-project`, `qa-project`, `prod-project`
   390→- Sync waves: infrastructure resources (wave 0) → application deployments (wave 10)
   391→- New services are auto-discovered when their files are added to `environments/` directories
   392→
   393→---

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "### 6.7 Ingress Architecture\n\n```\nInternet → GCP LB (TLS termination) → Kong Ingress Controller → Application Services\n```\n\n- **External services**: Kong Ingress with `konghq.com/plugins` annotations (rate-limiting, CORS, auth)\n- **Internal services**: ClusterIP only, accessed via `service.namespace.svc.cluster.local`\n- **TLS**: cert-manager + Let's Encrypt (ClusterIssuer) for automated certificate management\n- **DNS per environment**: `dev.api.konecta.io`, `qa.api.konecta.io`, `api.konecta.io`",
  "new_string": "### 6.7 Ingress Architecture (Dual Load Balancer)\n\nTwo separate ingress paths with independent load balancers:\n\n**Path 1 — API / Backend Ingress (Kong)**\n```\nInternet → GCP External LB + Cloud Armor (WAF)\n         → Kong Ingress Controller (shared-kong)\n         → {env}-api-services, {env}-mcp-servers\n```\n- Kong handles API routing, rate limiting, authentication plugins, CORS\n- `konghq.com/plugins` annotations per Ingress resource\n- Cloud Armor OWASP top-10 WAF rules on the LB\n\n**Path 2 — Frontend Ingress (GKE Gateway API + IAP)**\n```\nInternet → GCP External LB + Cloud Armor (WAF) + GKE Gateway API\n         → IAP (Identity-Aware Proxy) / IAM authentication\n         → {env}-web-apps (Nuxt 3 frontends)\n```\n- **GKE Gateway API** (`gateway.networking.k8s.io`): Native GKE ingress using `Gateway` and `HTTPRoute` resources\n- **IAP (Identity-Aware Proxy)**: Google-managed identity layer — authenticates users via Google/OIDC before traffic reaches the pod. Configured via `BackendConfig` with IAP enabled.\n- **Cloud Armor**: WAF policies applied at the LB level (same OWASP rules as API path)\n- **IAM**: Access controlled by IAM policies on the IAP resource — only authorized users/groups can reach the frontend\n- No Kong involvement for frontend traffic\n\n**Shared Configuration**\n- **Internal services**: ClusterIP only, accessed via `service.namespace.svc.cluster.local`\n- **TLS**: cert-manager + Let's Encrypt for both paths (separate certificates per domain)\n- **DNS per environment**:\n  - API: `dev.api.konecta.io`, `qa.api.konecta.io`, `api.konecta.io`\n  - Frontend: `dev.app.konecta.io`, `qa.app.konecta.io`, `app.konecta.io`"
}

   638→## 9. Security Architecture
   639→
   640→### 9.1 Container Image Security
   641→
   642→- **Multi-stage builds**: Builder dependencies never ship in runtime image
   643→- **Base image policy**: Pinned digests, updated monthly, slim/distroless only
   644→- **Vulnerability scanning**: Trivy pre-push, CRITICAL/HIGH block deployment
   645→- **Image signing**: Cosign keyless (OIDC) — every image cryptographically signed
   646→- **SBOM**: Generated for every build, stored as OCI annotation
   647→- **Registry**: Artifact Registry with vulnerability scanning enabled, IAM-restricted access

> AGENT

Done. The blueprint now reflects:

- **Separate frontend LB**: `{env}-web-apps` namespaces are fronted by their own GCP External Load Balancer using **GKE Gateway API** + **IAP (Identity-Aware Proxy)** for IAM-based user authentication + **Cloud Armor** WAF
- **Separate API LB**: `{env}-api-services` and `{env}-mcp-servers` continue through the existing path: GCP LB + Cloud Armor → Kong Ingress Controller
- The architecture diagram, ingress section (6.7), and network security section (9.5) all reflect this dual-ingress design

The key security benefit: frontend users must authenticate through Google IAP/IAM before any request reaches the Nuxt pods, while API traffic is managed through Kong's plugin-based auth model.

   371→### 6.6 Service Mesh
   372→
   373→**Decision: No (initially).** Kong handles ingress traffic management, auth, and rate limiting. Introducing Istio/Linkerd during migration adds unnecessary risk. Revisit post-migration if mTLS between services or advanced traffic management is required.
   374→
   375→### 6.7 Ingress Architecture (Dual Load Balancer)
   376→
   377→Two separate ingress paths with independent load balancers:
   378→
   379→**Path 1 — API / Backend Ingress (Kong)**
   380→```
   381→Internet → GCP External LB + Cloud Armor (WAF)
   382→         → Kong Ingress Controller (shared-kong)
   383→         → {env}-api-services, {env}-mcp-servers
   384→```
   385→- Kong handles API routing, rate limiting, authentication plugins, CORS
   386→- `konghq.com/plugins` annotations per Ingress resource
   387→- Cloud Armor OWASP top-10 WAF rules on the LB
   388→
   389→**Path 2 — Frontend Ingress (GKE Gateway API + IAP)**
   390→```
   391→Internet → GCP External LB + Cloud Armor (WAF) + GKE Gateway API
   392→         → IAP (Identity-Aware Proxy) / IAM authentication
   393→         → {env}-web-apps (Nuxt 3 frontends)
   394→```
   395→- **GKE Gateway API** (`gateway.networking.k8s.io`): Native GKE ingress using `Gateway` and `HTTPRoute` resources
   396→- **IAP (Identity-Aware Proxy)**: Google-managed identity layer — authenticates users via Google/OIDC before traffic reaches the pod. Configured via `BackendConfig` with IAP enabled.
   397→- **Cloud Armor**: WAF policies applied at the LB level (same OWASP rules as API path)
   398→- **IAM**: Access controlled by IAM policies on the IAP resource — only authorized users/groups can reach the frontend
   399→- No Kong involvement for frontend traffic
   400→
   401→**Shared Configuration**
   402→- **Internal services**: ClusterIP only, accessed via `service.namespace.svc.cluster.local`
   403→- **TLS**: cert-manager + Let's Encrypt for both paths (separate certificates per domain)
   404→- **DNS per environment**:
   405→  - API: `dev.api.konecta.io`, `qa.api.konecta.io`, `api.konecta.io`
   406→  - Frontend: `dev.app.konecta.io`, `qa.app.konecta.io`, `app.konecta.io`
   407→
   408→### 6.8 ArgoCD ApplicationSet Strategy
   409→

  "new_string": "**Path 1 — API / Backend Ingress (Kong)**\n```\nInternet → GCP External LB + Cloud Armor (WAF)\n         → Kong Ingress Controller (shared-kong)\n         → {env}-api-services, {env}-mcp-servers\n```\n- Kong handles API routing, rate limiting, authentication plugins, CORS\n- `konghq.com/plugins` annotations per Ingress resource\n- Cloud Armor OWASP top-10 WAF rules on the LB\n\n**Internal — LiteLLM via Kong (all LLM traffic)**\n```\nAny service pod → Kong (internal route) → shared-litellm (LiteLLM proxy) → Vertex AI / LLM providers\n```\n- LiteLLM sits behind Kong as an internal-only Kong route (no external exposure)\n- **All services** that need LLM access call Kong's internal endpoint (e.g. `kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/llm/...`), which routes to LiteLLM\n- Kong applies rate limiting, auth, and observability plugins on LLM traffic\n- Benefits: centralized LLM access control, usage metering, consistent auth, audit trail\n- NetworkPolicy on `shared-litellm` namespace: allow ingress **only from Kong**, deny all direct access"
}

No dual-stack or traffic cutover needed.\n\n**Phase 1 — Platform Foundation**\n- Create `kix-platform-ci-workflows` repo in `konecta-ix-platform` with standard pipeline\n- Add base Helm chart to `kix-platform-modules/helm/base-service-chart/`, publish to Artifact Registry\n- Configure ArgoCD ApplicationSets in `kix-platform-infrastructure`\n- Create `kix-platform-service-template` template repo\n- Install GitHub App across all orgs for cross-org dispatch\n- Configure GitHub OIDC → GCP Workload Identity Pool (per org)\n- Deploy Kong + LiteLLM (behind Kong) on kix-infrastructure\n- Validate standard pipeline end-to-end with 1 pilot MCP server\n\n**Phase 2 — MCP Servers (14 services)**\n- Migrate all MCP servers to kix-infrastructure using standard pipeline\n- Deploy kix-mcp-gateway first, then remaining servers\n- Each service gets a new domain (e.g., `mcp-gateway.dev.konecta.io`)\n- Decommission corresponding Cloud Run services as each migrates\n\n**Phase 3 — Business Apps & Frontends (6 services)**\n- Migrate business apps (kix-app-hrfraud, kix-usecase-iq-portal, kix-app-hrportal)\n- Frontend apps deploy behind GKE Gateway API + IAP on new domains\n- Migrate remaining: kix-usecase-testiq, kix-demo-prototypes\n\n**Phase 4 — Data Pipelines & Cleanup (4 services)**\n- Migrate RAG pipelines (kix-usecase-rag-ingestion, kix-usecase-rag-googledrive)\n- Migrate bot (kix-demo-porfolioguru) to K8s Deployment\n- Tear down all Cloud Run services\n- Remove Cloud Build configurations\n\n### Stateless Services\n- **All application services are stateless** — state lives in Cloud SQL, Firestore, GCS\n- Container images contain no data; configuration via ConfigMaps/Secrets\n- No persistent volumes needed for application pods\n- Temporary storage (`emptyDir`) for: `/tmp`, ML model caches, video processing scratch space"

   310→**Cross-org access**: The shared CI workflows in `konecta-ix-platform` are accessible to all other orgs via GitHub Enterprise's internal visibility setting. A GitHub App installed across all orgs handles cross-repo dispatch to the gitops repo.

   312→**Rationale**: Repo-per-service with org-level boundaries gives teams autonomy and clear ownership. The platform org owns the standards (base chart, CI workflows, gitops state). Service teams own their code and Helm values.
   313→
   314→### 6.2 Namespace Strategy
   315→
   316→**Environment × Domain Boundaries (14 namespaces total)**
   317→
   318→| Namespace Pattern | Contents |
   319→|-------------------|----------|
   320→| `{env}-api-services` | FastAPI backends, business apps, bots, data pipelines |
   321→| `{env}-mcp-servers` | MCP protocol servers |
   322→| `{env}-web-apps` | Nuxt 3 frontends |
   323→| `shared-kong` | Kong API Gateway (routes to APIs, MCP servers, and LiteLLM) |
   324→| `shared-litellm` | LiteLLM proxy (accessible only via Kong, not directly) |
   325→| `shared-argocd` | ArgoCD (existing) |
   326→| `shared-monitoring` | Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger |
   327→| `shared-cert-manager` | TLS certificate management |
   328→
   329→Each namespace gets: ResourceQuotas, LimitRanges, default NetworkPolicies (deny-all ingress), RBAC bindings.
   330→
   331→### 6.3 Resource Profiles
   332→
   333→| Profile | CPU Request | CPU Limit | Mem Request | Mem Limit | Use For |
   334→|---------|------------|-----------|-------------|-----------|---------|
   335→| **API Service** | 100m | 500m | 256Mi | 512Mi | FastAPI APIs, MCP servers, bots, data pipelines |
   336→| **API Heavy** | 250m | 1000m | 512Mi | 1Gi | RAG ingestion, document processing, services with ML deps |
   337→| **Frontend** | 50m | 200m | 128Mi | 256Mi | Nuxt 3 SSR |
   338→
   339→### 6.4 Health Probes
   340→
   341→| Service Type | Liveness | Readiness | Startup |
   342→|-------------|----------|-----------|---------|
   343→| FastAPI | `GET /healthz` 8000 | `GET /readyz` 8000 (checks DB) | N/A |
   344→| MCP Server (SSE) | `GET /healthz` 8000, period=30s | `GET /readyz` 8000, initial=30s | N/A |
   345→| Nuxt 3 | `GET /` 3000 | `GET /_health` 3000 | N/A |
   346→| Heavy services | `GET /healthz` 8000 | `GET /readyz` 8000 | failureThreshold=18 (3min) |
   347→
   348→### 6.5 Autoscaling (HPA)
   349→
   350→| Tier | Min | Max | Target CPU | Scale-Down Window |
   351→|------|-----|-----|------------|-------------------|
   352→| User-facing (frontends, public APIs) | 2 | 20 | 60% | 5 min |
   353→| Internal APIs, MCP servers | 2 | 10 | 75% | 10 min |
   354→| Data pipelines, bots | 1 | 5 | 80% | 15 min |

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "- Sync waves: infrastructure resources (wave 0) → application deployments (wave 10)\n- New services are auto-discovered when their files are added to `environments/` directories\n\n---\n\n## 7. Secure Docker Builds",
  "new_string": "- Sync waves: infrastructure resources (wave 0) → application deployments (wave 10)\n- New services are auto-discovered when their files are added to `environments/` directories\n\n### 6.9 Cloud SQL Auth Proxy Sidecar\n\nServices that connect to Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL) use the **Cloud SQL Auth Proxy** as a sidecar container. This is baked into the base Helm chart as an opt-in pattern.\n\n**Why sidecar (not shared proxy)**:\n- Each pod gets its own authenticated connection to Cloud SQL\n- Workload Identity per service account — least-privilege IAM per service\n- No shared proxy bottleneck; scales with the application pods\n- Google-recommended pattern for GKE\n\n**How it works**:\n```\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  Pod                                     │\n│  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  │\n│  │  App          │  │  cloud-sql-proxy │  │\n│  │  Container    │──│  sidecar         │──│──▶ Cloud SQL (private IP)\n│  │  (FastAPI)    │  │  (port 5432)     │  │\n│  └──────────────┘  └──────────────────┘  │\n│  localhost:5432                           │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n- App connects to `localhost:5432` — no connection string changes needed\n- Sidecar handles IAM authentication via Workload Identity (no passwords in env vars)\n- Private IP connectivity to Cloud SQL (no public IP exposure)\n\n**Base chart integration** (opt-in via Helm values):\n```yaml\n# In service values.yaml — only services needing Cloud SQL enable this\ncloudSqlProxy:\n  enabled: true\n  instanceConnectionName: \"project:region:instance\"\n  port: 5432\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: 50m\n      memory: 64Mi\n    limits:\n      cpu: 200m\n      memory: 128Mi\n```\n\nWhen `cloudSqlProxy.enabled: true`, the base chart template injects:\n- A `cloud-sql-proxy` sidecar container (`gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2`)\n- `--private-ip` flag (uses VPC-native private IP, not public)\n- `--auto-iam-authn` flag (Workload Identity, no password needed)\n- The service account annotation for Workload Identity must have `roles/cloudsql.client` IAM role\n\n**Services requiring Cloud SQL sidecar**:\n| Service | Database |\n|---------|----------|\n| kix-app-hrfraud | PostgreSQL (HR fraud data) |\n| kix-usecase-iq-portal | PostgreSQL (IQ portal data) |\n\n**Connection string pattern** (app-side):\n```\nDATABASE_URL=postgresql+asyncpg://user@localhost:5432/dbname\n```\nNo password in the connection string when using `--auto-iam-authn`. The proxy handles authentication via the pod's Workload Identity.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Secure Docker Builds"
}
```

Done. Here's a summary of all the changes made in this round:

**LiteLLM behind Kong**:
- Architecture diagram updated — LiteLLM shown inside Kong's routing scope, not as a standalone peer
- Ingress section (6.7) adds an **internal route** pattern: services → Kong → LiteLLM → LLM providers
- NetworkPolicy on `shared-litellm`: ingress only from Kong, deny all direct access
- Namespace table clarifies LiteLLM is "accessible only via Kong"

**No production / new domains**:
- Removed all dual-stack, DNS cutover, and downtime mitigation language
- Tiering replaced with simple **migration priority** (P1-P4) — platform core first, demos last
- Migration phases simplified — services migrate directly to new domains, Cloud Run decommissioned per service
- Risk register cleaned up — removed downtime risk, added LiteLLM routing risk

**Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar** (new section 6.9):
- Opt-in sidecar pattern baked into the base Helm chart via `cloudSqlProxy.enabled: true`
- Uses `cloud-sql-proxy:2` with `--private-ip` and `--auto-iam-authn` (Workload Identity, no passwords)
- App connects to `localhost:5432` — no connection string changes
- Lists services requiring it (kix-app-hrfraud, kix-usecase-iq-portal)
- Includes resource profile for the sidecar container

> DEVELOPER

for two tiered apps like hrfraud, whats the best pattern for deploying two workloads in a single pod with proper interconnected dependacies rather than sending lots externally on cloudrun

> AGENT

Good question. There are two patterns worth considering here, and they have different trade-offs. Let me lay them out before updating the blueprint.

**Option A — Multi-container pod (sidecar pattern)**
```
Pod: kix-app-hrfraud
├── container: backend (FastAPI :8000)
├── container: frontend (Nuxt :3000, calls localhost:8000)
└── container: cloud-sql-proxy (sidecar :5432)
```
- Frontend calls backend on `localhost:8000` — zero network hop
- Atomic deploy (both update together or not at all)
- Shared lifecycle, shared Workload Identity
- **Downside**: Can't scale frontend and backend independently, different resource profiles forced into one pod, if one container OOMs the whole pod restarts

**Option B — Separate Deployments, same namespace (ClusterIP)**
```
Deployment: kix-app-hrfraud-backend  → Service: backend.{env}-api-services.svc.cluster.local:8000
Deployment: kix-app-hrfraud-frontend → calls backend via ClusterIP DNS (never leaves cluster)
```
- Traffic stays cluster-internal (not external like Cloud Run — sub-millisecond latency within same node, single-digit ms across nodes)
- Independent scaling, independent rollbacks
- **Downside**: Two deployments to manage, version skew possible during rolling updates

For HR Fraud and IQ Portal, I'd actually recommend **Option A (multi-container pod)** because:
- The frontend is tightly coupled to the backend (same release cycle, same API contract)
- Atomic deploys eliminate version skew — the frontend and backend are always compatible
- `localhost` communication is simpler than managing internal DNS and service discovery
- These aren't high-scale services that need independent scaling
- The Cloud SQL proxy sidecar is already in the pod, so you'd have 3 containers total

Let me add this as a pattern in the blueprint.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/KIX_MIGRATION_BLUEPRINT.md",
  "old_string": "No password in the connection string when using `--auto-iam-authn`. The proxy handles authentication via the pod's Workload Identity.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Secure Docker Builds",
  "new_string": "No password in the connection string when using `--auto-iam-authn`. The proxy handles authentication via the pod's Workload Identity.\n\n### 6.10 Multi-Tier App Pattern (Frontend + Backend in Single Pod)\n\nTwo-tiered apps like kix-app-hrfraud and kix-usecase-iq-portal (Nuxt frontend + FastAPI backend) deploy as a **multi-container pod** rather than separate Deployments. This eliminates the inter-service network hops that made Cloud Run deployments slow and complex.\n\n**Pod layout**:\n```\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  Pod: kix-app-hrfraud                                            │\n│                                                                  │\n│  ┌──────────────┐   localhost:8000   ┌──────────────────────┐    │\n│  │  frontend     │ ─────────────────▶│  backend              │    │\n│  │  (Nuxt SSR)   │                   │  (FastAPI)            │    │\n│  │  :3000        │                   │  :8000                │    │\n│  └──────────────┘                   └──────────┬───────────┘    │\n│                                                 │ localhost:5432 │\n│                                      ┌──────────▼───────────┐    │\n│                                      │  cloud-sql-proxy     │    │\n│                                      │  (sidecar)           │────│──▶ Cloud SQL\n│                                      │  :5432               │    │\n│                                      └──────────────────────┘    │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n         ▲                    ▲\n         │                    │\n    Frontend LB          API LB (Kong)\n  (Gateway API + IAP)   (internal routes)\n```\n\n**Why single pod (not separate Deployments)**:\n- **`localhost` communication**: Frontend calls backend on `localhost:8000` — zero network hop, no DNS resolution, no Service objects needed for the backend\n- **Atomic deploys**: Frontend and backend always deploy together — eliminates API version skew during rolling updates\n- **Shared lifecycle**: Same release cycle, same repo, same Helm chart — these are tightly coupled components, not independent microservices\n- **Simpler networking**: Only the frontend port (3000) and optionally backend port (8000) need to be exposed via K8s Services; inter-container traffic never touches the cluster network\n- **Fewer moving parts**: One Deployment, one HPA, one PodDisruptionBudget, one set of health probes\n\n**Helm values pattern** (opt-in via base chart):\n```yaml\n# values.yaml for kix-app-hrfraud\ncontainers:\n  backend:\n    image:\n      repository: eu-docker.pkg.dev/project/kix-services/kix-app-hrfraud-backend\n      tag: sha-abc1234\n    port: 8000\n    resources:\n      requests: { cpu: 100m, memory: 256Mi }\n      limits: { cpu: 500m, memory: 512Mi }\n    probes:\n      liveness: { path: /healthz, port: 8000 }\n      readiness: { path: /readyz, port: 8000 }\n    env:\n      DATABASE_URL: \"postgresql+asyncpg://user@localhost:5432/dbname\"\n      LITELLM_ENDPOINT: \"http://kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/llm\"\n\n  frontend:\n    image:\n      repository: eu-docker.pkg.dev/project/kix-services/kix-app-hrfraud-frontend\n      tag: sha-def5678\n    port: 3000\n    resources:\n      requests: { cpu: 50m, memory: 128Mi }\n      limits: { cpu: 200m, memory: 256Mi }\n    probes:\n      liveness: { path: /, port: 3000 }\n      readiness: { path: /_health, port: 3000 }\n    env:\n      API_BASE: \"http://localhost:8000\"    # ← backend via localhost\n\ncloudSqlProxy:\n  enabled: true\n  instanceConnectionName: \"project:eu-west1:hrfraud-db\"\n  port: 5432\n\n# Two K8s Services expose the pod on different ingress paths\nservices:\n  api:\n    port: 8000\n    targetPort: 8000\n    ingress:\n      class: kong                           # ← API LB\n      path: /api/hrfraud\n  frontend:\n    port: 3000\n    targetPort: 3000\n    ingress:\n      class: gateway-api                    # ← Frontend LB (Gateway API + IAP)\n      host: hrfraud.app.konecta.io\n```\n\n**Dual ingress on a single pod**: Two K8s Service objects point at the same pod on different ports. The API Service routes through Kong; the frontend Service routes through the GKE Gateway API + IAP. Both LBs reach the same pod, but through different ports and auth models.\n\n**CI pipeline for multi-tier apps**: The standard pipeline builds **two images** (backend + frontend) from the same repo. Both image tags are updated atomically in the gitops values file:\n```\nci.yaml triggers:\n  1. Build backend image → sha-abc1234\n  2. Build frontend image → sha-def5678\n  3. Dispatch to gitops: update both tags in one commit\n  4. ArgoCD syncs → pod rolls with both new images\n```\n\n**When NOT to use this pattern**:\n- Services that need independent scaling (e.g., API serving 10x more traffic than its frontend)\n- Services with very different resource profiles where one container would waste resources\n- Loosely coupled services that release on different cadences\n- Services where the frontend is a static SPA (no SSR) — serve from CDN instead\n\n**Apps using this pattern**:\n| App | Containers | Ports |\n|-----|-----------|-------|\n| kix-usecase-iq-portal | Nuxt frontend (3000) + FastAPI backend (8000) + cloud-sql-proxy (5432) | 3 containers |\n| kix-app-hrfraud | Nuxt frontend (3000) + FastAPI backend (8000) + cloud-sql-proxy (5432) | 3 containers |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Secure Docker Builds"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. Section 6.10 now defines the multi-tier app pattern. The key points:

**Single pod, 3 containers**: Nuxt frontend + FastAPI backend + Cloud SQL proxy — all communicate via `localhost`. No cluster network traffic between frontend and backend.

**Dual ingress on one pod**: Two K8s Services point at the same pod on different ports. Kong routes API traffic to `:8000`, GKE Gateway API + IAP routes frontend traffic to `:3000`. Different auth models, same pod.

**Atomic deploys**: The CI pipeline builds both images from the same repo and updates both tags in a single gitops commit. ArgoCD rolls the pod with both new images at once — no version skew possible.

**vs Cloud Run**: On Cloud Run, frontend→backend calls went through the external load balancer and back. In this pattern they're `localhost:8000` — zero network hop.

The pattern is opt-in in the base chart. Apps that don't need it (single-tier MCP servers, standalone APIs) just use the standard single-container config.

> AGENT

No. HR Fraud uses PostgreSQL but runs it as a **local containerized instance** (`postgres:16-alpine` in docker-compose). There's no Cloud SQL reference anywhere in the codebase — `db_host` defaults to `localhost` and the docker-compose wires it to a local container.

That said, the question is whether you *want* it on Cloud SQL when it moves to kix-infrastructure, or whether it should run as a PostgreSQL pod in-cluster. Let me update the blueprint to be accurate about the current state.
