# DevSecOps Policy & Standards - Complete Handbook Extract

## 1. INTRODUCTION & PRINCIPLES

**Purpose**: Establishes comprehensive DevSecOps standards for software development teams across all cloud providers (AWS, Azure, GCP, on-premises).

**Core Principles**:
- Security by Default
- Shift Left (identify issues early)
- Automation First
- Continuous Improvement
- Cloud Agnostic approach
- Toolchain Consistency
- Fail Fast (pipeline failures stop immediately)

**Scope**: Applies to all development teams, applications, and infrastructure regardless of cloud provider or whether using Konecta global DevSecOps toolchain or customer-provided toolchain.

---

## 2. APPROVED TOOLCHAIN

| Category | Approved Tools | Purpose |
|----------|---|---|
| IDE | Visual Studio Code | Development environment |
| Code Management | Bitbucket, GitHub | Version control |
| Branching | Git Flow | Standardized model |
| CI/CD | Bitbucket Pipelines, GitHub Actions | Pipeline automation |
| Developer Platform | Backstage | Service catalog |
| Infrastructure as Code | Terraform | Cloud provisioning |
| Configuration Management | Ansible | OS hardening, config |
| Secret Management | HashiCorp Vault | Centralized storage |
| Container Security | Trivy, Aqua | Image scanning |
| Image Signing | Cosign (Sigstore) | Binary verification |
| SBOM Generation | Syft | Bill of Materials |
| Dependency Analysis | Dependency-Track | SCA |
| Code Quality | SonarQube | Static analysis |
| Unit Testing | JUnit | Developer testing |
| E2E Testing | Cypress | End-to-end testing |
| API Testing | Postman | Functional testing |
| Security Testing | OWASP ZAP | DAST |
| Performance Testing | Apache JMeter | Load testing |
| Artifact Registry | Cloud-native Registry | Container storage |
| Observability | Grafana, Prometheus | Metrics visualization |
| Notifications | Google Chat, Email | Team communications |

---

## 3. PIPELINE ARCHITECTURE

### Code Pipeline Flow

**Sequence** (failure at any stage stops pipeline):

1. **Lint & Validate** (ESLint, Prettier)
2. **Secret Scan** (Trivy)
3. **SCA** (Dependency-Track)
4. **SAST** (SonarQube)
5. **Build & Unit Test** (Maven/Gradle, JUnit)
6. **Container Build** (Docker)
7. **Container Scanning** (Trivy, Aqua)
8. **Sign Image & SBOM** (Cosign, Syft)
9. **E2E & DAST Testing** (Cypress, Postman, OWASP ZAP)
10. **Monitor** (Grafana, Prometheus)

### Infrastructure Pipeline Flow

1. **Lint** (tflint)
2. **Policy Check** (OPA)
3. **Test** (terratest)
4. **Deploy** (Terraform)

### Environment Deployment Flow

Development → Pre-production → Production

---

## 4. SECURITY SCANNING & QUALITY GATES

### SAST (Static Application Security Testing)
- **Tool**: SonarQube
- **Frequency**: Every commit via CI/CD
- **Quality Gate**: No critical or high severity issues allowed
- **Remediation SLA**: 
  - Critical: 24 hours
  - High: 7 days
  - Medium: 30 days

### SCA (Software Composition Analysis)
- **Tool**: Dependency-Track
- **SBOM Source**: Syft-generated CycloneDX SBOMs
- **Scan Frequency**: Daily + on commit
- **Remediation SLA** (same as SAST)

### Pipeline Security Gates

| Gate | Tool | Stage | Action |
|------|------|-------|--------|
| Secret Detection | Trivy | Pre-build | Stop pipeline |
| Dependency Vulnerabilities | Dependency-Track | Pre-build | Stop pipeline |
| SAST Quality Gate | SonarQube | Pre-build | Stop pipeline |
| Container Vulnerability | Trivy | Post-build | Stop pipeline |
| Image Signature | Cosign | Post-build | Stop pipeline |
| DAST Baseline | OWASP ZAP | Post-deploy (pre-prod) | Block promotion |

### Vulnerability Remediation SLAs

| Severity | Detection to Triage | Triage to Fix | Fix to Deploy |
|----------|---|---|---|
| Critical | 4 hours | 24 hours | 48 hours |
| High | 24 hours | 7 days | 14 days |
| Medium | 7 days | 30 days | 45 days |
| Low | 30 days | 90 days | Next release |

### Automated Vulnerability Tracking

When Critical or Major vulnerabilities detected:
- Pipeline stops immediately
- Jira ticket auto-created with details
- Links to commit/PR that triggered scan
- Assigns based on component ownership rules

---

## 5. ZERO TRUST & ACCESS MANAGEMENT

### Core Principle
"Never trust, always verify. Every access request is authenticated, authorized, and continuously validated."

### Human Access Standards
- **MFA**: Mandatory for all human access
- **SSO**: Use organizational identity provider
- **RBAC**: Request roles, not direct permissions
- **Access Reviews**: Quarterly review with automatic expiration for temporary access

### Service Account Standards

**Preferred Approach: Workload Identity Federation**
- Leverage cloud-native workload identity (AWS IAM Roles for Service Accounts, GCP Workload Identity, Azure Managed Identity)
- Eliminate long-lived credential files
- Use OIDC for external systems (GitHub Actions, GitLab CI)

**Legacy/Fallback: Service Account Keys**
- Only when Workload Identity unavailable
- Generate temporary tokens, not permanent keys
- Mandatory automatic rotation (30-90 days maximum)
- Store in secret management systems, never in code

### Authorization Model
"Least Privilege = Minimum Permissions + Time-Limited + Regularly Reviewed"

**Examples**:
- ✅ Developer can read code, create PRs (not push to main)
- ✅ CI/CD can deploy to dev/staging (not production without approval)
- ✅ Service account can write to specific bucket (not entire project)
- ✅ GitHub Actions uses Workload Identity to deploy to GCP (no keys)
- ✅ Kubernetes pod uses service mesh identity for API calls
- ⚠️ Legacy app uses service account with key rotation enabled
- ❌ Admin access "just in case"
- ❌ Service account key in repository
- ❌ Same service account key shared across environments

### Environment Independence & Isolation

**Requirements**:
- Each environment (dev, staging, prod) isolated: separate cloud accounts/projects, network boundaries
- Configurable: environment-specific settings externalized
- Immutable: same artifact promoted across environments

**DO**:
- ✅ Externalize configuration (environment variables, ConfigMaps, parameter stores)
- ✅ Use same container image/binary across all environments
- ✅ Inject secrets at runtime from secure stores
- ✅ Use Terraform workspaces or Helm values for environment differences
- ✅ Tag artifacts with commit SHA and promote through pipeline

**DON'T**:
- ❌ Hardcode environment values in code
- ❌ Rebuild artifacts for each environment
- ❌ Share secrets between environments
- ❌ Use production credentials in non-prod environments

---

## 6. CODE CONTROL PRACTICES

### Branching Strategy: Git Flow (Mandatory)

Git Flow is the required branching model for all repositories.

---

## 7. SECRET MANAGEMENT

**Tool**: HashiCorp Vault

**Requirements**:
- Centralized secret storage
- Runtime injection at deployment
- No hardcoded secrets in code or configuration
- Automatic rotation policies
- Audit logging of secret access

---

## 8. CONTAINER & IMAGE SECURITY

### Container Scanning
- **Tools**: Trivy (secrets, vulnerabilities), Aqua (runtime protection)
- **Timing**: Post-build before promotion
- **Gate**: Critical/High vulnerabilities stop pipeline

### Image Signing & Integrity
- **Tool**: Cosign (Sigstore)
- **Requirement**: All images must be signed
- **Gate**: Unsigned images blocked from deployment

### SBOM Generation
- **Tool**: Syft
- **Format**: CycloneDX
- **Requirement**: Generated with every build
- **Tracking**: Stored with artifact metadata

---

## 9. TESTING REQUIREMENTS

### Unit Testing
- **Tool**: JUnit
- **Requirement**: Integrated into build stage
- **Standards**: Developer-level testing mandatory

### E2E Testing
- **Tool**: Cypress
- **Stage**: Post-deployment to pre-production
- **Requirement**: Baseline testing before production promotion

### API Testing
- **Tool**: Postman
- **Coverage**: Functional and integration testing
- **Automation**: Integrated into pipeline

### Performance Testing
- **Tool**: Apache JMeter
- **Requirement**: Load and performance validation
- **Threshold**: Must meet baseline performance criteria

### DAST (Dynamic Application Security Testing)
- **Tool**: OWASP ZAP
- **Stage**: Post-deployment to pre-production
- **Gate**: Baseline vulnerabilities block production promotion

---

## 10. DEPLOYMENT

### Environment Flow
Development → Pre-production → Production

### Artifact Promotion
- Same artifact/image promoted through environments
- No rebuilding per environment
- Configuration externalized and environment-specific

### Deployment Standards
- Infrastructure as Code via Terraform
- Configuration management via Ansible
- Immutable infrastructure approach

---

## 11. MONITORING & OBSERVABILITY

### Tools
- **Metrics**: Prometheus
- **Visualization**: Grafana
- **Logging**: [Specified in observability section]
- **Alerting**: Integrated with notifications (Google Chat, Email)

### Post-Deployment Monitoring
- Pipeline includes continuous monitoring stage
- Metrics collection and visualization mandatory
- Alert thresholds established for anomalies

---

## 12. TOOL CHAIN INSTANCES BY ENVIRONMENT

| Environment | Requirements | Justification |
|---|---|---|
| Development | Shared instance | Cost optimization, rapid iteration |
| Pre-production | Dedicated instance recommended | Production-like validation |
| Production | Dedicated instance required | Isolation, security, compliance |

---

## 13. ZERO TOUCH PROVISIONING (ZTP) LEVELS

- **Level 0**: Manual (no automation)
- **Level 1**: Scripted (basic scripts, some manual steps)
- **Level 2**: Automated (fully automated, minimal approval gates)
- **Level 3**: Self-Service (complete self-service with policy enforcement)

**Target**: Level 2 minimum for all tool chain components

---

## 14. POLICY FAILURE & ENFORCEMENT

**⚠️ Pipeline Failure Policy**: 
"Any stage failure will immediately halt the pipeline. No artifacts will be promoted to subsequent stages until all issues are resolved."

---

## 15. FUTURE UPDATES

The next handbook release will include comprehensive Generative AI (GenAI) policies covering:
- Safe and effective GenAI tool application in development
- Security considerations
- Data privacy requirements
- Code quality standards
- Integration into DevSecOps workflow

---

## KEY TAKEAWAYS

This handbook mandates a **shift-left security approach** with **automated gates** at every pipeline stage. All tools are **cloud-agnostic** and **interchangeable** (e.g., Bitbucket Pipelines or GitHub Actions). **Workload identity federation** is the preferred authentication mechanism, eliminating long-lived keys. **No artifacts bypass** security gates—pipeline stops on Critical/Major findings.
```

   575→
   576→**Dual ingress on a single pod**: Two K8s Service objects point at the same pod on different ports. The API Service routes through Kong; the frontend Service routes through the GKE Gateway API + IAP. Both LBs reach the same pod, but through different ports and auth models.
   577→
   578→**CI pipeline for multi-tier apps**: The standard pipeline builds **two images** (backend + frontend) from the same repo. Both image tags are updated atomically in the gitops values file:
   579→```
   580→ci.yaml triggers:
   581→  1. Build backend image → sha-abc1234
   582→  2. Build frontend image → sha-def5678
   583→  3. Dispatch to gitops: update both tags in one commit
   584→  4. ArgoCD syncs → pod rolls with both new images
   585→```
   586→
   587→**When NOT to use this pattern**:
   588→- Services that need independent scaling (e.g., API serving 10x more traffic than its frontend)
   589→- Services with very different resource profiles where one container would waste resources
   590→- Loosely coupled services that release on different cadences
   591→- Services where the frontend is a static SPA (no SSR) — serve from CDN instead
   592→
   593→**Apps using this pattern**:
   594→| App | Containers | Ports |
   595→|-----|-----------|-------|
   596→| kix-usecase-iq-portal | Nuxt frontend (3000) + FastAPI backend (8000) + cloud-sql-proxy (5432) | 3 containers |
   597→| kix-app-hrfraud | Nuxt frontend (3000) + FastAPI backend (8000) + cloud-sql-proxy (5432) | 3 containers |
   598→

   600→
   601→## 7. Secure Docker Builds
   602→
   603→### 7.1 Base Image Policy
   604→
   605→| Stack | Builder Stage | Runtime Stage |
   606→|-------|--------------|---------------|
   607→| Python (standard) | `python:3.x-slim` | `python:3.x-slim` |
   608→| Python (ML: opencv, whisper, ffmpeg) | `python:3.x-slim` + apt deps | `python:3.x-slim` + runtime-only libs |
   609→| Node.js | `node:20-alpine` | `gcr.io/distroless/nodejs20-debian12` |
   610→
   611→- Pin to **full digest** in CI (updated monthly by Renovate/Dependabot)
   612→- Parameterize Python version via `ARG PYTHON_VERSION=3.11`
   613→- **Never use `:latest`**
   614→
   615→### 7.2 Multi-Stage Build Patterns
   616→
   617→**Python services:**
   618→```
   619→Stage 1 "builder": python:3.x-slim → install build deps → pip install --prefix=/install
   620→Stage 2 "runtime": python:3.x-slim → COPY --from=builder /install → non-root USER → ENTRYPOINT
   621→```
   622→
   623→**Node.js services:**
   624→```
   625→Stage 1 "deps": node:20-alpine → npm ci
   626→Stage 2 "builder": COPY source → npm run build → npm prune --omit=dev
   627→Stage 3 "runtime": distroless/nodejs20 → COPY .output → no shell → ENTRYPOINT
   628→```
   629→
   630→### 7.3 Security Hardening
   631→
   632→- **Non-root user** (UID 65532) in all images, enforced by `runAsNonRoot: true` in pod spec
   633→- **Read-only root filesystem** — writable paths via `emptyDir` mounts (`/tmp`, `/app/.cache`)
   634→- **No shell** in Node.js production images (distroless)
   635→- **Drop ALL capabilities** in pod security context
   636→- **No `.env` files** baked into images; all config via K8s secrets/configmaps
   637→- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` for Python images
   638→
   639→### 7.4 Dependency Management
   640→
   641→- Python: `pip-compile` or `uv lock` for pinned requirements with hashes
   642→- Node.js: `npm ci` only (never `npm install` in CI)
   643→- Weekly Renovate/Dependabot PRs for dependency updates
   644→
   645→### 7.5 Build Metadata (OCI Labels)
   646→
   647→All images include: `org.opencontainers.image.created`, `.revision` (git SHA), `.version`, `.source`, `.title`, `.vendor` — final stage only.
   648→
   649→### 7.6 Image Size Targets
   650→
   651→| Category | Target | Optimization |
   652→|----------|--------|-------------|
   653→| Python standard | < 200 MB | Multi-stage, no cache, strip pyc |
   654→| Python ML/heavy | < 500 MB | opencv-headless, models as external volumes |
   655→| Node.js (Nuxt) | < 80 MB | Distroless runtime, npm prune |
   656→

   661→### 8.1 Standard Pipeline Architecture (Repo-Per-Service)
   662→
   663→Every service repo follows the **same pipeline** by calling reusable workflows from `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows`. A service onboards by adding a single `ci.yaml` file.
   664→
   665→**Shared Workflows Repo (`konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows/.github/workflows/`)**:
   666→
   667→```
   668→kix-platform-ci-workflows/
   669→└── .github/workflows/
   670→    ├── standard-pipeline.yaml      # Main orchestrator (called by every service)
   671→    ├── _build-image.yaml           # Reusable: Docker build + BuildKit cache
   672→    ├── _scan-and-sign.yaml         # Reusable: Trivy + SBOM (Syft) + Cosign sign
   673→    ├── _push-registry.yaml         # Reusable: Push to Artifact Registry
   674→    ├── _update-gitops.yaml         # Reusable: Dispatch to kix-platform-infrastructure
   675→    ├── _promote.yaml               # Reusable: Promote image tag between environments
   676→    └── _lint-and-test.yaml         # Reusable: Language-aware lint + unit tests
   677→```
   678→
   679→**Standard Service Repo Pipeline (every service copies this)**:
   680→
   681→```yaml
   682→# .github/workflows/ci.yaml (in each service repo — identical across all repos)
   683→name: CI
   684→on:
   685→  push:
   686→    branches: [main]
   687→  pull_request:
   688→    branches: [main]
   689→  workflow_dispatch:
   690→
   691→jobs:
   692→  pipeline:
   693→    uses: konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows/.github/workflows/standard-pipeline.yaml@v1
   694→    with:
   695→      service-name: ${{ github.event.repository.name }}
   696→      dockerfile-path: ./Dockerfile
   697→      chart-path: ./chart
   698→      python-version: "3.11"     # or node-version: "20" for Node.js services
   699→    secrets: inherit
   700→```
   701→
   702→**That's it.** Every service repo has one small workflow file. All logic lives in the shared workflows repo. Updates to the pipeline roll out to all services by bumping the `@v1` tag.
   703→
   704→**Cross-org workflow sharing**: GitHub Enterprise allows sharing workflows across organizations. The `kix-platform-ci-workflows` repo is set to **internal** visibility, making it callable from `konecta-ix-mcp-servers`, `konecta-ix-apps`, `konecta-ix-workloads`, etc.
   705→
   706→### 8.2 Standard Pipeline Stages
   707→
   708→Every PR and merge goes through the same stages:
   709→
   710→```
   711→┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐
   712→│  1. LINT &   │──▶│  2. BUILD    │──▶│  3. SCAN &   │──▶│  4. PUSH &   │──▶│  5. DEPLOY   │
   713→│     TEST     │   │    IMAGE     │   │     SIGN     │   │   REGISTRY   │   │   (GitOps)   │
   714→└──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘
   715→  PR + merge         merge only         merge only         merge only         merge only
   716→  ├ ruff/eslint      ├ BuildKit         ├ Trivy scan       ├ Push to AR       ├ Dispatch to
   717→  ├ unit tests       ├ multi-stage      ├ SBOM (Syft)      ├ Tag: sha-xxx     │ gitops repo
   718→  ├ type checks      ├ GHA cache        ├ Cosign sign      └ Tag: v1.2.3      ├ Update dev
   719→  └ pip audit/       └ OCI labels         (OIDC)             (on git tag)     │ values.yaml
   720→    npm audit                                                                  └ ArgoCD syncs
   721→```
   722→
   723→**On PR**: Only stage 1 runs (lint, test). Fast feedback.
   724→**On merge to main**: All 5 stages run. Image built, scanned, signed, pushed, deployed to dev.
   725→
   726→### 8.3 Image Tagging Strategy
   727→
   728→| Tag | When | Purpose |
   729→|-----|------|---------|
   730→| `sha-{7-char}` | Every build | Immutable build identifier |
   731→| `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | On git tag | Release version |
   732→| `{env}-latest` | On promotion | Environment pointer (mutable) |
   733→
   734→**Rule**: SHA tags are immutable. Never overwrite. Promotion copies the SHA tag reference, not the image.
   735→
   736→### 8.4 Security Gates
   737→
   738→```
   739→Build → Trivy Scan → SBOM Generation (Syft) → Push → Cosign Sign (OIDC)
   740→         ↓ block                                        ↓
   741→   CRITICAL/HIGH CVE                           Kyverno admission policy
   742→   fails the build                             enforces signed images (prod)
   743→```
   744→
   745→- **Trivy**: CRITICAL/HIGH block push; MEDIUM logged; LOW informational
   746→- **SBOM**: SPDX + CycloneDX dual format, attached as OCI annotation
   747→- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore (no long-lived keys)
   748→- **Kyverno**: Cluster admission controller verifies image signatures before deploy (prod only)
   749→
   750→### 8.5 GitOps Flow (Cross-Repo)
   751→
   752→```
   753→konecta-ix-mcp-servers/                    konecta-ix-platform/
   754→kix-mcp-gateway                            kix-platform-infrastructure
   755→────────────────────                       ───────────────────────────
   756→1. PR merged to main
   757→2. Pipeline builds sha-abc1234
   758→3. Image pushed to Artifact Registry
   759→4. Pipeline triggers repository_dispatch ──▶ 5. Automated PR or direct commit:
   760→                                                environments/dev/kix-mcp-gateway.yaml
   761→                                                  image.tag: sha-abc1234
   762→                                             6. ArgoCD detects change → syncs dev
   763→```
   764→
   765→**Cross-org dispatch**: A GitHub App installed across all orgs (`konecta-ix-platform`, `konecta-ix-mcp-servers`, `konecta-ix-apps`, etc.) enables the `_update-gitops.yaml` workflow to commit to `kix-platform-infrastructure`. The App has write access only to the gitops repo, scoped to minimal permissions.
   766→
   767→### 8.6 Environment Promotion
   768→
   769→| Environment | Trigger | Sync Mode | Change Window |
   770→|-------------|---------|-----------|---------------|
   771→| Dev | Every merge to main | Auto-sync | 24/7 |
   772→| QA | Manual workflow dispatch or PR to kix-platform-infrastructure | Manual sync | 08:00-18:00 UTC |
   773→| Prod | Manual workflow dispatch + approval | Manual sync | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |
   774→
   775→**Promotion workflow** (from `kix-platform-ci-workflows/_promote.yaml`):
   776→1. Engineer triggers `promote` workflow on the service repo (or directly on kix-platform-infrastructure)
   777→2. Workflow copies the image tag from `environments/dev/{service}.yaml` to `environments/qa/{service}.yaml`
   778→3. Creates a PR on kix-platform-infrastructure (QA/Prod promotions always go through PR review)
   779→4. After merge, ArgoCD syncs
   780→
   781→### 8.7 Rollback Strategy
   782→
   783→- **Automated** (prod): Prometheus alert on error rate >5% for 3 min → webhook reverts commit in kix-platform-infrastructure → ArgoCD syncs previous image
   784→- **Manual**: Engineer reverts the `environments/prod/{service}.yaml` entry to previous SHA tag via PR to kix-platform-infrastructure
   785→- **RTO target**: <5 min (detection + revert + sync)
   786→- **Git as source of truth**: Every rollback is a visible Git commit in kix-platform-infrastructure
   787→
   788→### 8.8 Secrets Management (CI)
   789→
   790→- **GitHub OIDC** → GCP Workload Identity Pool (no JSON keys, configured per org)

Now I have both documents fully. Here's the comparison:

---

## Discrepancies: Blueprint vs DevSecOps Handbook

### MISSING FROM BLUEPRINT (Handbook requires, blueprint doesn't have)

**1. Secret Scanning — Pre-Build Stage**
- Handbook: Trivy secret scan as a **dedicated stage before build**, pipeline stops on detection
- Blueprint: No explicit secret scanning stage. Trivy is only mentioned for container vulnerability scanning post-build. Secret detection in source code before build is absent.

**2. SAST — SonarQube**
- Handbook: **SonarQube** mandatory on every commit, quality gate blocks pipeline on critical/high
- Blueprint: No SAST tool. Only linting (ruff/eslint) and type checks. No SonarQube or equivalent.

**3. SCA — Dependency-Track**
- Handbook: **Dependency-Track** as dedicated SCA tool, consumes Syft SBOMs, scans daily + on commit, pipeline stops on critical/high
- Blueprint: Only `pip audit` / `npm audit` (lightweight). No Dependency-Track instance. Syft generates SBOMs but they're not fed into a dedicated SCA platform for continuous tracking.

**4. DAST — OWASP ZAP**
- Handbook: **OWASP ZAP** post-deployment in pre-prod, blocks promotion to production
- Blueprint: No DAST stage at all. No OWASP ZAP. Pipeline ends at deployment — no post-deploy security validation.

**5. E2E Testing — Cypress**
- Handbook: **Cypress** for end-to-end testing post-deployment to pre-prod
- Blueprint: No E2E testing stage. Only unit tests on PR.

**6. API Testing — Postman**
- Handbook: **Postman** for functional/integration API testing, integrated into pipeline
- Blueprint: No API testing stage.

**7. Performance Testing — JMeter**
- Handbook: **Apache JMeter** for load/performance testing with baseline thresholds
- Blueprint: No performance testing stage.

**8. Runtime Container Security — Aqua**
- Handbook: **Aqua** for runtime container protection (alongside Trivy for scanning)
- Blueprint: No runtime container security tool. Trivy handles scanning only.

**9. Secret Management — HashiCorp Vault**
- Handbook: **HashiCorp Vault** is the mandated secret management tool with centralized storage, rotation policies, audit logging
- Blueprint: Uses **External Secrets Operator + GCP Secret Manager**. GCP-native equivalent, but not Vault. This is flagged as "GCP native equivalent is fine" per your instruction, but worth noting the handbook explicitly names Vault.

**10. Branching Strategy — Git Flow**
- Handbook: **Git Flow** is mandatory for all repositories
- Blueprint: No branching strategy specified. The pipeline triggers on `push to main` and `pull_request to main`, which implies trunk-based development, not Git Flow (which uses `develop`, `release/*`, `hotfix/*` branches).

**11. Vulnerability Remediation SLAs**
- Handbook: Explicit SLAs (Critical: 24h fix, High: 7 days, Medium: 30 days, Low: 90 days) with triage-to-fix-to-deploy timelines
- Blueprint: No remediation SLAs defined.

**12. Automated Jira Ticket Creation**
- Handbook: Critical/Major vulnerabilities auto-create Jira tickets with commit/PR links and component ownership assignment
- Blueprint: No automated ticketing on vulnerability detection. Only "logged" or "blocks pipeline".

**13. Pipeline Stage Ordering**
- Handbook order: Lint → **Secret Scan** → **SCA** → **SAST** → Build & Unit Test → Container Build → Container Scan → Sign & SBOM → **E2E & DAST** → Monitor
- Blueprint order: Lint & Test → Build Image → Scan & Sign → Push → Deploy
- Blueprint is missing 4 pre-build stages (secret scan, SCA, SAST) and 1 post-deploy stage (E2E/DAST).

**14. Notifications — Google Chat / Email**
- Handbook: **Google Chat** and **Email** for team notifications
- Blueprint: Mentions Slack for notifications. The handbook specifies Google Chat.

**15. Developer Platform — Backstage**
- Handbook: **Backstage** as the developer platform / service catalog
- Blueprint: No developer portal mentioned (though `kix-services-backstage` exists in the GitHub org structure).

---

### ALIGNED (Blueprint matches handbook)

| Handbook Requirement | Blueprint Coverage |
|---|---|
| Trivy for container scanning | Trivy post-build, CRITICAL/HIGH blocks |
| Cosign for image signing | Cosign keyless via OIDC/Sigstore |
| Syft for SBOM | Syft with CycloneDX (blueprint also adds SPDX) |
| Workload Identity Federation | GCP Workload Identity + GitHub OIDC (no long-lived keys) |
| Same artifact promoted across envs | Immutable SHA tags, promoted via gitops values |
| Configuration externalized | ConfigMaps, External Secrets, Helm values per env |
| Environment isolation | Separate namespaces per env, separate ArgoCD projects |
| Prometheus + Grafana | In observability stack |
| Pipeline stops on failure | CRITICAL/HIGH blocks push, Kyverno blocks unsigned images |
| GitHub Actions for CI/CD | Standard pipeline via reusable workflows |
| Terraform for IaC | Existing Terraform modules in kix-platform-modules |
| No secrets in code | ESO, Workload Identity, `docker build --secret`, no .env in images |
| Least privilege | Per-service Workload Identity, namespace RBAC, Kyverno policies |

---

### SUMMARY: Gaps to Close

| Priority | Gap | Handbook Tool | Action Needed |
|----------|-----|---------------|---------------|
| **High** | No SAST | SonarQube | Add SonarQube stage to `_lint-and-test.yaml` |
| **High** | No secret scanning (pre-build) | Trivy (secret mode) | Add `trivy fs --scanners secret` stage before build |
| **High** | No SCA platform | Dependency-Track | Deploy Dependency-Track, feed Syft SBOMs into it |
| **High** | No DAST | OWASP ZAP | Add post-deploy ZAP baseline scan in pre-prod promotion |
| **Medium** | No E2E testing | Cypress | Add E2E stage to promotion workflow |
| **Medium** | No API testing | Postman/Newman | Add API test stage to promotion workflow |
| **Medium** | No performance testing | JMeter | Add perf baseline gate (at least for Tier 1 services) |
| **Medium** | No Git Flow branching | Git Flow | Align branching model or get an exception |
| **Medium** | No remediation SLAs | Policy | Define and document SLAs in pipeline docs |
| **Medium** | No auto-ticketing | Jira integration | Add Jira ticket creation on CRITICAL/HIGH findings |
| **Low** | No Aqua (runtime) | Aqua | Evaluate for runtime protection post-migration |
| **Low** | Slack vs Google Chat | Google Chat | Switch notification channel |
| **Neutral** | GCP Secret Manager vs Vault | HashiCorp Vault | GCP-native equivalent — acceptable per your guidance |

   682→# .github/workflows/ci.yaml (in each service repo — identical across all repos)
   683→name: CI
   684→on:
   685→  push:

   703→
   704→**Cross-org workflow sharing**: GitHub Enterprise allows sharing workflows across organizations. The `kix-platform-ci-workflows` repo is set to **internal** visibility, making it callable from `konecta-ix-mcp-servers`, `konecta-ix-apps`, `konecta-ix-workloads`, etc.
   705→
   706→### 8.2 Standard Pipeline Stages
   707→
   708→Every PR and merge goes through the same stages:
   709→
   710→```
   711→┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐
   712→│  1. LINT &   │──▶│  2. BUILD    │──▶│  3. SCAN &   │──▶│  4. PUSH &   │──▶│  5. DEPLOY   │
   713→│     TEST     │   │    IMAGE     │   │     SIGN     │   │   REGISTRY   │   │   (GitOps)   │
   714→└──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └──────────────┘
   715→  PR + merge         merge only         merge only         merge only         merge only
   716→  ├ ruff/eslint      ├ BuildKit         ├ Trivy scan       ├ Push to AR       ├ Dispatch to
   717→  ├ unit tests       ├ multi-stage      ├ SBOM (Syft)      ├ Tag: sha-xxx     │ gitops repo
   718→  ├ type checks      ├ GHA cache        ├ Cosign sign      └ Tag: v1.2.3      ├ Update dev

   720→    npm audit                                                                  └ ArgoCD syncs
   721→```
   722→
   723→**On PR**: Only stage 1 runs (lint, test). Fast feedback.
   724→**On merge to main**: All 5 stages run. Image built, scanned, signed, pushed, deployed to dev.
   725→
   726→### 8.3 Image Tagging Strategy
   727→
   728→| Tag | When | Purpose |
   729→|-----|------|---------|
   730→| `sha-{7-char}` | Every build | Immutable build identifier |
   731→| `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` | On git tag | Release version |
   732→| `{env}-latest` | On promotion | Environment pointer (mutable) |
   733→
   734→**Rule**: SHA tags are immutable. Never overwrite. Promotion copies the SHA tag reference, not the image.
   735→

   739→Build → Trivy Scan → SBOM Generation (Syft) → Push → Cosign Sign (OIDC)

   741→   CRITICAL/HIGH CVE                           Kyverno admission policy

   743→```

   747→- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore (no long-lived keys)

  "old_string": "### 8.4 Security Gates\n\n```\nBuild → Trivy Scan → SBOM Generation (Syft) → Push → Cosign Sign (OIDC)\n         ↓ block                                        ↓\n   CRITICAL/HIGH CVE                           Kyverno admission policy\n   fails the build                             enforces signed images (prod)\n```\n\n- **Trivy**: CRITICAL/HIGH block push; MEDIUM logged; LOW informational\n- **SBOM**: SPDX + CycloneDX dual format, attached as OCI annotation\n- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore (no long-lived keys)\n- **Kyverno**: Cluster admission controller verifies image signatures before deploy (prod only)",
  "new_string": "### 8.4 Security Gates\n\nPer the Konecta DevSecOps Handbook, every gate is fail-stop — pipeline halts immediately on critical/high findings.\n\n| Gate | Tool | Stage | Trigger | Action on Failure |\n|------|------|-------|---------|-------------------|\n| **Secret Detection** | Trivy (`--scanners secret`) | 2 (pre-build) | Every PR + merge | Stop pipeline, no exceptions |\n| **Dependency Vulnerabilities** | Dependency-Track (via Syft SBOM) | 3 (pre-build) | Every PR + merge | Stop on critical/high |\n| **SAST Quality Gate** | SonarQube | 4 (pre-build) | Every PR + merge | Stop on critical/high |\n| **Container Vulnerabilities** | Trivy (image scan) | 6 (post-build) | Every merge | Stop on critical/high |\n| **Image Signature** | Cosign (Sigstore/OIDC) | 6 (post-build) | Every merge | Unsigned images blocked |\n| **DAST Baseline** | OWASP ZAP | 9 (post-deploy QA) | Before prod promotion | Block promotion on findings |\n| **Admission Control** | Kyverno | Deploy-time | Every sync | Reject unsigned images, block `:latest` |\n\n**Tool details:**\n\n- **Trivy secret scan**: Scans source filesystem for hardcoded secrets, keys, tokens before any build occurs. Zero tolerance — any finding stops the pipeline.\n- **SonarQube**: Hosted instance in `shared-devsecops` namespace. Runs static analysis on every PR. Quality gate: no new critical or high severity issues. Results posted as PR comment via GitHub integration.\n- **Dependency-Track**: Hosted instance in `shared-devsecops` namespace. Syft generates CycloneDX SBOMs from source dependencies (stage 3) and from built images (stage 6). Both uploaded to Dependency-Track for continuous vulnerability tracking. Pipeline stops on critical/high.\n- **OWASP ZAP**: Runs as a containerized baseline scan against the service's QA endpoint after deployment. Blocks promotion to prod if baseline vulnerabilities found. Integrated into the `_promote.yaml` workflow as a pre-condition.\n- **Cosign**: Keyless signing via GitHub OIDC → Sigstore. No long-lived keys.\n- **Kyverno**: Cluster admission controller enforces signed images, blocks `:latest` tag, requires Konecta Artifact Registry as source.\n\n**Vulnerability remediation SLAs** (per DevSecOps Handbook):\n\n| Severity | Detection → Triage | Triage → Fix | Fix → Deploy |\n|----------|-------------------|-------------|-------------|\n| Critical | 4 hours | 24 hours | 48 hours |\n| High | 24 hours | 7 days | 14 days |\n| Medium | 7 days | 30 days | 45 days |\n| Low | 30 days | 90 days | Next release |"
}

   850→- **RTO target**: <5 min (detection + revert + sync)
   851→- **Git as source of truth**: Every rollback is a visible Git commit in kix-platform-infrastructure
   852→
   853→### 8.8 Secrets Management (CI)
   854→
   855→- **GitHub OIDC** → GCP Workload Identity Pool (no JSON keys, configured per org)
   856→- Dynamic secret fetching from GCP Secret Manager at build time
   857→- Build secrets via `docker build --secret` (never in image layers)
   858→- Token lifetime ~1 hour, auto-refreshing, full audit trail
   859→- **GitHub App** for cross-org dispatch (kix-platform-infrastructure commits), installed across all orgs, scoped to minimal permissions
   860→
   861→### 8.9 Onboarding a New Service
   862→
   863→Adding a new service to the platform:
   864→
   865→1. **Create repo** in the appropriate org from `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-service-template`:
   866→   - Standard `Dockerfile` (Python or Node.js variant)
   867→   - Standard `chart/` directory with `Chart.yaml` depending on base chart
   868→   - Standard `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` calling shared pipeline
   869→   - Standard `.dockerignore`, `CODEOWNERS`
   870→2. **Add to gitops repo**: Create `environments/{dev,qa,prod}/{service-name}.yaml` in `kix-platform-infrastructure`
   871→3. **ArgoCD auto-discovers** via ApplicationSet git directory generator
   872→4. **First push** triggers the standard pipeline — fully operational
   873→
   874→No pipeline code to write. No Helm templates to create. Just fill in `values.yaml` with service-specific config.
   875→
   876→---
   877→
   878→## 9. Security Architecture
   879→
   880→### 9.1 Container Image Security
   881→
   882→- **Multi-stage builds**: Builder dependencies never ship in runtime image
   883→- **Base image policy**: Pinned digests, updated monthly, slim/distroless only
   884→- **Vulnerability scanning**: Trivy pre-push, CRITICAL/HIGH block deployment
   885→- **Image signing**: Cosign keyless (OIDC) — every image cryptographically signed
   886→- **SBOM**: Generated for every build, stored as OCI annotation
   887→- **Registry**: Artifact Registry with vulnerability scanning enabled, IAM-restricted access
   888→
   889→### 9.2 Supply Chain Security
   890→
   891→- **Dependency pinning**: Hash-verified `requirements.txt` (Python), `package-lock.json` (Node.js)
   892→- **Dependency scanning**: `pip audit` / `npm audit` in CI, Renovate for updates
   893→- **GitHub Actions hardening**:
   894→  - Pin action versions by SHA (not tag)
   895→  - Minimal `GITHUB_TOKEN` permissions per workflow
   896→  - No third-party actions without security review
   897→  - OIDC for all GCP interactions (no stored credentials)
   898→
   899→### 9.3 Runtime Security
   900→
   901→- **Pod Security Standards**: `restricted` profile enforced via namespace labels
   902→  - `runAsNonRoot: true`
   903→  - `readOnlyRootFilesystem: true`
   904→  - `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`
   905→  - `capabilities.drop: [ALL]`
   906→  - `seccompProfile.type: RuntimeDefault`
   907→- **No privileged containers**. No `hostNetwork`, `hostPID`, `hostIPC`.
   908→- Writable paths only via explicit `emptyDir` volume mounts
   909→
   910→### 9.4 Secrets Management (Runtime)
   911→
   912→**External Secrets Operator (ESO) + GCP Secret Manager**
   913→
   914→- ESO installed in `shared-secrets` namespace
   915→- `SecretStore` per environment, authenticated via Workload Identity
   916→- `ExternalSecret` CRDs in each service Helm chart pull secrets from GCP
   917→- Secrets never stored in Git or Helm values files
   918→- Automatic rotation support via ESO refresh interval
   919→
   920→**Workload Identity for GCP API access**:
   921→```yaml
   922→serviceAccount:
   923→  annotations:
   924→    iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: {service}@{project}.iam.gserviceaccount.com
   925→```
   926→No service account JSON keys. Ever.
   927→
   928→### 9.5 Network Security
   929→
   930→- **Dual load balancer isolation**: API traffic (Kong) and frontend traffic (Gateway API + IAP) on separate LBs — blast radius is contained per path
   931→- **IAP (Identity-Aware Proxy)** on frontend LB: Users authenticate via Google/OIDC before reaching Nuxt frontends. Access controlled by IAM policies on the IAP resource.
   932→- **Cloud Armor WAF** on both load balancers (OWASP top-10 rules, DDoS protection)
   933→- **Default deny ingress** on all application namespaces
   934→- **Explicit NetworkPolicies** per service: allow only expected sources
   935→- **GCP health check CIDRs** allowed (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)
   936→- **Private cluster**: No public node IPs, egress via Cloud NAT
   937→- **DNS policies**: Restrict external DNS resolution where possible
   938→
   939→### 9.6 RBAC & Namespace Isolation
   940→
   941→- **Service accounts per namespace** with least-privilege Roles
   942→- **ArgoCD Projects** restrict which namespaces each team can deploy to
   943→- **Namespace ResourceQuotas** prevent resource exhaustion attacks
   944→- **Kyverno policies**:
   945→  - Enforce signed images (prod)
   946→  - Block `latest` tag
   947→  - Require resource limits
   948→  - Enforce label standards
   949→
   950→### 9.7 Admission Control
   951→
   952→- **Kyverno** (preferred over OPA/Gatekeeper for readability):
   953→  - Verify Cosign image signatures
   954→  - Enforce pod security standards
   955→  - Block privileged containers
   956→  - Require Konecta Artifact Registry as image source
   957→  - Enforce annotation/label standards
   958→
   959→---
   960→
   961→## 10. Observability
   962→
   963→### Stack
   964→| Component | Tool | Purpose |
   965→|-----------|------|---------|
   966→| Metrics | Prometheus | Scrape K8s + application metrics |
   967→| Dashboards | Grafana | Visualization, alerting |
   968→| Tracing | Jaeger | Distributed traces (OpenTelemetry) |
   969→| Logging | GCP Cloud Logging | Centralized log aggregation |
   970→| Instrumentation | OpenTelemetry SDK | App-level traces, metrics, logs |
   971→| Collection | OTel Collector (DaemonSet) | Receive, process, export telemetry |
   972→
   973→### Application Integration
   974→- All services emit OpenTelemetry traces/metrics (already instrumented)
   975→- OTel Collector as DaemonSet in `shared-monitoring` receives and exports
   976→- Prometheus scrapes `/metrics` endpoints via PodMonitor/ServiceMonitor CRDs
   977→- Grafana dashboards per service tier (AI workloads, APIs, frontends)
   978→
   979→### Key Alerts
   980→- Pod CrashLoopBackOff > 2 restarts
   981→- Error rate > 5% for 3 min (triggers rollback)
   982→- P95 latency > 2x baseline
   983→- HPA at max replicas for > 10 min
   984→- Certificate expiry < 14 days
   985→- ArgoCD sync failure
   986→
   987→---
   988→
   989→## 11. Cost & Operational Considerations
   990→
   991→### Cloud Run vs GKE Cost Model
   992→
   993→| Factor | Cloud Run | GKE (Target) |
   994→|--------|-----------|---------------|
   995→| Billing | Per-request (scale to zero) | Reserved capacity (always-on) |
   996→| Idle cost | $0 | Node pool minimum |
   997→| Burst cost | High (cold starts + per-invocation) | Lower (pre-provisioned pods) |
   998→| Predictability | Variable | Predictable |
   999→
  1000→**Mitigation for idle cost**: GKE Autopilot scales node pools down. Low-traffic Tier 3 services can run with 1 replica (HPA min=1). Consider `minReplicas: 0` with KEDA for truly idle services.
  1001→
  1002→### Operational Overhead
  1003→- **Helm chart maintenance**: Base chart library reduces per-service overhead
  1004→- **ArgoCD operations**: Self-healing reduces manual intervention
  1005→- **Monitoring**: Prometheus + Grafana replaces fragmented Cloud Run metrics
  1006→- **On-call**: Standardized runbooks across all services (same platform, same patterns)
  1007→
  1008→### Disaster Recovery
  1009→- **Multi-region**: Secondary GKE cluster in eu-west4 (already provisioned)
  1010→- **Failover**: DNS-based failover to secondary cluster
  1011→- **Data**: Cloud SQL cross-region replicas, GCS multi-regional buckets
  1012→- **RPO**: Near-zero (managed services handle replication)
  1013→- **RTO**: <15 min (ArgoCD can deploy to secondary cluster from same Git state)
  1014→
  1015→---
  1016→
  1017→## 12. Risk Register
  1018→
  1019→| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
  1020→|------|-----------|--------|------------|
  1021→| Resource sizing incorrect for K8s | Medium | Medium | Start with generous limits, tune based on metrics after migration |
  1022→| Secret exposure during migration | Low | Critical | External Secrets Operator, no secrets in Git, Workload Identity only |
  1023→| ArgoCD sync storms (24 services) | Low | Medium | Sync waves, rate limiting, progressive rollout |
  1024→| Team unfamiliarity with Helm/K8s | Medium | Medium | Pilot phase in Phase 1, runbook documentation, training |
  1025→| Kong ingress misconfiguration | Low | High | Test routes per service before decommissioning Cloud Run |
  1026→| Shared workflow drift | Low | Medium | Versioned `@v1` tag, Renovate bumps, changelog for breaking changes |
  1027→| LiteLLM routing via Kong | Low | Medium | NetworkPolicy enforces Kong-only access; test LLM routes early in Phase 1 |
  1028→
  1029→---

  1033→| Component | Technology | Version |
  1034→|-----------|-----------|---------|
  1035→| Container Orchestration | GKE Autopilot | Latest |
  1036→| Package Management | Helm | 3.13+ |
  1037→| GitOps CD | ArgoCD | 2.8+ |
  1038→| CI | GitHub Actions | N/A |
  1039→| Container Registry | GCP Artifact Registry | N/A |
  1040→| API Gateway | Kong | Existing |
  1041→| LLM Proxy | LiteLLM | Existing |
  1042→| Secrets | External Secrets Operator + GCP Secret Manager | Latest |
  1043→| Policy Engine | Kyverno | 1.11+ |
  1044→| Cert Management | cert-manager + Let's Encrypt | Latest |
  1045→| Vulnerability Scanning | Trivy | 0.48+ |
  1046→| Image Signing | Cosign (Sigstore) | 2.2+ |
  1047→| SBOM | Syft | 0.80+ |
  1048→| Metrics | Prometheus | Latest |
  1049→| Dashboards | Grafana | Latest |
  1050→| Tracing | Jaeger + OpenTelemetry | Latest |
  1051→| IaC | Terraform | >= 1.5.0 |
  1052→
  1053→## Appendix B: Namespace Map
  1054→
  1055→```
  1056→shared-argocd          (existing)
  1057→shared-kong            (existing)
  1058→shared-litellm         (existing)
  1059→shared-monitoring      (new)

  1066→```
  1067→
  1068→## Appendix C: Repository & Namespace Mapping
  1069→
  1070→### Platform Repos (konecta-ix-platform)
  1071→| Repository | Purpose |
  1072→|------------|---------|
  1073→| `kix-platform-infrastructure` | GitOps repo (ArgoCD source of truth, Terraform, env values) |
  1074→| `kix-platform-modules` | Shared Terraform modules + Helm base chart |
  1075→| `kix-platform-ci-workflows` | Reusable GitHub Actions workflows (standard pipeline) |
  1076→| `kix-platform-service-template` | Template repo for bootstrapping new services |
  1077→| `kix-platform-docs` | Platform documentation |

  1079→### Service Repos (In Scope)
  1080→| GitHub Org | Service Repo | K8s Namespace | Tier |

### **2. DOCKERFILES**

#### **Single Dockerfile** (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/kon-iq-portal/Dockerfile`)

**Base Images:**
- `node:22-alpine` (all stages)

**Build Stages:**
1. **dependencies** - Production dependencies only (`npm ci --omit=dev`)
2. **dev-dependencies** - All dependencies including dev dependencies
3. **builder** - Builds Nuxt app with `npm run generate`
4. **runner** - Final production image

**Key Features:**
- BuildKit cache mounts for npm and Nuxt build cache
- Multi-stage build for optimization
- Non-root user (nodejs:1001)
- Security hardening (read-only file permissions)
- Health check on `/health` endpoint
- `dumb-init` for proper signal handling
- Graceful shutdown support for Cloud Run

**Build Arguments:**
- `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` - OAuth client ID (build-time for frontend)
- `NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT` - Environment name (production/staging/dev)
- `BUILD_DATE` - Build timestamp
- `VCS_REF` - Git commit SHA
- `COMMIT_SHA` - Short git SHA for version display

**Security Considerations:**
- ✅ Uses alpine base (smaller attack surface)
- ✅ Non-root user
- ✅ Read-only filesystem where possible
- ✅ No secrets in build layers (uses build args)
- ✅ Regular security updates (`apk upgrade`)
- ⚠️ Uses `node:22-alpine` (latest stable, good)
- ⚠️ No vulnerability scanning in Dockerfile itself (done in cloudbuild.yaml)

**Security Issues Found:**
- None critical - follows best practices
- Minor: Could add `--no-cache` to apk commands for smaller image

**Runtime Configuration:**
- Port: 8080 (Cloud Run standard)
- Node options: `--max-old-space-size=512` (memory optimization)
- Entrypoint: `dumb-init` + `docker-entrypoint.sh`

---

const pool = process.env.DATABASE_URL
    ? new Pool({ connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL })
    : new Pool({
        host: process.env.DB_HOST || 'localhost',  // Can be /cloudsql/...
        port: parseInt(process.env.DB_PORT || '5432'),
        database: process.env.DB_NAME || 'iqplatform',
        user: process.env.DB_USER || 'konecta',
        password: process.env.DB_PASSWORD || 'konecta'
    });
```

**Unix Socket Connection for Cloud SQL:**
- Set `DB_HOST=/cloudsql/${INSTANCE_CONNECTION_NAME}`

### **6. CI/CD CONFIGURATION**

#### **Cloud Build** (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/kon-iq-portal/cloudbuild.yaml`)

**Pipeline Steps:**
1. **Setup BuildKit Builder** - Docker buildx for advanced caching
2. **Build App Image** - Multi-stage Docker build with cache
3. **Push Cache Tag** - Registry cache for faster builds
4. **Scan for Vulnerabilities** - Artifact Registry scanning (optional)
5. **Deploy to Cloud Run** - Update service with new image
6. **Update Traffic** - Route 100% traffic to latest revision

**Key Features:**
- BuildKit registry cache (`--cache-from/--cache-to`)
- Inline cache for faster pulls
- Security scanning with Artifact Registry
- Provenance/SBOM disabled for Cloud Run compatibility
- Zstandard compression for faster push/pull
- High-CPU machine (E2_HIGHCPU_8) for faster builds
- 200GB disk for large builds

--build-arg=BUILD_DATE=$BUILD_ID
--build-arg=VCS_REF=$COMMIT_SHA
--build-arg=COMMIT_SHA=$SHORT_SHA
```

--min-instances=${_MIN_INSTANCES} # Default: 0
--max-instances=${_MAX_INSTANCES} # Default: 10
--concurrency=${_CONCURRENCY}     # Default: 80
--timeout=${_TIMEOUT}             # Default: 300s
--cpu-boost                        # Faster cold starts
```

- `_REGION` - europe-west1

- **Users:** Email, roles, permissions
- **Agents:** AI agents catalog
- **MCP Servers:** Model Context Protocol servers
- **Knowledge Bases:** RAG corpus management
- **Use Case Requests:** Request tracking and approval workflow
- **Success Stories:** Case studies
- **Courses & Demos:** Training materials
- **Settings:** Dynamic configuration
- **API Tokens:** Token-based authentication
- **Analytics:** Usage tracking

---

### **10. GCP-SPECIFIC CODE**

#### **Cloud Storage Usage** (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/kon-iq-portal/backend/services/upload.service.ts`)
- **Buckets:**
  - `GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET` - Agent/showcase images (public)
  - `GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET` - Demo videos (public)
  - `GCS_RAG_BUCKET` - Knowledge base documents (private)
- **Operations:**
  - File uploads (images, videos, documents)
  - Signed URL generation (v4)
  - Public URL generation for public buckets
- **Client:** `@google-cloud/storage` v7.17.3

#### **Vertex AI Integration** (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/kon-iq-portal/backend/services/rag.service.ts`)
- **Models:**
  - `gemini-2.5-flash` (default)
  - `gemini-2.0-flash-exp` (configurable)
- **Features:**
  - RAG retrieval from Vertex AI corpus
  - Structured output with schema definition
  - Multi-language support
  - Source citation
- **Configuration:**
  - Corpus: `projects/${RAG_PROJECT_ID}/locations/${RAG_LOCATION}/ragCorpora/${RAG_CORPUS_ID}`
  - Temperature: 0.7 (configurable)
  - Max tokens: 2048
- **Client:** `@google-cloud/vertexai` v1.10.0

#### **Cloud Tasks** (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/kon-iq-portal/backend/services/cloud-tasks.service.ts`)
- **Queue:** `rag-ingestion-poc`
- **Location:** europe-west1
- **Purpose:** Async RAG document ingestion
- **Target:** External ingestion service (kon-rag-ingestion)
- **Payload:** File metadata for ingestion
- **Client:** `@google-cloud/tasks` v6.2.1

#### **Secret Manager** (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-tic/kon-iq-portal/backend/db/migrations/00024_add_rag_pipeline_token.ts`)
- **Secrets:**
  - `KON_IQ_RAG_CLIENT_SECRET` - RAG pipeline API token
  - `koniq-jwt-secret` - JWT signing key
  - `koniq-google-client-id` - OAuth client ID
  - `db-password` - Database password
- **Usage:** TypeScript migration fetches secret from Secret Manager
- **Client:** `@google-cloud/secret-manager` v6.1.1

#### **Pub/Sub Usage**
**No Pub/Sub found** in the codebase.

#### **Summary of GCP Services:**
1. ✅ **Cloud Storage** - File storage (images, videos, documents)
2. ✅ **Vertex AI** - RAG and LLM inference
3. ✅ **Cloud Tasks** - Async job queue
4. ✅ **Secret Manager** - Secrets storage
5. ✅ **Cloud Run** - Application hosting
6. ✅ **Cloud SQL** - PostgreSQL database (VPC + Unix socket)
7. ✅ **Cloud Build** - CI/CD pipeline
8. ✅ **Artifact Registry** - Container image storage
9. ✅ **VPC** - Private networking
10. ❌ **Pub/Sub** - Not used

---

### **11. DEPLOYMENT WORKFLOW**

**Branch Strategy:**
- `develop` → Dev environment
- `staging` → Staging environment
- `main` → Production environment

**Deployment Flow:**
1. Code pushed to Bitbucket
2. Cloud Build trigger fires
3. BuildKit builds Docker image with cache
4. Image pushed to Artifact Registry
5. Vulnerability scanning (optional)
6. Cloud Run service updated
7. Traffic routed to new revision

**Terraform Management:**
- Cloud Run service configuration
- Cloud SQL instance
- VPC and networking
- IAM roles and service accounts
- Secret Manager secrets
- Cloud Build triggers

---

### **12. KEY FINDINGS FOR MIGRATION**

#### **Current Architecture:**
- **Monolithic SPA + API** - Single container serves both frontend and backend
- **No separate frontend deployment** - Nuxt generates static files, Express serves them
- **Cloud Run** - Single service, scales to zero
- **Cloud SQL** - Shared PostgreSQL instance across environments
- **Multiple GCP Projects:**
  - `kd-corp-dgt-svc-apps-dev` - Main project for storage/networking
  - `kd-lab-464110` - Vertex AI and RAG
- **No Kubernetes** - Pure serverless (Cloud Run)

#### **Security Considerations:**
- ✅ Secrets in Secret Manager (not in code/env)
- ✅ Non-root container user
- ✅ JWT authentication with HttpOnly cookies
- ✅ OAuth domain restriction (@konecta.com)
- ✅ Role-based access control (10+ roles)
- ✅ API token scopes for machine-to-machine auth
- ⚠️ No WAF/CDN (direct Cloud Run exposure)
- ⚠️ Public Cloud Run endpoints (authentication required at app level)

#### **Scalability:**
- Min instances: 0 (scales to zero)
- Max instances: 10 (configurable)
- Concurrency: 80 requests per instance
- Cold start optimization: CPU boost enabled
- Database connection pooling (2-10 connections)

---

     1→# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1.4
     2→# Multi-stage build for optimized Nuxt + Express API deployment with BuildKit
     3→
     4→# ============================================
     5→# Dependencies Stage - Production only
     6→# ============================================
     7→FROM node:22-alpine AS dependencies
     8→
     9→WORKDIR /app
    10→
    11→# Copy package files
    12→COPY package*.json ./
    13→
    14→# Install production dependencies with BuildKit cache mount
    15→RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.npm \
    16→    npm ci --omit=dev && \
    17→    npm cache clean --force
    18→
    19→# ============================================
    20→# Dev Dependencies Stage - All dependencies
    21→# ============================================
    22→FROM node:22-alpine AS dev-dependencies
    23→
    24→WORKDIR /app
    25→
    26→# Copy package files
    27→COPY package*.json ./
    28→
    29→# Install all dependencies with BuildKit cache mount
    30→RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.npm \
    31→    npm ci && \
    32→    npm cache clean --force
    33→
    34→# ============================================
    35→# Builder Stage - Build application
    36→# ============================================
    37→FROM dev-dependencies AS builder
    38→
    39→# Build arguments
    40→ARG VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID
    41→ARG NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT=production
    42→ARG BUILD_DATE
    43→ARG VCS_REF
    44→ARG COMMIT_SHA=unknown
    45→
    46→# Environment variables for build
    47→ENV VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=${VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID}
    48→ENV NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT=${NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT}
    49→ENV NODE_ENV=production
    50→
    51→WORKDIR /app
    52→
    53→# Copy configuration files first (better layer caching)
    54→# COPY nuxt.config.ts
    55→COPY nuxt.config.ts tsconfig.app.json tsconfig.node.json ./
    56→# COPY nuxt.config.ts tsconfig.json tsconfig.app.json tsconfig.node.json ./
    57→# COPY tailwind.config.js ./
    58→# COPY eslint.config.js ./
    59→
    60→# Copy source code
    61→COPY src ./src
    62→COPY backend ./backend
    63→COPY public ./public
    64→COPY docker-entrypoint.sh ./
    65→
    66→# Copy version files and write commit SHA
    67→COPY version.txt ./
    68→RUN echo "${COMMIT_SHA}" > version_commit.txt
    69→
    70→# Build the Nuxt application (generates static files)
    71→RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/app/.nuxt \
    72→    --mount=type=cache,target=/app/node_modules/.cache \
    73→    npm run generate
    74→
    75→# ============================================
    76→# Production Stage - Minimal runtime image
    77→# ============================================
    78→FROM node:22-alpine AS runner
    79→
    80→# Metadata labels
    81→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.title="Konecta IQ Portal"
    82→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.description="Nuxt.js AI-Powered Knowledge Portal with Express API"
    83→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.vendor="Konecta TIC"
    84→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.created="${BUILD_DATE}"
    85→LABEL org.opencontainers.image.revision="${VCS_REF}"
    86→
    87→WORKDIR /app
    88→
    89→# Install runtime dependencies and security updates
    90→RUN apk add --no-cache \
    91→    dumb-init \
    92→    curl \
    93→    && apk upgrade --no-cache
    94→
    95→# Create non-root user
    96→RUN addgroup -g 1001 -S nodejs && \
    97→    adduser -S nodejs -u 1001
    98→
    99→# Set environment variables
   100→ENV NODE_ENV=production \
   101→    HOST=0.0.0.0 \
   102→    PORT=8080 \
   103→    ENVIRONMENT=production \
   104→    NODE_OPTIONS="--max-old-space-size=512"
   105→
   106→# Copy production dependencies (includes Express, pg, tsx, etc.)
   107→COPY --from=dependencies --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/node_modules /app/node_modules
   108→
   109→# Copy package.json for npm scripts
   110→COPY --from=builder --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/package*.json /app/
   111→
   112→# Copy TypeScript configuration (needed for tsx to run server)
   113→COPY --from=builder --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/tsconfig*.json /app/
   114→
   115→# Copy server code (Express API backend)
   116→COPY --from=builder --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/backend /app/backend
   117→
   118→# Copy version files
   119→COPY --from=builder --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/version.txt /app/
   120→COPY --from=builder --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/version_commit.txt /app/
   121→
   122→# Copy generated static frontend files to dist/ (Express serves from here)
   123→COPY --from=builder --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/.output/public /app/dist
   124→
   125→# Copy and prepare entrypoint script
   126→COPY --from=builder --chown=nodejs:nodejs /app/docker-entrypoint.sh /app/
   127→RUN chmod +x /app/docker-entrypoint.sh
   128→
   129→# Security: Make application files read-only where possible
   130→RUN chmod -R 555 /app/dist && \
   131→    chmod -R 555 /app/backend && \
   132→    # chmod -R 444 /app/package*.json /app/tsconfig*.json
   133→    chmod -R 444 /app/package*.json
   134→
   135→# Switch to non-root user
   136→USER nodejs
   137→
   138→# Expose port
   139→EXPOSE 8080
   140→
   141→# Health check for Cloud Run
   142→HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=5s --start-period=40s --retries=3 \
   143→  CMD node -e "\
   144→    const http = require('http'); \
   145→    const options = { \
   146→      hostname: '127.0.0.1', \
   147→      port: 8080, \
   148→      path: '/health', \
   149→      method: 'GET', \
   150→      timeout: 3000 \
   151→    }; \
   152→    const req = http.request(options, (res) => { \
   153→      process.exit(res.statusCode === 200 ? 0 : 1); \
   154→    }); \
   155→    req.on('error', () => process.exit(1)); \
   156→    req.on('timeout', () => { req.destroy(); process.exit(1); }); \
   157→    req.end();"
   158→
   159→# Use dumb-init for proper signal handling (graceful shutdown on Cloud Run)
   160→ENTRYPOINT ["dumb-init", "--"]
   161→CMD ["/app/docker-entrypoint.sh"]
   162→

     1→# Cloud Build Pipeline for kon-iq-portal
     2→# Optimized with BuildKit and advanced caching strategies
     3→# Substitution variables are injected by Terraform trigger
     4→
     5→steps:
     6→  # ============================================
     7→  # 0. Setup BuildKit Builder
     8→  # ============================================
     9→  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
    10→    id: '0. Setup BuildKit Builder'
    11→    entrypoint: 'bash'
    12→    args:
    13→      - '-c'
    14→      - |
    15→        docker buildx create --use --name=cloudbuild --driver=docker-container --driver-opt=network=host || true
    16→        docker buildx inspect --bootstrap
    17→    env:
    18→      - 'DOCKER_BUILDKIT=1'
    19→    timeout: 120s
    20→
    21→  # ============================================
    22→  # 1. Build Docker Image with Advanced BuildKit
    23→  # ============================================
    24→  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
    25→    id: '1. Build App Image'
    26→    waitFor: ['0. Setup BuildKit Builder']
    27→    env:
    28→      - 'DOCKER_BUILDKIT=1'
    29→      - 'BUILDKIT_PROGRESS=plain'
    30→    args:
    31→      - 'buildx'
    32→      - 'build'
    33→      # Use the builder we created
    34→      - '--builder=cloudbuild'
    35→      # BuildKit advanced cache configuration
    36→      # Registry cache (remote, shared across builds)
    37→      - '--cache-from=type=registry,ref=${_IMAGE_PATH}:buildcache'
    38→      - '--cache-to=type=registry,ref=${_IMAGE_PATH}:buildcache,mode=max,image-manifest=true,oci-mediatypes=true'
    39→      # Inline cache for faster pulls
    40→      - '--cache-from=type=inline'
    41→      # Image-based cache as additional fallback
    42→      - '--cache-from=${_IMAGE_PATH}:latest'
    43→      # Build arguments
    44→      - '--build-arg=BUILDKIT_INLINE_CACHE=1'
    45→      - '--build-arg=VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=${_VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID}'
    46→      - '--build-arg=NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT=${_ENVIRONMENT}'
    47→      - '--build-arg=BUILD_DATE=$BUILD_ID'
    48→      - '--build-arg=VCS_REF=$COMMIT_SHA'
    49→      - '--build-arg=COMMIT_SHA=$SHORT_SHA'
    50→      # Target final stage (if multi-stage Dockerfile)
    51→      - '--target=runner'
    52→      # Image tags
    53→      - '--tag=${_IMAGE_PATH}:${COMMIT_SHA}'
    54→      - '--tag=${_IMAGE_PATH}:${_BRANCH_NAME}'
    55→      - '--tag=${_IMAGE_PATH}:latest'
    56→      # BuildKit optimizations
    57→      - '--platform=linux/amd64'
    58→      # Metadata and provenance (disable for Cloud Run compatibility)
    59→      - '--provenance=false'
    60→      - '--sbom=false'
    61→      # Output configuration with compression
    62→      - '--output=type=registry,push=true,compression=zstd,compression-level=3,force-compression=true'
    63→      # Context
    64→      - '.'
    65→    timeout: 1200s
    66→
    67→  # ============================================
    68→  # 2. Push BuildKit Cache Tag
    69→  # ============================================
    70→  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/docker'
    71→    id: '2. Push Cache Tag'
    72→    waitFor: ['1. Build App Image']
    73→    entrypoint: 'bash'
    74→    args:
    75→      - '-c'
    76→      - |
    77→        echo "BuildKit cache automatically managed via registry cache type"
    78→        echo "Cache reference: ${_IMAGE_PATH}:buildcache"
    79→    timeout: 30s
    80→
    81→  # ============================================
    82→  # 3. Security Scanning (After Push)
    83→  # ============================================
    84→  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/gcloud'
    85→    id: '3. Scan for Vulnerabilities'
    86→    waitFor: ['1. Build App Image']
    87→    entrypoint: 'bash'
    88→    args:
    89→      - '-c'
    90→      - |
    91→        echo "Scanning image for vulnerabilities..."
    92→        gcloud artifacts docker images scan ${_IMAGE_PATH}:${COMMIT_SHA} \
    93→          --remote \
    94→          --format='value(response.scan)' > /workspace/scan_id.txt || true
    95→    timeout: 300s
    96→    allowFailure: true
    97→
    98→  # ============================================
    99→  # 4. Deploy to Cloud Run
   100→  # ============================================
   101→  - name: 'gcr.io/google.com/cloudsdktool/cloud-sdk:alpine'
   102→    id: '4. Deploy to Cloud Run'
   103→    waitFor: ['1. Build App Image']
   104→    entrypoint: 'gcloud'
   105→    args:
   106→      - 'run'
   107→      - 'services'
   108→      - 'update'
   109→      - '${_SERVICE_NAME}'
   110→      - '--project=${_PROJECT_ID}'
   111→      - '--image=${_IMAGE_PATH}:${COMMIT_SHA}'
   112→      - '--region=${_REGION}'
   113→      - '--platform=managed'
   114→      # Performance settings
   115→      - '--cpu=${_CLOUD_RUN_CPU}'
   116→      - '--memory=${_CLOUD_RUN_MEMORY}'
   117→      - '--min-instances=${_MIN_INSTANCES}'
   118→      - '--max-instances=${_MAX_INSTANCES}'
   119→      - '--concurrency=${_CONCURRENCY}'
   120→      - '--timeout=${_TIMEOUT}'
   121→      # Health check settings

   125→      # Revision naming
   126→      - '--revision-suffix=${SHORT_SHA}-${BUILD_ID}'
   127→    timeout: 300s
   128→
   129→  # ============================================

   131→  # ============================================
   132→  - name: 'gcr.io/google.com/cloudsdktool/cloud-sdk:alpine'
   133→    id: '5. Update Traffic'
   134→    waitFor: ['4. Deploy to Cloud Run']
   135→    entrypoint: 'gcloud'
   136→    args:

   141→      - '--project=${_PROJECT_ID}'
   142→      - '--region=${_REGION}'
   143→      - '--to-revisions=LATEST=100'
   144→    timeout: 180s
   145→
   146→
   147→# Images are pushed directly by BuildKit (--output=type=registry,push=true)
   148→# No need for separate images: section as BuildKit handles push automatically
   149→
   150→# Build options
   151→options:

   153→  machineType: 'E2_HIGHCPU_8'
   154→
   155→  # Disk size for large node_modules and BuildKit cache

   158→  # Logging configuration

     1→#!/bin/sh
     2→set -e
     3→
     4→echo "========================================="
     5→echo "Konecta IQ Portal - Startup"
     6→echo "========================================="
     7→
     8→# Track missing variables
     9→MISSING_VARS=""
    10→MISSING_DB_VARS=""
    11→MISSING_GCS_VARS=""
    12→MISSING_AUTH_VARS=""
    13→
    14→echo ""
    15→echo "[Environment Check] Validating configuration..."
    16→echo ""
    17→
    18→# Database variables
    19→echo "Database Configuration:"
    20→if [ -z "$DB_HOST" ]; then
    21→  echo "  ❌ DB_HOST: <not set>"
    22→  MISSING_DB_VARS="$MISSING_DB_VARS DB_HOST"
    23→else
    24→  echo "  ✅ DB_HOST: $DB_HOST"
    25→
    26→  # Only check DB_PORT if not using Unix socket (Cloud SQL)
    27→  case "$DB_HOST" in
    28→    /*)
    29→      echo "  ℹ️  DB_PORT: <not applicable> (using Unix socket)"
    30→      ;;
    31→    *)
    32→      if [ -z "$DB_PORT" ]; then
    33→        echo "  ⚠️  DB_PORT: <not set> (will default to 5432)"
    34→      else
    35→        echo "  ✅ DB_PORT: $DB_PORT"
    36→      fi
    37→      ;;
    38→  esac
    39→fi
    40→
    41→if [ -z "$DB_NAME" ]; then
    42→  echo "  ❌ DB_NAME: <not set>"
    43→  MISSING_DB_VARS="$MISSING_DB_VARS DB_NAME"
    44→else
    45→  echo "  ✅ DB_NAME: $DB_NAME"
    46→fi
    47→
    48→if [ -z "$DB_USER" ]; then
    49→  echo "  ❌ DB_USER: <not set>"
    50→  MISSING_DB_VARS="$MISSING_DB_VARS DB_USER"
    51→else
    52→  echo "  ✅ DB_USER: $DB_USER"
    53→fi
    54→
    55→if [ -z "$DB_PASSWORD" ]; then
    56→  echo "  ⚠️  DB_PASSWORD: <not set> (connection may fail)"
    57→else
    58→  echo "  ✅ DB_PASSWORD: ****** (set)"
    59→fi
    60→
    61→echo ""
    62→
    63→# GCS Bucket variables
    64→echo "Google Cloud Storage Configuration:"
    65→if [ -z "$GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET" ]; then
    66→  echo "  ❌ GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET: <not set>"
    67→  MISSING_GCS_VARS="$MISSING_GCS_VARS GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET"
    68→else
    69→  echo "  ✅ GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET: $GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET"
    70→fi
    71→
    72→if [ -z "$GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET" ]; then

    74→  MISSING_GCS_VARS="$MISSING_GCS_VARS GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET"
    75→else

     1→/**
     2→ * Shared Configuration Constants
     3→ *
     4→ * This file consolidates all environment variables and constants used across the application.
     5→ * Import from this file instead of accessing process.env directly to avoid duplication.
     6→ */
     7→
     8→// ============================================
     9→// SERVER CONFIGURATION
    10→// ============================================
    11→
    12→export const PORT = parseInt(process.env.PORT || '3002', 10);
    13→export const NODE_ENV = process.env.NODE_ENV || 'development';
    14→export const IS_PRODUCTION = NODE_ENV === 'production';
    15→export const IS_DEVELOPMENT = NODE_ENV === 'development';
    16→
    17→// CORS Configuration - reads from environment variable
    18→const originsString = process.env.ALLOWED_ORIGINS || 'http://localhost:3002,http://localhost:3000,http://localhost:5173';
    19→export const ALLOWED_ORIGINS = originsString.split(',').map(origin => origin.trim());
    20→
    21→// ============================================
    22→// AUTHENTICATION & SECURITY
    23→// ============================================
    24→
    25→// JWT Secret - REQUIRED in production
    26→export const JWT_SECRET = process.env.JWT_SECRET;
    27→
    28→// Validate JWT_SECRET is set in production
    29→if (IS_PRODUCTION && !JWT_SECRET) {
    30→    throw new Error('FATAL: JWT_SECRET environment variable must be set in production');
    31→}
    32→
    33→// Google OAuth
    34→export const GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID = process.env.GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID;
    35→
    36→// Email domain restriction - FALLBACK ONLY
    37→// This is only used when database is unavailable or not yet initialized
    38→// Admins should configure allowed domains via Admin Panel > Configuration > Security
    39→export const DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN = process.env.DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN || 'konecta.com';
    40→
    41→// ============================================
    42→// DYNAMIC SETTINGS (Database-backed with caching)
    43→// ============================================
    44→
    45→// Settings cache with 5-minute TTL
    46→const settingsCache = new Map<string, { value: any; expiresAt: number }>();
    47→const CACHE_TTL_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000; // Will be replaced with constant after TIME CONSTANTS section
    48→
    49→/**
    50→ * Get allowed domains from database with caching
    51→ * Falls back to DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN if database is not available *
    52→ */
    53→export async function getDynamicSettings() {
    54→    const cacheKey = 'dynamic_settings';
    55→    const cached = settingsCache.get(cacheKey);
    56→
    57→    if (cached && cached.expiresAt > Date.now()) {
    58→        return cached.value;
    59→    }
    60→
    61→    try {
    62→        // Lazy import to avoid circular dependency
    63→        const { db } = await import('../db/database.js');
    64→
    65→        const domainsResult = await db.query(
    66→            'SELECT value FROM settings WHERE category = $1 AND name = $2',
    67→            ['security', 'allowed_domains']
    68→        );
    69→
    70→        const allowedDomains = domainsResult.rows[0]?.value
    71→            ? JSON.parse(domainsResult.rows[0].value)
    72→            : [DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN];
    73→
    74→        const settings = {
    75→            allowedDomains: Array.isArray(allowedDomains) ? allowedDomains : [DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN]
    76→        };
    77→
    78→        // Cache the result
    79→        settingsCache.set(cacheKey, {
    80→            value: settings,
    81→            expiresAt: Date.now() + CACHE_TTL_MS
    82→        });
    83→
    84→        return settings;
    85→    } catch (error) {
    86→        console.warn('Failed to load dynamic settings from database, using env fallbacks:', error);
    87→        return {
    88→            allowedDomains: [DEFAULT_ALLOWED_DOMAIN]
    89→        };
    90→    }
    91→}
    92→
    93→/**
    94→ * Clear settings cache (useful after updating settings in database)
    95→ */
    96→export function clearSettingsCache() {
    97→    settingsCache.clear();
    98→}
    99→

   243→/**
   244→ * Validates that all required environment variables are set
   245→ * Call this at application startup
   246→ */
   247→export function validateRequiredEnvVars(): void {
   248→    const requiredVars = [
   249→        { name: 'JWT_SECRET', value: JWT_SECRET, severity: 'critical' },
   250→        { name: 'GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET', value: GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET, severity: IS_PRODUCTION ? 'critical' : 'warning' },
   251→        { name: 'GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET', value: GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET, severity: IS_PRODUCTION ? 'critical' : 'warning' },
   252→    ];
   253→
   254→    const missing = requiredVars.filter(({ value }) => !value);
   255→
   256→    if (missing.length > 0) {
   257→        const critical = missing.filter(({ severity }) => severity === 'critical');
   258→        const warnings = missing.filter(({ severity }) => severity === 'warning');
   259→
   260→        if (critical.length > 0) {
   261→            console.error('❌ CRITICAL: Missing required environment variables:');
   262→            critical.forEach(({ name }) => console.error(`   - ${name}`));
   263→            throw new Error('Missing critical environment variables');
   264→        }
   265→
   266→        if (warnings.length > 0) {
   267→            console.warn('⚠️  WARNING: Missing recommended environment variables:');
   268→            warnings.forEach(({ name }) => console.warn(`   - ${name}`));
   269→        }
   270→    }
   271→}
   272→
   273→/**
   274→ * Logs current configuration (sanitized)
   275→ * Useful for debugging environment setup
   276→ */

   285→    console.log(`   VIDEO_BUCKET: ${VIDEO_BUCKET}`);
   286→    console.log(`   JWT_SECRET: ${JWT_SECRET ? '[SET]' : '[NOT SET]'}`);
   287→    console.log(`   GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID: ${GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID ? '[SET]' : '[NOT SET]'}`);

     1→import pkg from 'pg';
     2→const { Pool } = pkg;
     3→import fs from 'fs';
     4→import path from 'path';
     5→import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
     6→import { runMigrations as runDatabaseMigrations } from './migrate.js';
     7→
     8→// Import all model classes
     9→import {
    10→    UserModel,
    11→    AgentModel,
    12→    SettingsModel,
    13→    UseCaseModel,
    14→    CourseModel,
    15→    DemoModel,
    16→    AnalyticsModel,
    17→    ChatModel,
    18→    McpServerModel,
    19→    StatsModel
    20→} from '../models/index.js';
    21→
    22→const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
    23→const __dirname = path.dirname(__filename);
    24→
    25→// PostgreSQL connection pool
    26→const pool = process.env.DATABASE_URL
    27→    ? new Pool({ connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL })
    28→    : new Pool({
    29→        host: process.env.DB_HOST || 'localhost',
    30→        port: parseInt(process.env.DB_PORT || '5432'),
    31→        database: process.env.DB_NAME || 'iqplatform',
    32→        user: process.env.DB_USER || 'konecta',
    33→        password: process.env.DB_PASSWORD || 'konecta'
    34→    });
    35→
    36→// Export pool as db for compatibility
    37→export const db = pool;
    38→
    39→/**
    40→ * Run database migrations
    41→ * This replaces the old initializeDatabase() function
    42→ * Migrations are versioned SQL files that run automatically on startup
    43→ */
    44→export async function runMigrations() {
    45→    await runDatabaseMigrations(pool);
    46→}
    47→
    48→/**
    49→ * @deprecated Use runMigrations() instead
    50→ * Kept for backwards compatibility
    51→ */
    52→export async function initializeDatabase() {
    53→    const schemaPath = path.join(__dirname, 'schema.sql');
    54→    const schema = fs.readFileSync(schemaPath, 'utf-8');
    55→
    56→    // Execute schema
    57→    await pool.query(schema);
    58→
    59→    console.log('✓ Database initialized successfully');
    60→}
    61→
    62→// Valid roles for the platform
    63→export const VALID_ROLES = [
    64→    'user',           // Normal user, no admin panel access
    65→    'admin',          // Full access to all admin panel features
    66→    'agent_manager',  // Can manage agents only
    67→    'showcase_manager',   // Can manage showcases only
    68→    'training_manager', // Can manage training/courses only
    69→    'usecase_manager',  // Can manage use case requests (full CRUD)
    70→    'usecase_viewer',    // Can view use case requests only (read-only)
    71→    'success_story_manager',  // Can manage success stories only
    72→    'knowledgebase_manager',  // Can manage knowledge bases only
    73→    'documentation_manager'   // Can manage documentation sites
    74→] as const;
    75→
    76→export type UserRole = typeof VALID_ROLES[number];
    77→
    78→// Role display names for UI
    79→export const ROLE_LABELS: Record<UserRole, string> = {
